- **Iron condors** anchored between S1 (7,328) and the Call Wall (7,400) reflect the regime's mean-reversion character. Be mindful of the 08:30 ET catalyst window when sizing.

**Defined-Risk Directional (Conditional — Bull case):**
If Nonfarm Productivity prints strongly and the Call Wall at 7,400 is convincingly broken on volume, the positive gamma environment should not be confused with a momentum accelerator. *A break of the Call Wall in positive gamma is typically a slow grind, not an explosion.* If pursuing upside exposure above 7,400, **debit call spreads targeting R2 at 7,439** with defined risk are appropriate — but size conservatively given RSI and extension levels.

**Risk Management Priority:**
- The week-level risk outlook remains **elevated**: Core CPI and CPI s.a. (April) are on deck later this week. These are the prints that could materially reset the regime narrative. Do not carry naked short vol exposure into that window.
- VIX at 17.39 — not cheap, but not pricing genuine panic. Outright long vol as a hedge against the geopolitical reversal scenario has asymmetric merit given the non-confirmation today.

---

**Summary Posture:** Today was a genuine macro re-rating event delivered cleanly within a positive gamma regime that smoothed the ride without reversing the trend. The setup into Thursday is tactically delicate — SPX is pressing a structural ceiling (7,400 Call Wall / R1 confluence) with overbought technicals, a high-impact data print pre-market, and a VIX that refused to confirm the rally. The positive gamma regime supports range-bound, income-oriented structures as the base case. Directional bulls need a clean Productivity beat *and* a Call Wall breach to justify chasing. Manage size accordingly ahead of the week's remaining macro risk.

*Good luck tomorrow.*

Basic Materials (-2.4%) and Utilities (-3.7%) bore the brunt of the damage, while defensives held in, suggesting investors are de-risking selectively around commodity and rate-sensitive exposures rather than initiating an outright risk-off posture.

## 2. Regime Validation: Positive Gamma Delivered Exactly What It Should

The morning called **Positive Gamma / High Volatility (70% confidence)** — an internally somewhat contradictory setup, and the market resolved it accordingly with a **mixed session**. The day-type forecast of HIGH VOLATILITY did not materialize in a directional sense; instead, the session behaved as positive gamma environments characteristically do. With dealers long options, their hedging behavior — **selling into strength, buying weakness** — acted as a natural circuit breaker. The 7,385 high was quickly faded back toward 7,337, and the 7,321 low was absorbed without follow-through. The 0.87% range confirms dealer hedging flows dampened directional extension on both sides.

**Regime verdict: Confirmed.** Positive gamma acted as the structural governor it's designed to be. The HIGH VOLATILITY forecast was a partial miss — the catalyst load was real, but the gamma regime's dampening effect capped realized vol.

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Vol Compression Consistent With Structure

VIX closed at **17.08 (-1.79%)**, declining despite SPX also pulling back. This is the key tell: SPX fell modestly while implied volatility compressed — a signature of **positive gamma at work**. Dealers holding long options positions are not urgently re-hedging into a slow grind lower; they're collecting premium decay and selling into any brief vol spike. The VIX decline on a down-SPX day reinforces that there is no underlying panic bid for protection — the market remains structurally complacent relative to its technical extension.

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Friday, May 08, 2026 | SPX 7,398.93 | VIX 17.19

---

## 1. Session Flow — What Drove Today's Move

Today's session printed a **moderate trend day** to the upside, carrying SPX through the 7,400 psychological threshold on an intraday basis (high: 7,401.50) before a fractional fade into the close at 7,398.93 (+0.85%). The primary drivers appear to have been **macro and momentum-oriented**: headline news points to a strong April Jobs Report and a sustained chip-stock bid as the catalysts powering what is now the **sixth consecutive weekly gain** for the index. This was not an options-mechanics-driven session in the traditional sense — the move was fundamentally catalyzed, with dealer flows operating in the background as a dampening rather than amplifying force.

Sector rotation was **rotational, not broad-based**, with Technology leading (+0.9%) and Basic Materials flat (+0.0%), while Industrials (-0.9%) and Utilities (-2.4%) lagged meaningfully. The 3.3% cross-sector dispersion is a key tell: this was not a rising-tide session. Capital is being actively rotated into growth/AI-adjacent names and away from defensives, consistent with the Jobs data reducing near-term recession anxiety and the ongoing AI narrative maintaining its bid.

---

## 2. Regime Validation — Did the Session Confirm the Morning Call?

**Partially contradicted.** The morning regime call was **Positive Gamma** with a forecast of **High Volatility** at 70% confidence. Positive gamma environments typically see dealers selling into rallies and buying dips — a self-dampening mechanism that compresses realized volatility and produces range-bound, mean-reverting sessions.

What we got instead was a **moderate trend day** — directional, with SPX grinding higher through the session rather than oscillating. The session range was a contained **0.52%**, which is not inconsistent with positive gamma suppression of intraday noise, but the **directional character** of the move suggests exogenous macro catalysts (Jobs Report, chip strength) overrode the mean-reversion mechanics that positive gamma typically enforces. In short: the range stayed compressed as the regime would predict, but the **trend character** reflected a macro catalyst strong enough to override typical dealer hedging dynamics. The regime didn't break — it was simply subordinated to a strong fundamental impulse.

The closing regime remains **Positive Gamma**, confirming no structural flip occurred during the session.

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay — Dealer Flows and Volatility Confirmation

The VIX closing at **17.19 (+0.65%)** on a +0.85% SPX day is a **divergence worth noting**. In a typical risk-on rally, implied volatility compresses. The modest VIX uptick alongside an equity gain suggests **the market is not entirely comfortable** at these levels — put protection is being maintained or added even as the index grinds to all-time territory. This is not alarming at current absolute levels (17.19 remains well below historical stress thresholds), but it does indicate **hedging demand is not collapsing** despite six consecutive up weeks.

In a **Positive Gamma** environment, dealers are **long options**, meaning they are delta-hedging by **selling into rallies and buying dips**. This likely explains the contained 0.52% session range — dealer sell-side pressure near the 7,400 level acted as a natural ceiling, consistent with the Call Wall sitting at exactly **7,400**. The intraday high of 7,401.50 barely clipped that level before retreating. That is a textbook positive gamma pin dynamic at the Call Wall.

This backdrop supported the risk-on character of the session without triggering a rates-driven de-rating.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications — Structures for the Current Regime

**Regime: Positive Gamma | Catalyst Load: Low (Tomorrow) / Elevated (This Week)**

In a **Positive Gamma** environment, dealers are **long options** and hedge by **selling into rallies and buying dips**, actively dampening directional moves. This creates a mean-reverting, range-bound backdrop that **favors income and premium-selling structures** — not directional speculation.

**Actionable Framework:**

- **Iron Condors / Credit Spreads** are the regime-appropriate structure for Monday's low-catalyst session. With the expected move at ±80 points and the regime actively suppressing vol, premium sellers have a structural edge in a contained range. Define risk carefully given the overbought RSI backdrop.

- **Call Wall at 7,400 as a short-strike anchor:** The Call Wall's alignment with current price makes it a natural upper bound for any call spread short leg. Resistance here is dealer-enforced, not just technical.

- **Put Wall at 7,385 as the lower bound reference:** Credit put spreads with the short leg at or above 7,385 benefit from the positive gamma support bid.

- **Pre-CPI Caution is non-negotiable:** Do not carry naked or undefined short vol exposure into next week's CPI print. If selling premium into Monday's range, ensure structures expire before or are sized to survive a CPI-driven gap. The weekly elevated risk flag is real.

- **Avoid aggressive long directional structures (debit spreads/calls):** In positive gamma, the dealer hedging flow actively works against momentum. A breakout above 7,400 faces structural selling. The risk/reward for upside debit structures is asymmetrically poor in this regime.

---

**Bottom Line:** SPX closed out its sixth straight winning week on a solid macro foundation, but is now sitting at an overbought extension against a Call Wall at 7,400 in a positive gamma regime. Monday is a low-risk session by calendar standards — the range-bound, mean-reverting character of positive gamma should prevail. The strategic priority is **disciplined premium capture within defined risk parameters**, with a hard eye on CPI as the week's binary event. Carry light, stay defined, and revisit sizing before Wednesday.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Monday, May 11, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## 1. Session Flow: Geopolitical Headlines Drove a Contained Melt-Up

Today's primary driver was macro/geopolitical in nature. President Trump's rejection of a US-Iran peace proposal sent oil prices sharply higher, lifting Energy as the session's standout sector. Simultaneously, residual optimism around AI themes — anchored in names like NVIDIA and Intel — kept Technology bid. The net result was a constructive, headline-driven session that shook off early dips and ground modestly higher.

That said, the *scope* of the move was decidedly narrow. SPX closed +0.19% on the day, with a session range of just 0.61% (High: 7,428.97 / Low: 7,384.20). The narrative felt larger than the price action warranted — a classic sign of dealer flows absorbing directional pressure rather than amplifying it.

**One critical note on sourcing:** The news feed referenced an SPX close of 5,428 and VIX at 14.2 — these figures are inconsistent with verified market data. Per source of truth: **SPX closed at 7,412.84** and **VIX closed at 18.38 (+6.93%)**. Sector leadership in the news feed (Tech +2.8%, Energy +3.2%) also diverges from confirmed sector rotation data; confirmed leaders were **Energy +1.8%** and **Basic Materials +1.2%**, with **Communication Services -0.7%** and **Healthcare -0.4%** lagging. The risk-on *tone* from the news is directionally correct — the magnitude is overstated.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Positive Gamma Confirmed — Barely

The morning called for a **MIXED / CHOP** session under a **positive gamma regime** with 55% confidence. What we got was a **range-bound** session — arguably the most literal expression of that forecast. The 0.61% range and mean-reverting character through the session are textbook positive gamma behavior: dealers, positioned long options (long gamma), were systematically **selling into strength and buying into weakness**, capping the range and suppressing momentum.

Forecast: ✅ Confirmed.

However, regime confidence must be qualified. The GEX flip level sits at **7,410.0** — SPX closed at **7,412.84**, a margin of just 2.84 points. This is an explicitly borderline regime. The positive gamma classification is technically accurate at the close, but the market is not operating from a position of structural depth. A modest overnight shift in dealer positioning or a pre-CPI flush tomorrow could flip this regime cleanly negative.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: A Discordant Signal Worth Monitoring

Here is the most important technical friction from today's session: **VIX rose +6.93% to 18.38 on a day when SPX closed in positive territory**. This divergence demands attention.

In a healthy risk-on rally with compressed volatility, you would expect VIX to decline as SPX advances — the classic inverse relationship. Instead, implied volatility *expanded* into a positive close. This tells us one of two things (or both):

- **Tail hedging activity is accelerating.** Institutional players are buying protection ahead of tomorrow's CPI print, driving put premium and skew higher.
- **The market's confidence in the rally is shallow.** The geopolitical headline-driven bid is not being endorsed by the options market. Traders are paying up for downside insurance rather than selling vol into the move.

Combined with an SPY RSI(14) at **76 (overbought)**, price sitting **+8.0% above the 50-SMA** and **+9.8% above the 200-SMA**, this is not a market that has earned its altitude. Mean-reversion risk is elevated on any disappointment.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: CPI Is the Only Number That Matters

Tuesday's session is shaped almost entirely by one event: **April CPI, due at 08:30 ET (high impact)**. Core CPI both month-over-month and year-over-year prints simultaneously, making this a binary volatility event. Fed Governor Williams speaks at 03:15 ET and NFIB Business Optimism prints at 06:00 ET — both medium impact and likely to be overshadowed unless the Williams remarks contain unexpected hawkish signaling.

**Expected Move:** +/- 86 points on SPX. That is a wide band relative to today's 0.61% range, reflecting the options market's explicit acknowledgment of binary CPI risk.

**Key Levels for Tomorrow:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,420 | Near-term ceiling; 7 points above close |
| Put Wall | 7,400 | Near-term floor; 12.84 points below close |
| R1 | 7,450 | First meaningful resistance on a CPI beat |
| R2 | 7,487 | Extended bull case target |
| S1 | 7,376 | First support on a CPI miss |
| S2 | 7,339 | Elevated risk support; ~74 points below close |
| GEX Flip | 7,410 | Regime boundary — critical intraday watch level |

The **Put Wall at 7,400** and **Call Wall at 7,420** define an extraordinarily tight 20-point band around current price. With SPX closing at 7,412.84, the market is essentially pinned between these two walls into tomorrow morning. This compression is characteristic of a market awaiting a binary catalyst — and it means the reaction *to* CPI, not the level itself, will determine the session's character.

**Scenario Framework:**

- **CPI comes in soft (below expectations):** Expect an immediate bid through the Call Wall at 7,420 toward R1 at 7,450. If the regime flips negative gamma on an upside surprise in price *action* (i.e., spot clears the flip level with momentum), dealer hedging behavior would shift to buying into the rally — potentially extending the move toward R2 at 7,487.

- **CPI comes in hot (above expectations):** The Put Wall at 7,400 becomes the first test. A clean break below 7,400 brings S1 at 7,376 into focus rapidly. Given the borderline gamma regime, a break below the GEX flip level (7,410) on a hot print could trigger negative gamma dynamics — dealers selling into weakness, amplifying the drawdown toward S1 and potentially S2 at 7,339 in a disorderly tape.

- **CPI inline:** The positive gamma regime — if it holds — should reassert a choppy, range-bound character between the Put and Call walls. This is the least volatile outcome but perhaps the most dangerous for premium sellers given the VIX expansion already underway.

**Overnight risk is elevated.** The VIX expansion into today's close signals the market is already pricing pre-CPI anxiety. Gaps at the open are plausible in either direction.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Defined Risk Is Non-Negotiable Into CPI

Given the confluence of a **borderline gamma regime**, **elevated VIX at 18.38**, **overbought technicals**, and a **high-impact binary CPI print**, the strategic framework for tomorrow must prioritize defined risk above all else.

**What the regime says — with appropriate caution:**

The closing regime is *technically* positive gamma, but at 2.84 points from the flip, it carries no structural weight. Do **not** treat this as a stable, dampening environment. Plan for the possibility that the regime is negative gamma intraday — particularly if CPI drives a sustained move away from current spot.

**If positive gamma holds (CPI inline, muted reaction):**
- The session should exhibit mean-reverting, range-bound character between 7,400 and 7,420.
- **Income structures** are theoretically aligned — iron condors or credit spreads centered on that range could benefit from suppressed realized volatility.
- However, with VIX already elevated and CPI risk still live until 08:30, initiating these structures pre-data is imprudent. Wait for the post-print confirmation of range-bound behavior before selling premium.

**If the regime shifts negative gamma (CPI surprise, directional break):**
- Dealer hedging dynamics reverse: dealers would be **buying into a rally or selling into a decline**, amplifying the directional move rather than dampening it.
- **Directional structures** are appropriate — debit spreads or defined-risk verticals in the direction of the CPI surprise.
- On a downside break: Put debit spreads targeting S1 (7,376) or S2 (7,339) as strikes. On an upside break: Call debit spreads with R1 (7,450) and R2 (7,487) as targets.
- Keep structure width proportional to the +/- 86 expected move; avoid overleveraging into a regime that remains uncertain.

**Universal risk management for tomorrow:**
- Size conservatively. The VIX at 18.38 means option premium is elevated — you are paying up for exposure in either direction.
- RSI at 76 and price at +9.8% above the 200-SMA create significant asymmetric downside risk on a CPI upside surprise. Do not fade a downside move aggressively without clear regime confirmation.
- The Call Wall at 7,420 — just 7 points above the close — is an immediate cap. Any structure with upside exposure above 7,420 pre-data requires a clear catalyst to justify the premium spend.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today's session did exactly what a borderline positive gamma environment should — it went nowhere on a modest headline. Tomorrow it has nowhere to hide. CPI at 08:30 is the event, the GEX flip at 7,410 is the regime trigger, and 7,400 / 7,420 are the guardrails. Trade defined risk, size for the expected move, and let the print tell you which side of the regime you're operating in before committing to a directional thesis.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy | Evening Recap Desk*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Tuesday, May 12, 2026 | SPX Close: 7,400.96 | VIX: 17.99

---

## 1. Session Flow: Geopolitical Macro Drove Rotation, Not Directionality

Today's tape was shaped by a single macro catalyst: Trump's signal that the Iran ceasefire was "on life support," sending Brent crude through $104 and WTI to $98. The energy complex was the clear beneficiary, with XLE printing roughly +2.6% on the session. However, the headline-driven bid was narrow and conflicted.

**What the market data tells us:** SPX closed at 7,400.96, down -0.16% on the session — a figure that flatly contradicts the modestly positive narrative in some news feeds. The session range was 0.97%, with a high of 7,409.57 and a low of 7,338.54. The intraday low is notable — it tested toward S1 (7,364) before recovering, but the close well inside the day's range suggests neither side could build conviction.

Breadth confirmed the lack of follow-through: advancers lagged decliners on both NYSE and Nasdaq, and despite the energy spike, our sector data shows **Healthcare (+1.3%) and Consumer Defensive (+1.2%)** as today's actual session leaders — a defensive rotation, not the risk-on narrative that oil headlines implied. Technology (-0.5%) and Consumer Cyclical (-0.9%) lagged. The market was hedging, not chasing.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Positive Gamma Confirmed — Morning Call Stands, Day Type Missed

The morning called **Positive Gamma** and predicted a **High Volatility** session with 70% confidence. The regime call was correct; the day type call was not.

**Regime:** Confirmed. With dealers long options (long gamma), their hedging mechanics required selling into strength and buying into weakness — and that is precisely what played out. The 7,338 intraday low was absorbed and faded; the session settled near mid-range at 7,400.96. The 0.97% range is consistent with a damped, mean-reverting positive gamma environment. There was no breakout, no trend extension, no momentum cascade.

**Day Type Miss:** The High Volatility prediction did not materialize in terms of realized SPX range. Oil volatility was real, but energy vol translated into sector rotation rather than index directionality. The geopolitical catalyst created noise, not signal, at the index level. Dealer gamma suppression was the dominant force, absorbing the macro shock and keeping SPX range-bound.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: A Divergence Worth Monitoring

VIX closed at **17.99, down -2.13%** on the session. This is the critical data point to anchor to.

The news feed references a VIX *increase* — that data conflicts with our closing print of 17.99 (-2.13%) and should be disregarded in favor of market data. The VIX decline alongside a marginally lower SPX close is, in fact, consistent with the positive gamma regime: options were decaying into a range-bound session, implied vol was being sold, and dealers were not being forced into aggressive directional hedging.

What *does* warrant attention is the broader technical backdrop:

- **SPY RSI(14): 72** — firmly overbought
- **Price +7.7% above 50-SMA, +9.5% above 200-SMA** — historically elevated mean-reversion risk
- **10Y yield: 4.46% (+4bp today)** — rates continuing to grind higher, compressing equity multiples at the margin

The 2Y-10Y spread at +46bp reflects a normal curve, but the upward drift in the long end — catalyzed by energy-driven inflation re-pricing — is a structural headwind that the positive gamma regime is currently masking at the index level. VIX at 17.99 is benign, but the setup is not without latent risk.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: PPI Is the Pivot Event | Levels 7,364–7,450 Define the Battlefield

**Overnight Context:** No GEX flip level exists in the current structure — the market sits in absolute positive gamma territory. That means tomorrow's range is bounded by the **Put Wall at 7,350** on the downside and the **Call Wall at 7,450** on the upside. The expected move is ±84 points from today's close, framing a range of approximately **7,317 to 7,485**.

**Key Levels for Wednesday:**
- **7,450 (Call Wall):** Primary upside resistance. In a positive gamma regime, dealer hedging flows will naturally suppress attempts to clear this level unless an outsized catalyst drives options buyers to overwhelm the structure.
- **7,438 (R1):** First technical resistance to watch on any morning gap. A clean open above here on a hot PPI print (stagflation read) would be unusual and short-lived absent a regime flip.
- **7,364 (S1):** The critical near-term support. Today's low of 7,338 already tested below this zone intraday before recovering. A failure to hold S1 on a soft/hot PPI print opens a path toward 7,327 (S2) and the Put Wall at 7,350.
- **7,350 (Put Wall):** Structural floor. Dealer long-gamma hedging intensifies below this level, but a sustained breach would signal meaningful positioning shift.

**Tomorrow's Catalyst Stack (all medium impact):**

- **OPEC Monthly Report (06:00 ET):** Given today's Iran headline risk and oil at multi-month highs, this report carries above-average relevance. A production cut signal or upward demand revision would amplify energy inflation concerns heading into PPI. Monitor pre-market energy complex reaction.

- **PPI Suite (08:30 ET):** This is the session-defining event. Four PPI prints drop simultaneously — headline MoM/YoY, and core Ex Food, Energy & Trade MoM/YoY. With oil +2.9% today and inflation anxiety re-emerging as a market theme, a hot PPI print risks a rates-driven de-rating narrative. The 10Y already moved +4bp today; a surprise to the upside on PPI could push yields toward 4.50-4.55%, pressuring the multiple on an already overbought SPX.

**Scenario Framework:**

|---|---|---|

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Income Structures Remain the Correct Fit — With a Caveat on Technicals

The closing positive gamma regime continues to favor **range-bound, mean-reverting** session behavior. Dealers are long options and will hedge by selling into strength and buying into weakness — flows that mechanically dampen moves. This is the structural underpinning for income-oriented structures.

**Structures that align with the regime:**
- **Iron Condors:** With the 7,350 Put Wall and 7,450 Call Wall defining a natural ~100-point structural range, condors selling above 7,450 and below 7,350 have logical strike anchors. The ±84 expected move supports this framing.
- **Credit Spreads (Put side):** With S1 at 7,364 as a near-term floor and positive gamma absorbing downside moves, put credit spreads in the 7,300–7,350 zone offer a defined-risk income approach, though today's intraday dip to 7,338 is a reminder that the Put Wall is not impenetrable intraday.

**The Caveat That Cannot Be Ignored:**

RSI at 72 and price running 9.5% above the 200-SMA represent a structural vulnerability that coexists with the gamma regime. Positive gamma suppresses *daily* volatility — it does not prevent a multi-session repricing if a macro shock (geopolitical escalation, hot inflation data consecutive sessions) overwhelms the options structure. Tomorrow's PPI is a single catalyst; two or three consecutive inflation surprises in a week would begin to pressure VIX higher and could shift the gamma regime.

**For now, the tactical setup supports income structures within the 7,350–7,450 band. Size risk accordingly given overbought technicals. PPI at 8:30 is the binary — let the number print before committing to directional bias at tomorrow's open.**

---

*Risk Outlook: Moderate. PPI is the fulcrum event. Positive gamma provides a structural buffer, but overbought technicals and geopolitical tail risk (Iran/oil) argue for disciplined position sizing. The Call Wall at 7,450 is your ceiling; the Put Wall at 7,350 is your floor — respect both.*

# Evening Derivatives Recap — Wednesday, May 13, 2026

---

## 1. Session Flow: What Drove Today's Move

Today's session produced a **moderate trend day** — not the choppy, mean-reverting action that a positive gamma environment typically prescribes. The primary driver was a **rotation narrative centered on AI/chip names and Communication Services**, with the sector advancing +1.7% on the day. Headlines pointing to an S&P 500 all-time high and a chip-led rally suggest that momentum flows overpowered the dampening effect of dealer hedging, at least intraday.

Critically, this came **one day after a hot April CPI print** (3.8% YoY, 0.6% MoM — the highest headline reading in roughly three years). Rather than sustaining the risk-off tone from Tuesday, the market compartmentalized the inflation shock and rotated aggressively into growth/tech proxies. Boston Fed President Collins adding hawkish commentary — flagging the possibility of rate hikes if inflation broadens — did not derail the bid, which itself is notable. The market is, for now, selectively ignoring macro headwinds.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Did Today Confirm or Contradict the Morning Call?

**Contradicted — partially.** The morning forecast called for a **MIXED/CHOP session** with 55% confidence under a positive gamma regime. The actual session printed as a **moderate trend day**, with SPX ranging 1.15% from low (7,375.13) to high (7,460.04) and closing near session highs at **7,444.25 (+0.59%)**.

In a positive gamma regime, dealers are long options and hedge by **selling into strength and buying weakness** — a dynamic that typically compresses intraday ranges and produces mean-reverting behavior. Today's session showed directional persistence, particularly on the upside, suggesting that dealer hedging flows were **insufficient to fully contain the move**. Two plausible explanations:

1. **Catalyst asymmetry:** The AI/chip rotation generated strong directional conviction, overwhelming the dampening effect of dealer gamma hedging near the session open.
2. **Proximity to structural levels:** The Call Wall sits at **7,450** — just above today's close of 7,444.25. Price pressing into the call wall can itself generate resistance and partial mean-reversion late in the session, which is consistent with the rally stalling near the highs (7,460.04) and pulling back to settle below 7,450.

The closing regime remains **positive gamma**, so the structural backdrop did not flip — but today demonstrated that even a positive gamma environment can trend when catalysts are sufficiently one-sided.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay

VIX closed at **17.87 (-0.67%)**, confirming the SPX directional move. The modest VIX compression is consistent with a positive gamma environment — dealers were hedging by selling into the rally, which kept implied volatility from collapsing more aggressively. Had this been a negative gamma session, we'd expect sharper VIX contraction as directional momentum fed on itself.

The **1.15% session range** is also instructive. For context, a pure positive gamma day often sees compressed ranges well below 1%; today's range at 1.15% is elevated relative to that baseline, reinforcing the idea that exogenous flows (rotational buying) partially overrode dealer hedging behavior. That said, the close near the session midpoint-to-upper range — rather than a blowout extension — does reflect some positive gamma stabilization in the final hours.

One overlay that demands attention: **SPY RSI(14) is at 75 (overbought)**, with price now **+8.1% above the 50-SMA and +10.1% above the 200-SMA**. This is a stretched positioning setup. VIX at 17.87 is not pricing material tail risk relative to those technicals, which creates a potential asymmetry heading into the back half of the week.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Levels, Catalysts, and Overnight Context

**Structural Framework:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,450 | Price closed just below; resistance ceiling |
| R1 | 7,481 | First meaningful extension target above call wall |
| R2 | 7,519 | Upper bound of the expected move range |
| Put Wall | 7,400 | Structural support floor |
| S1 | 7,407 | First technical support |
| S2 | 7,370 | Session low proximity; critical if support breaks |
| Expected Move (±) | ±84pts | Range: ~7,360 – 7,528 |

The **Call Wall at 7,450** is the single most important level heading into Thursday's open. SPX closed at 7,444.25 — effectively at the Call Wall. A sustained break and hold above 7,450 on volume would open the path toward R1 at 7,481 and potentially R2 at 7,519. Conversely, rejection from this level sends price back toward the 7,407–7,400 Put Wall zone, which represents the structural support floor within the positive gamma construct.

**Thursday Catalysts (08:30 ET — pre-market):**

A cluster of **medium-impact macro data** hits simultaneously at the open:
- **Retail Sales Ex-Autos (Apr)** and **Retail Sales YoY (Apr)** — given the hot CPI backdrop (3.8% YoY), a solid retail sales print would complicate the Fed's path and could renew rate-hike anxiety as flagged by Collins today. Consensus per Barron's anticipates resilient retail sales even with higher gas prices.
- **Jobless Claims** (Continuing + 4-Week Average) — labor market health is a secondary Fed input but increasingly relevant in a stagflationary setup.
- **Import Prices YoY (Apr)** — low-impact but additive to the inflation narrative.

The risk here is a **data release combination** (hot retail + elevated jobless claims) that presents a stagflation signal and forces a repricing of rate expectations at the open. With price pinned to the Call Wall, a macro surprise could generate an outsized move — either direction — before dealers can fully absorb it.

**Week-level risk escalation:** FOMC Minutes are on the calendar this week. Position sizing should account for the possibility that the Minutes reflect a more hawkish internal debate than the May meeting statement conveyed.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

**Regime:** Positive gamma (closing). Dealer hedging behavior is **sell rallies / buy dips**, acting as a natural buffer around the current price range.

**The setup for Thursday is a classic positive gamma squeeze-or-contain binary:**

- **Base case (regime holds):** Price oscillates between the Put Wall (7,400) and Call Wall (7,450), with dealers continuing to dampen moves in either direction. **Income structures are well-suited here** — iron condors or credit spreads centered around the 7,400–7,450 structural range benefit from the mean-reverting character that positive gamma provides.

- **Bull case (Call Wall breaks):** A hot retail sales print interpreted as *growth resilience* (not inflation fear) could push price through 7,450 with momentum toward R1 at 7,481. In this scenario, positive gamma still applies, but the Call Wall becomes support once cleared. A call debit spread targeting the R1/R2 zone (7,481–7,519) would capture that extension with defined risk.

- **Bear case (macro shock):** A stagflation signal from the 08:30 data (hot prices + weakening labor) could break the 7,407 S1 quickly, with the Put Wall at 7,400 as the next meaningful floor. Below 7,400, the positive gamma buffer becomes less reliable. A put debit spread targeting the S2/7,370 zone provides defined-risk downside coverage.

**Critical caution — technicals:** SPY RSI at 75 and price extended 8–10% above key moving averages represent significant mean-reversion risk on any catalyst miss. Income structures should be sized conservatively given this overextension, particularly with FOMC Minutes risk later in the week. The risk/reward on short-premium structures narrows considerably when the underlying is this stretched on the upside.

---

**Bottom line:** The market cleared a macro headwind (hot CPI) and pressed to all-time highs, but it now sits directly on the Call Wall (7,450) in a positive gamma regime with overbought technicals and a simultaneous macro data dump pre-market. Tomorrow's open is binary around 7,450. Know your levels, define your risk, and treat the 7,400–7,450 band as the battleground.

---

## 1. Session Flow: What Drove Today's Move

Today's session delivered a historic milestone — SPX closed above 7,500 for the first time, settling at **7,501.24 (+0.77%)** on a session range of just **62.7 points (0.84%)**. The driver was a continuation of the broader macro risk-on narrative: CPI and PPI data earlier this week, while hot enough to generate brief concern, failed to derail the tape. Markets interpreted the inflation prints as non-disqualifying for equities, and price discovered that thesis today in the form of a clean breakout to all-time highs.

**Technology (+1.9%)** was the clear engine, consistent with a momentum-led, index-driven session. The sector leadership is worth noting — Tech and **Utilities (+1.3%)** leading simultaneously suggests this wasn't purely a growth/risk-on rotation; defensive participation adds a layer of breadth credibility. On the other side, **Basic Materials (-0.9%)** and **Real Estate (-0.6%)** lagged, reflecting modest rate sensitivity with the 10Y yield ticking up **1bp to 4.47%**.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Morning Call vs. Actual Session

The morning forecast called for **MIXED/CHOP** (55% confidence) under a **positive gamma** regime. What we got was a **moderate trend day** — not a blowout directional move, but a sustained, directional grind higher that contradicted the chop forecast.

How do we reconcile this? The positive gamma regime was correct and remained intact through the close — dealer hedging flows were working to **dampen volatility**, which explains the compressed 0.84% session range. What the regime call underestimated was the **underlying directional pressure**: a historic milestone print (7,500) combined with Tech momentum was sufficient to generate a controlled trend even within a dampened dealer environment. Positive gamma didn't reverse the direction; it simply kept the move orderly. The forecast was wrong on character, but the regime mechanics were consistent — no panic, no blow-off extension, just a measured grind.

The **55% confidence** flag on this morning's call was appropriate in hindsight. This was not a high-conviction regime setup, and the moderate trend outcome falls within the uncertainty band.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Confirmation and Risk Flags

VIX closed at **17.26 (-3.42%)**, confirming the SPX rally directionally — vol sold off as price made new highs, exactly the expected behavior in a risk-on, positive gamma environment. There was no divergence to flag here.

That said, two technical risk flags deserve attention heading into tomorrow:

- **SPY RSI(14) at 78** — deeply overbought on a 14-period basis. This alone doesn't reverse trends, but it raises the mean-reversion probability materially, particularly in a positive gamma environment where dealers are inherently stabilizing around a mean.
- **Price is +8.8% above the 50-SMA and +10.8% above the 200-SMA.** These are extended readings. When positive gamma is present and price is this stretched, the dampening effect of dealer hedging can work against continuation — dealers will be **selling into further rallies and buying dips** to maintain delta neutrality, creating natural friction at extended levels.

In other words: positive gamma protected the rally today, but at these RSI and SMA extension levels, that same mechanic becomes a headwind for further immediate upside.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Levels, Catalysts, and Overnight Framing

**Closing Regime: POSITIVE GAMMA** — no GEX flip level in play. Key structural references are the **Put Wall at 7,500** and **Call Wall at 7,525**.

The **Put Wall at 7,500** is the immediate backstory — today's close essentially landed on it. This level now functions as a critical near-term support pivot. Positive gamma mechanics mean dealers are likely to defend this level on the margin; a dip back toward 7,500 should see buying support from hedging flows. A sustained break below it would be the first technical signal that the regime is under pressure.

**Key levels for Friday:**
| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| R2 | 7,576 | Outer upside extension; likely cap without catalyst |
| R1 | 7,539 | First meaningful resistance; above Call Wall |
| Call Wall | 7,525 | Dealer resistance zone; rallies may stall here |
| Put Wall | **7,500** | Critical support; also psychological round number |
| S1 | 7,464 | First meaningful downside pivot |
| S2 | 7,426 | Full retracement risk if S1 fails |

**Expected move: ±82 points.** That puts the range at roughly **7,419 – 7,583**, which comfortably contains all four pivot levels above. This is a well-defined range setup.

**Tomorrow's catalysts are modest in isolation but worth monitoring collectively:**
- **NY Empire State Manufacturing (08:30 ET)** — the highest-impact print of the day. Given the tariff/trade uncertainty backdrop, a weak read could introduce soft-landing doubt. A beat reinforces current macro complacency.
- **Industrial Production / Capacity Utilization / Manufacturing Production (09:15 ET)** — low individual impact, but a cluster of weak manufacturing prints could build a narrative around industrial softness that weighs on risk sentiment at the open.

None of tomorrow's data individually threatens the current rally thesis. However, given the **overbought RSI** and **historic round-number close**, the tape is priced for good news. Any miss in the manufacturing cluster could provide an excuse for profit-taking into the weekend.

**Weekly Risk Elevation — FOMC Minutes on deck.** Position sizing should reflect that the minutes represent the most significant event risk of the remaining week and could reprice rate expectations materially if the tone is more hawkish than the current market narrative accommodates.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

**Regime: Positive Gamma | Catalyst Load: Moderate Tomorrow / Elevated Weekly**

In a **positive gamma** environment, dealers are **long options** and hedge by **selling rallies and buying dips**. The net effect is mean-reversion and range compression — the market's natural tendency is to oscillate within a defined range rather than trend aggressively.

Combined with an overbought RSI (78), price extended well above moving averages, and the tape sitting directly on the Put Wall at 7,500, the setup favors **income and range-bound structures** over directional momentum plays.

**Structures that fit the regime:**
- **Iron Condors centered on 7,450–7,550** — captures the expected dampening effect of positive gamma, with wings positioned near S1 (7,464) and R1 (7,539). The ±82 expected move defines the outer bounds.
- **Put Credit Spreads (7,450/7,400 or 7,425/7,375)** — if you want to express a mild bullish lean while staying defined-risk, the Put Wall at 7,500 provides mechanical support. Keep width modest given the weekly FOMC Minutes risk.
- **Call Credit Spreads above the Call Wall (7,525/7,575)** — the Call Wall at 7,525 and overbought technicals make upside extension a low-probability path in a positive gamma regime without a clear catalyst.

**What to avoid:** Naked long premium directional plays (long calls for breakout, long puts for reversal) are expensive propositions in a positive gamma, dampened-vol environment unless sized small and catalyzed by a specific data surprise. The RSI overshoot *looks* like a put opportunity, but positive gamma mechanics actively work against sharp selloffs — waiting for regime confirmation before pressing directional downside bets is the disciplined posture.

**Risk management note:** The FOMC Minutes later this week represent a potential regime-disruptor. Structures initiated tomorrow should either be exited or have defined risk ahead of that release. A hawkish minutes surprise could spike VIX, crush positive gamma dynamics, and widen spreads sharply.

# Evening Derivatives Recap — Friday, May 15, 2026

**Prepared for Professional Distribution | Post-Close**

---

## 1. Session Flow: What Drove Today's Tape

Today's -1.24% decline in SPX was macro-driven, full stop. The dominant catalyst was a deepening global bond selloff tied to mounting inflation pressures, with the 10-year Treasury yield surging **12 basis points to 4.59%** — a move large enough to reprice rate-sensitive equity risk premiums in real time. Market participants are now actively pricing out Fed rate cuts and, in some corners of the rates complex, beginning to whisper about hikes — a significant sentiment shift from the consensus that characterized the prior six-week rally.

The sector tape confirmed this read precisely. **Energy led (+1.2%)**, consistent with rising oil prices and reflation themes. **Basic Materials and Utilities were the session's worst performers (-1.6% and -1.7%, respectively)** — a textbook bond-selloff rotation, with rate-sensitive and defensive sectors bearing the brunt of yield normalization pressure. Technology was effectively flat (-0.0%), which is notable given its long-duration sensitivity; this likely reflects idiosyncratic support from optical/AI subsectors in the news cycle rather than broad tech resilience.

Earnings were not a material driver today. 0DTE/technical flow played a secondary role, discussed below.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: A Significant Contradiction

This is the most important mechanical note in tonight's recap. **The morning regime call was POSITIVE gamma, with a MIXED/CHOP day predicted at 55% confidence.** What we got was the opposite in terms of session character: a **STRONG TREND day**, with SPX dropping from the session high of **7,454.85** to a low of **7,397.50** — a range of roughly **57 points**, but the session range metric of **0.78%** tells us the move was directional and not choppy within that band.

This is a **regime contradiction.** In a positive gamma environment, dealer hedging behavior should have been stabilizing — selling into weakness, buying into strength, dampening volatility. That clearly did not happen to the degree the model implied.

**Why did positive gamma fail to contain the move?** Two likely explanations:

- **The macro catalyst (12bp yield spike) was large enough to overpower dealer stabilization flows.** Positive gamma dampens technically-driven or flow-driven moves. It does not reliably contain moves driven by exogenous repricing of the risk-free rate. When the fundamental discount rate shifts materially intraday, even long-gamma dealer positioning can be overwhelmed.
- **The 55% confidence level on the morning call was a candid signal of regime uncertainty.** At that confidence level, the model was essentially telling us the environment was borderline. A strong macro catalyst at the margin was always capable of breaking the predicted chop pattern.

Importantly, the **closing regime remains POSITIVE**, meaning dealers are still net long options into the weekend. The macro move happened *within* a positive gamma structure — it bent the regime, it did not break it.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay

VIX closed at **18.43, +6.78%** on the day — a meaningful but not panicked spike. This is directionally consistent with SPX's decline and confirms that the session's risk-off character was real, not a low-vol drift lower. The VIX move is proportionate: a 1.24% SPX decline alongside a sub-7% VIX move suggests options markets absorbed the selling with some orderliness, which is consistent with the positive gamma structure still exerting *some* stabilizing influence even if it couldn't prevent the trend entirely.

At **18.43**, VIX remains well below structurally elevated territory (20-25+). This is not a fear event — it is a repricing event. The distinction matters for positioning: this is not a panic-driven dislocation but a rate-driven fundamental reassessment. That argues against chasing protection aggressively at current premiums and in favor of structured, defined-risk positioning heading into next week.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Overnight and Next-Session Framing

**Closing Gamma Regime: POSITIVE** — No GEX flip level is defined; structure is anchored by put/call walls.

**Key Structural Levels:**

| Level | Price | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,500 | Ceiling; dealer hedging creates resistance above this |

| S1 | 7,371.0 | First meaningful support below put wall |
| S2 | 7,334.0 | Extended downside in a trend continuation scenario |

**The most important observation for the overnight:** SPX closed at **7,408.5**, a mere **8.5 points above the 7,400 Put Wall.** This is a critical proximity risk. The put wall acts as a gravitational support in a positive gamma regime — dealers will be incentivized to buy dips near this level as put delta hedging intensifies. However, a clean break and close *below* 7,400 over the weekend or early Monday would meaningfully shift the risk picture.

The **expected move for tomorrow is ±86 points**, framing a range of roughly **7,322 to 7,494**. This brackets S2 on the downside and approaches R2/Call Wall on the upside.

**Tomorrow's Catalyst Load:** Low. The calendar is light, which argues for range-bound activity — particularly in a positive gamma structure with no defined flip level. However, the week-ahead risk is elevated with **FOMC Minutes** on the docket. Weekend headlines on global bond markets, central bank rhetoric, or commodity moves could set the overnight tone.

**Weekend Risk:** The global bond selloff narrative is live and ongoing. Any further deterioration in sovereign yields over the weekend — particularly in JGBs or Gilts, which have been transmitting global rate pressure — could gap SPX lower on Monday's open through the put wall. This tail risk deserves respect given Friday's close proximity to 7,400.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications for Next Session

**Regime: Positive Gamma | Catalyst Load: Low | VIX: 18.43 | Proximity to Put Wall: Critical**

In a **positive gamma regime with a light catalyst calendar**, the mechanical bias favors mean-reversion and range-bound behavior. Dealers are long options (long gamma) and will **sell into rallies and buy into dips** to maintain delta neutrality. This creates a natural dampening effect on directional moves — the same hedging force that partially cushioned today's macro-driven selloff.

**Preferred Structures:**

- **Credit Spreads / Iron Condors:** The positive gamma environment with a light Friday-into-Monday catalyst load supports income-oriented structures. A defined-range trade anchored around the 7,400 Put Wall (floor) and 7,500 Call Wall (ceiling) has mechanical logic — dealer flows tend to reinforce these boundaries in positive gamma regimes.
  - A **put credit spread** positioned at or just below S1 (7,371) offers a buffer below the put wall if the structure holds.
  - A **call credit spread** capped near R2/Call Wall (7,483-7,500) respects the ceiling created by dealer hedging above those levels.

- **Avoid naked directional exposure over the weekend:** The proximity to the 7,400 Put Wall introduces binary weekend risk. If macro headlines (bond market, central bank signals) gap the index through that level, positive gamma's stabilizing effect diminishes materially below it, and S1 (7,371) / S2 (7,334) come into play quickly. A gap lower in a now-more-volatile environment could see dealer hedging pressure intensify on the downside.

- **SPY RSI(14) at 66** and price sitting **+7.3% above the 50-SMA** and **+9.4% above the 200-SMA** are significant context inputs. The index is technically stretched. Selling call premium against these extended readings carries mean-reversion logic beyond just the gamma regime argument.

**Week-Ahead Sizing Note:** FOMC Minutes create event risk mid-week. Position sizing should be kept moderate — do not deploy full notional in structures that cannot tolerate a volatility spike. If VIX pushes through 20 on a rates-driven catalyst next week, premium levels will reprice meaningfully.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today was a macro-driven trend day that bent a positive gamma regime without breaking it. The close at 7,408.5 — just above a critical 7,400 Put Wall — is the overnight risk anchor. With a light catalyst load tomorrow, positive gamma mechanics should provide containment within the 7,371-7,446 band absent new macro shocks. The primary tail risk is a weekend bond-market deterioration that gaps the index below the put wall, removing that structural support. Manage size accordingly into next week's FOMC Minutes.

# Evening Derivatives Recap — Monday, May 18, 2026

*Prepared for institutional distribution | Post-close analysis*

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro Rotation, Not Gamma

Today's tape was driven by **macro and rates repricing**, not 0DTE or gamma mechanics. The primary catalyst was a partial reversal of last week's bond selloff — the 10-year yield oscillated through a tight range before settling at **4.61% (+2bp)**, while Brent crude sold off on reports the U.S. was considering a temporary waiver on Iranian oil sanctions.

The result was **selective rotation** rather than directional conviction. Per market context data, **Energy (+1.6%) and Consumer Defensive (+1.5%) led**, while **Technology (-1.9%) and Utilities (-1.6%) lagged** — a 3.5% cross-sector dispersion signature that is inconsistent with a broad risk-on or risk-off trend. This was a **sector-reallocation session**, with participants repositioning ahead of Wednesday's Nvidia earnings rather than making macro directional bets.

SPX closed at **7403.05 (-0.08%)** on a session range of just **1.10%** (High: 7434.06 / Low: 7353.17).

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Forecast Contradicted — Dampening Prevailed Despite Negative Gamma

The morning called for a **TREND (DOWN) day at 75% confidence** within a **negative gamma regime**. What actually materialized was a **range-bound session** — a meaningful contradiction worth unpacking.

In a negative gamma environment, dealers are **short options** and hedge by **buying into rallies and selling into declines**, which ordinarily amplifies directional moves. A true negative gamma trending session should have seen momentum build once a direction was established.

Instead, the tape was choppy and mean-reverting. Two factors likely explain the deviation:

- **Macro cross-currents were offsetting.** Yield softening was constructive for equities, but crude weakness and tech underperformance created competing sector headwinds. No single narrative dominated enough to ignite a trend.
- **Positioning inertia ahead of Nvidia (Wednesday).** With a defining catalyst 48 hours out, large participants appeared unwilling to chase directional exposure. That discretionary hesitation can mute gamma amplification even in a structurally negative regime.

The closing regime **remains NEGATIVE**, but today's session is a reminder that negative gamma is a *necessary*, not *sufficient*, condition for trending behavior — macro cross-currents and event proximity can absorb the amplification dynamic.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: VIX Confirms, But RSI Raises a Flag

VIX closed at **17.82 (-3.31%)**, declining on a flat-to-down SPX session — this is directionally coherent. Implied volatility compression on a non-trending day is consistent with the absence of realized volatility; the options market did not price in additional near-term risk premium despite the elevated technical setup.

However, the VIX level itself warrants attention in context: at 17.82, it is not complacent, but it has compressed meaningfully from recent peaks. Dealer flows were likely a **secondary, not primary**, factor in today's character, given the macro-dominant narrative. There is no evidence of aggressive 0DTE activity distorting intraday flows in either direction.

The more pressing technical signal: **SPY RSI(14) is at 71 (overbought), with price +7.0% above the 50-SMA and +9.3% above the 200-SMA.** This is an elevated mean-reversion risk profile. The market is extended by any standard deviation measure, and breadth divergence signals flagged in news flow suggest the rally is narrowing internally. This does not mean an immediate drawdown is imminent, but it raises the cost of unhedged long exposure significantly.

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Compressed Range, Fed Waller at Open

**Key levels to frame:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7425 | Overhead resistance; dealer supply above |
| Put Wall | 7400 | Near-term support floor; dealer sensitivity below |

**Catalysts to monitor:**

- **Fed Governor Waller Speech (09:00 ET)** — Medium impact, but the first Fed communication of the week and arriving before cash open. A gap open through either the Put Wall (7400) or toward R1 (7440) on Waller commentary should be taken seriously — in a negative gamma environment, **dealer hedging will amplify, not dampen, any directional move that establishes itself.**

## 5. Strategy Implications: Negative Gamma, but Respect the Compression

**The regime:** Closing negative gamma with no flip level. In this environment, dealers are **short options** and must **buy into strength and sell into weakness** to hedge — this configuration *favors trending and breakout behavior* when a catalyst provides direction.

**The complication:** Tomorrow's catalyst load is moderate, not high. Waller is a medium-impact event. The 25-point corridor between Put Wall (7400) and Call Wall (7425) creates a natural chop zone absent a clear directional trigger.

**Strategic framework for tomorrow:**

S1 at 7366 becomes the first objective, with S2 at 7329 as an extended target. **Defined-risk bearish structures (debit put spreads, bear verticals targeting the 7366–7329 zone) are structurally aligned** with this scenario.

- **If Waller is dovish and SPX breaks above 7425 (Call Wall):** Dealers buy into strength, momentum builds toward R1 at 7440 and potentially R2 at 7477 on follow-through. **Debit call spreads targeting R1/R2** make sense, though the overbought RSI (71) and proximity to the ±83 expected move ceiling demand disciplined profit targets rather than open-ended long deltas.

- **If SPX oscillates between 7400 and 7425 without resolution:** The macro rotation theme from today continues. No structural edge in either direction. Consider **reducing position size and waiting for a level break** rather than fading or chasing within the wall corridor.

**Income structures (iron condors, credit spreads) are NOT structurally favored** in a negative gamma regime — the amplification dynamic creates adverse gamma exposure for short volatility positions when moves occur.

**Risk management note:** With FOMC Minutes later in the week and SPX technically stretched, position sizing should reflect elevated week-level risk. Do not carry oversized overnight exposure into a session where a Fed speaker opens at 09:00 with no session buffer.

---

*Risk is a function of what you don't hedge. Trade accordingly.*

The sector rotation data confirms the defensive pivot clearly: **Healthcare (+1.3%) and Utilities (+1.1%)** led, while **Communication Services (-1.2%) and Financial Services (-1.0%)** lagged. Any language suggesting a higher-for-longer or rate-hike-leaning posture would validate today's rates-driven selloff and — in the context of **negative gamma** — risk amplifying the downside move through 7,340 and toward 7,280–7,317. A dovish surprise provides relief but must clear the Call Wall at 7,400 to materially change the narrative.

**The bond auction at 1 PM feeding directly into FOMC Minutes at 2 PM creates a two-hour risk window that will define the weekly close setup.** Overnight positioning should account for this binary compression.

---

## 5. STRATEGY IMPLICATIONS

**Regime: Negative Gamma | Catalyst Load: High | Risk Outlook: Elevated**

In a **negative gamma environment**, dealers are **short options** and must hedge by **buying strength and selling weakness** — their flows reinforce trends rather than dampen them. This is not an environment that rewards passive income structures.

**Appropriate structures for Wednesday:**

- **Defined-risk directional (debit spreads / put verticals):** With the Put Wall at 7,340 as a key level, a **put debit spread** targeting a move toward 7,317–7,280 makes structural sense if the FOMC Minutes carry a hawkish tone. Width should account for the ±84 expected move. Define risk explicitly — negative gamma amplifies moves in both directions.

- **Event-bracketed structures around FOMC Minutes:** A narrow **strangle or straddle entry pre-2PM** captures the binary outcome of the minutes, but elevated VIX at 18.06 means premium is not cheap. Size accordingly and consider debit-spread risk-reduction rather than naked long volatility.

- **Avoid credit spreads and iron condors.** With negative gamma in force, range-bound premium decay strategies are misaligned with the dealer hedging dynamic. Dealers are not absorbing moves — they are extending them.

- **Call Wall at 7,400 as a cap for defined upside targets:** Should positioning lean long, respect this level as a structural ceiling on any recovery trade. Do not set upside targets above 7,400 without confirmation of a regime shift.

**Risk Management Note:** Week-ahead risk remains elevated with **CB Consumer Confidence (May)** on the horizon. Position sizing should remain conservative relative to normal run-rate given the combination of a negative gamma regime, a technically extended tape, rising yields, and a high-impact Fed event tomorrow afternoon.

# Evening Derivatives Recap — Wednesday, May 20, 2026

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro Catalyst Overwhelmed the Gamma Framework

Today's move was unambiguously **macro-driven**. Reports of a U.S.-Iran nuclear deal approaching final stages hit the tape intraday, triggering a sharp risk-on rotation that lifted SPX +1.08% to close at **7,432.97** — within three handles of the session high of **7,435.69**. The move's character was telling: this was not a grind. Price established an early low at **7,357.46**, found footing, and then trended with momentum and limited pullback through the afternoon.

Sector internals confirm the macro thesis. **Consumer Cyclical (+2.3%)** and **Technology (+1.5%)** led — classic risk-on beneficiaries of a geopolitical decompression trade. On the other side, **Energy (-0.8%)** and **Consumer Defensive (-0.6%)** lagged, consistent with oil's ~5% decline on Iran truce optimism removing the geopolitical risk premium from crude. The **3.1% sector dispersion** is notable: this was a rotational, thesis-driven move, not a broad-based beta lift. Bond markets validated the narrative — the **10-year yield fell 10bps to 4.57%**, providing an additional tailwind for equities as the Iran premium unwound simultaneously in both crude and rates.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Morning Negative Gamma Call Was Correct — But the Outcome Shifted Intraday

The morning regime was called **NEGATIVE GAMMA** with a **HIGH VOLATILITY** day type at 70% confidence. That framing was structurally sound: in a negative gamma environment, dealers are **short options** and must **buy rallies and sell dips** to hedge their exposure, which amplifies directional moves. Today's session delivered exactly that amplification character — a 1.06% range with a strong trending structure, not the choppy, mean-reverting behavior that would have indicated positive gamma suppression.

The key development, however, is the **regime shift at the close**. By end of session, the gamma regime has **flipped to POSITIVE**. This is a meaningful structural change. The rally absorbed the negative gamma overhang — likely as short-dated puts decayed and call strikes were reached or exceeded — transitioning dealer positioning into net long gamma territory. Going into Thursday, the mechanical tailwind that amplified today's trend is no longer present; in fact, the regime now works in the opposite direction.

**This flip is the most important structural takeaway from today's session.**

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Confirmation With Nuance

VIX closed at **17.44, down 3.44%**, which is directionally consistent with a +1.08% SPX day — fear compressed as price rallied. However, the relationship is worth scrutinizing: a 3.44% VIX decline on a 1.08% SPX gain in a day that started in negative gamma territory suggests that **implied volatility was actively sold into the rally**, not merely marked down passively. Dealers covering short gamma hedges (buying into the rally) and options sellers pressing short vol as the Iran narrative clarified likely contributed to the vol compression.

Critically, VIX at **17.44** sits in a range that is consistent with the regime transition we saw — this level does not signal elevated fear, but it also does not confirm complacency. With **SPY RSI at 68** and price running **+7.0% above the 50-SMA and +9.5% above the 200-SMA**, the technical backdrop is stretched. VIX declining into an overbought tape is a combination that warrants respect — it historically precedes either a continued melt-up or a sharp mean reversion when the narrative catalyst exhausts.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Positive Gamma Regime, Bounded Range, Key Levels in Focus

**Closing Regime: POSITIVE GAMMA**

In a positive gamma environment, dealers are **long options** and hedge by **selling strength and buying weakness**. This creates a natural dampening effect — sessions tend to be range-bound and mean-reverting rather than trending. After today's macro-driven impulse move, a consolidation session Thursday is the base case implied by the regime.

**Key Levels:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,475 | Hard upside ceiling; dealer hedging flows turn to selling above this level |
| R1 | 7,470 | Converges with Call Wall — this is the primary upside boundary |
| R2 | 7,507 | Secondary resistance; would require a fresh catalyst to breach |
| Put Wall | 7,400 | Structural support floor; dealer buying expected at/below this level |
| S1 | 7,396 | Converges with Put Wall — the key downside inflection zone |
| S2 | 7,359 | Matches today's session low almost exactly; a re-test here would be a significant development |
| Expected Move | ±82 pts | Implies a 7,351–7,515 range; consistent with Call Wall as practical ceiling |

The **7,400–7,475 corridor** is the operative range for Thursday. With positive gamma in place, price should gravitate toward mean-reversion within this band unless a catalyst forces a break. The **Put Wall at 7,400** and **Call Wall at 7,475** act as gravitational poles — dealer hedging flows will mechanically resist sustained moves beyond either boundary.

**Overnight and Catalyst Watch:**

The 08:30 ET data cluster is moderate in aggregate impact:
- **Jobless Claims (4-Week Average) and Continuing Claims** (both medium impact) are the primary reads. Any material deterioration in claims would pressure the narrative that the labor market supports current equity valuations — particularly relevant given how stretched technicals already are.
- **Philly Fed Business Conditions** will be monitored for regional manufacturing pulse, though low-impact alone.
- **Housing data** is noise in the current macro context unless it dramatically misses.

None of these catalysts individually carry the weight to re-ignite the kind of macro-driven move we saw today. The Iran truce narrative is now partially priced. The more meaningful week-level risk remains **CB Consumer Confidence** later this week — size positions accordingly.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Positive Gamma Argues for Income Structures, Caution on Chasers

With the regime now **POSITIVE GAMMA** and dealer hedging flows set to dampen moves, the structural edge shifts toward **premium collection and range-bound income strategies**:

- **Iron Condors or Credit Spreads** framed around the 7,400–7,475 range offer logical placement given the Put Wall/Call Wall boundaries. The positive gamma regime provides mechanical support for the range-bound thesis.
- **Short-dated (0–2 DTE) call spreads** capped at or just below the 7,475 Call Wall are a defined-risk approach for traders who want to fade the potential for a continued breakout absent a fresh catalyst.
- **Avoid naked long premium (debit spreads, outright calls) as a primary posture** — positive gamma regimes systematically erode directional premium through time as dealers smooth out moves. The edge for long gamma structures is degraded.

**One critical risk to the positive gamma assumption:** if price breaks decisively through the **7,400 Put Wall to the downside** — particularly toward the S2/session low cluster at **7,357–7,359** — the regime could be challenged. A break of that magnitude could re-activate dealer short-side hedging pressure. Position accordingly: **define your risk on the downside at 7,396–7,400**, as a clean break there changes the mechanical picture.

On the upside, **RSI at 68 and price stretched 9.5% above the 200-SMA** argues against aggressively chasing through 7,475 without confirmation. Momentum traders who fade the regime should be aware they are swimming against the dealer flow in a positive gamma environment — that is a headwind, not a tailwind.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today's session was a textbook negative gamma amplification move on a macro catalyst (Iran). The regime has since flipped positive, resetting the mechanical framework. Tomorrow is a consolidation/digestion session by default unless claims data surprises materially. Trade the range (7,400–7,475), respect the walls, and keep position sizing disciplined ahead of elevated week-level risks.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy | EOD, May 20, 2026*

## 2. Regime Validation: Positive Gamma Confirmed the Compression, But Barely

The morning regime call was **POSITIVE gamma**, and the session broadly validated it — but only superficially. Yes, the range was compressed. Yes, the session was mean-reverting in character. Yes, dealers in a long-gamma environment were selling into the rally and buying weakness, which capped the upside near **7,539** and absorbed selling near **7,501**.

However, the **REGIME CAVEAT is critical here**: at the close, SPX sits just **14.1 points above the GEX flip level at 7,505.0**. That is not a comfortable buffer. The regime is **borderline positive** — it held today, but barely. The forecast called for high volatility, which did not materialize, and that miss is at least partially attributable to the positive gamma cushion absorbing the macro impulse. Had yields moved more aggressively or the geopolitical headline been more destabilizing, a slip below 7,505 could have flipped regime dynamics intraday and turned the tape sharply directional in either direction.

**Net verdict**: The positive gamma regime was validated by the session's tight range, but the proximity to the flip level means we are operating on a thin margin of structural support. Do not characterize today's calm as durable.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: A Divergence That Deserves Attention

Here is the tension in today's tape: **SPX gained 0.62%, yet VIX closed UP 2.54% to 17.01**. That is an atypical divergence. In an orderly, low-vol positive-gamma session with yields falling and risk assets bid, VIX should have compressed — or at minimum stayed flat. The fact that implied volatility rose alongside spot suggests **the market is quietly pricing in forward risk even as the headline index grinds higher**.

This is worth flagging explicitly. The SPY RSI(14) sits at **72 — overbought territory** — with price running **+7.7% above the 50-SMA and +10.6% above the 200-SMA**. These are extended readings by any institutional measure. The VIX uptick in conjunction with these technical extremes suggests that volatility buyers are active at these levels — hedgers are paying up for protection even as the index makes new highs. This is not a complacent market; it is an extended market where sophisticated participants are quietly fading the rally through options.

The gamma regime kept the session contained today, but the VIX divergence is a warning signal that the structural calm may not persist into the back half of the week.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Borderline Regime, Light Catalysts, and Consequential Levels

**Overnight and Early Session:**
The first event of note is **Fed Logan's speech at 04:00 ET** (medium impact). With the 10-year already down 6 basis points today and rates markets sensitive to Fed tone, any hawkish lean from Logan — particularly around inflation persistence or the pace of cuts — could pressure the long-end and introduce early overnight volatility. Watch for futures reaction at open in the context of today's close sitting uncomfortably close to the GEX flip.

**Key Levels for Wednesday:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,530.0 | Near-term ceiling; dealer short-gamma hedge resistance above |
| R1 | 7,557.0 | First technical resistance above the call wall |
| R2 | 7,594.0 | Extended upside target if breakout sustains |
| GEX Flip | ~7,505.0 | Regime boundary — critical to watch on any pullback |
| S1 | 7,482.0 | First meaningful support below flip level |
| Put Wall | 7,450.0 | Structural dealer support floor |
| S2 | 7,444.0 | Converges near the Put Wall; high-conviction support zone |

The **Call Wall at 7,530** is the most important near-term level. Today's high of 7,539 briefly pierced it intraday but closed below at 7,519. In a borderline positive-gamma regime, the Call Wall acts as a gravitational ceiling — dealers managing short call exposure will resist sustained moves above this level. A clean, volume-confirmed break and close above 7,530 would be technically significant and could force gamma-driven acceleration toward R1 at 7,557.

Conversely, any pullback that takes SPX below the **GEX flip at ~7,505** shifts dealer hedging behavior meaningfully. Below that level, if the regime transitions toward negative gamma, dealers would be **buying into further declines and selling into bounces** — amplifying rather than dampening moves. S1 at 7,482 and ultimately the Put Wall at 7,450 would become the next reference points in that scenario. Given the 10-point gap between today's close (7,519) and the flip level (7,505), this is not a theoretical risk — it is the dominant near-term structural concern.

The **expected move of ±81 points** frames tomorrow's implied range at roughly **7,438 to 7,600**. That aligns neatly with the Put Wall (7,450) as the lower bound and R2 (7,594) as the upper bound — a coherent structure. However, with only low-impact Richmond and Dallas Fed data in the morning (10:00–10:30 ET), the catalyst load is light. Light catalysts in a borderline regime and overbought tape create asymmetric risk — small surprises can have outsized impact when positioning is stretched.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Regime Uncertainty Demands Defined Risk

**Gamma Regime Context:**
Tomorrow opens in a **borderline positive gamma** environment. In a confirmed positive-gamma regime, dealer behavior is to **sell rallies and buy dips** — acting as a natural stabilizer. This supports range-bound, mean-reverting sessions and favors income-style structures. However, given the proximity to the GEX flip (14 points), that stabilizing behavior is conditional and fragile. A move through 7,505 flips the dynamic — in negative gamma, dealers **buy rallies and sell dips**, amplifying trend moves in either direction. Position sizing must account for both scenarios.

**Structures to Consider:**

- **If the regime holds positive and range-bound behavior persists** (spot remains above 7,505, below 7,530): The setup favors **iron condors or short-width credit spreads** anchored around the 7,450–7,530 band. The Call Wall and Put Wall define a natural premium decay corridor. The VIX at 17.01 provides modest but not excessive premium to sell.

- **If spot breaks above the Call Wall (7,530) with conviction**: This is a potential gamma squeeze scenario. Defined-risk **call debit spreads** toward R1 (7,557) capture the move without naked exposure into an overbought tape. Avoid selling calls into a confirmed breakout above the wall.

- **If spot breaks below the GEX flip (7,505)**: This is the highest-risk scenario and the one the market appears to be quietly hedging via today's VIX uptick. **Put debit spreads** targeting S1 (7,482) or the Put Wall (7,450) offer defined-risk downside participation. In a regime flip to negative gamma, moves toward these levels could be fast and non-linear.

**Risk Management Framework:**

The **week-level risk is elevated** with **PCE Price Index MoM and Personal Income MoM** due later in the week. These are the highest-impact prints on the calendar and will directly speak to the Fed's rate path narrative. Any structures initiated Wednesday should be sized to survive a PCE surprise — either direction. With SPY RSI at 72 and price running more than 10% above the 200-SMA, the risk/reward of adding fresh long delta exposure at these levels is unfavorable. **Asymmetric positioning — long vol or defined-risk bearish hedges — deserves allocation as a portfolio offset**, particularly into Thursday's PCE print.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today confirmed that positive gamma can suppress volatility even against a constructive macro backdrop — but the 14-point gap to the GEX flip is a structural fragility, not a comfort zone. The VIX rising alongside SPX is the clearest signal that smart money is not complacent here. Tomorrow's catalyst load is light, which cuts both ways: quiet sessions near regime boundaries can resolve quickly when the first meaningful order flow hits. Watch 7,505 and 7,530 as the two levels that define the character of the week.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy*
