# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Wednesday, May 27, 2026 | SPX 7520.36 | VIX 16.29

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro-Led Rotation, Gamma Contained the Tape

Today's session was driven by a combination of **macro headlines and positioning flows**, not a clean earnings or technical catalyst. The dominant intraday narrative centered on **U.S.-Iran diplomatic progress**, which pulled WTI crude sharply lower (approximately -4% to -4.5%) and modestly eased broader risk premiums. Lower oil fed directly into a rates-supportive backdrop — the 10-year yield settled at **4.48%, down 2bp** — which provided a mild equity tailwind but was insufficient to generate directional conviction.

The result was exactly what the session type delivered: **range-bound price action** spanning just **31 points (7499.72 – 7530.72)**, or a **0.41% range**. SPX closed essentially unchanged at **+0.02%**.

Sector rotation told the more honest story. **Consumer Defensive (+0.5%) and Communication Services (+0.4%)** led — a rotation into relative safety and quality, not a risk-on acceleration. **Technology (-1.1%) and Utilities (-1.7%)** lagged materially. The Technology underperformance was consistent with profit-taking commentary across semiconductor names following a near-vertical run in recent sessions. **Micron** was a notable idiosyncratic exception on the positive side, extending gains from its prior session surge. But broad-based tech selling confirmed this was a **rotation day, not a leadership day**.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Morning Forecast Confirmed

The morning regime called **Positive Gamma / Mixed-to-Chop**, with **55% confidence**. Today's session validated that call cleanly.

In a positive gamma environment, dealers are **long options** and hedge by **selling into strength and buying weakness** — behavior that mechanically suppresses directional range. A 31-point session range on an index trading above 7500 is a textbook positive gamma outcome. The tape repeatedly failed to sustain moves in either direction, mean-reverting through the session as dealer hedging absorbed both the Iran-headline dip and any follow-through from Micron-led optimism.

**The morning call holds. No regime contradiction to explain.**

That said, the important caveat carries forward into tomorrow's setup.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: VIX Confirmed, Dealers Were the Dampener

VIX closing at **16.29, down 4.24%** on a day where SPX was essentially flat is an informative divergence. This is not a fear-compression driven by a strong rally — it's vol being **bled out of the market structurally**, consistent with time decay and dealer hedging flows neutralizing realized volatility. When dealers are long gamma and actively fading moves, realized vol collapses, and implied vol follows.

The VIX print confirms two things: (1) the positive gamma environment was genuine and operative today, and (2) there was no underlying stress accumulating beneath the quiet tape. The Iran headline was absorbed, not amplified — a positive gamma outcome.

The rates move (10Y -2bp to 4.48%) was supportive but modest. The **2Y-10Y spread at +48bp** reflects a normalized curve that is not generating incremental pressure on risk assets from the rates complex at this moment.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Regime Is Borderline, PCE Is the Catalyst That Matters

**This is the critical risk framing for tomorrow.**

The closing regime is **positive gamma**, but spot closed at **7520.36** — just **9.6 points below the GEX flip level at 7530.0**. The regime must be characterized as **borderline and transitional**, not firm. The **Call Wall sits at 7530** — the same level as the GEX flip. This is the most important single level on the board heading into tomorrow.

**Key Levels for Thursday:**
- **7530 (Call Wall / GEX Flip):** The line in the sand. A sustained break and hold above 7530 transitions the regime toward negative gamma, which would change dealer hedging behavior materially — from dampening moves to amplifying them. Any breakout attempt above this level deserves respect.
- **7558 (R1) / 7596 (R2):** Resistance targets if a regime flip occurs and momentum accelerates. Under current positive gamma, these are unlikely to be tested unless a hot data miss aggressively unwinds the call wall.
- **7483 (S1) / 7475 (Put Wall):** Downside support cluster. The Put Wall at 7475 represents meaningful dealer long-delta positioning that should slow selling pressure. A close below 7475 would be structurally concerning.
- **7445 (S2):** Extended downside if the Put Wall fails to hold.
- **Expected Move: ±77 points** — implying a range of approximately **7443 – 7597** for the session.

**The Catalyst Stack at 08:30 ET is dense and high-impact:**
- **Core PCE Price Index YoY (Apr)** — *the* number. The Fed's preferred inflation gauge. Any print above consensus risks repricing the rate path and pressuring equities through a combination of duration selling and vol expansion.
- **PCE Price Index YoY (Apr)** — reinforces or contradicts Core PCE directionally.
- **Personal Income MoM (Apr)** — consumption capacity signal; a strong read in an already-overbought tape could paradoxically pressure markets if it raises stagflation concerns.
- **Jobless Claims 4-Week Average** and **Corporate Profits QoQ (Q1)** — secondary but additive to the macro picture.

**The overnight risk is asymmetric:** SPY RSI(14) is sitting at **74 (overbought)**, price is **+7.4% above the 50-SMA** and **+10.5% above the 200-SMA**. Mean-reversion risk is objectively elevated. A hot Core PCE print does not need to be a disaster to cause a meaningful correction — the technical setup is stretched enough that a modestly above-consensus inflation print could catalyze a flush toward 7483-7475 with limited cushion above.

Conversely, a benign or below-consensus Core PCE print removes the primary remaining macro overhang, which — combined with a potential push above 7530 and a gamma flip — could create an environment where moves are amplified to the upside. **The Call Wall at 7530 becomes the first tell.** Watch pre-market price action relative to 7530 before sizing any directional positioning.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Conditional Structuring Around the 7530 Hinge

The strategy approach for Thursday is explicitly conditional on regime and data.

**Scenario A — Core PCE benign, SPX holds 7530 and regime flips negative gamma:**
Dealer hedging behavior shifts. In a **negative gamma** environment, dealers are **short options** and must **buy rallies and sell dips** to hedge, which amplifies directional moves. In this scenario, a breakout above 7530 becomes self-reinforcing. Structures that benefit from directional extension — **debit call spreads, defined-risk directional verticals targeting R1 at 7558** — become appropriate. However, given the overbought technicals, sizing discipline remains paramount. Do not chase extended.

**Scenario B — Core PCE above consensus, SPX fails to hold 7530, regime stays positive or retreats:**
The market remains in (or reverts more firmly into) positive gamma. Dealer flows continue to dampen moves. This is an environment where **income structures — iron condors anchored between 7475 and 7530, or credit spreads using the put wall as a lower boundary** — reflect the mechanical behavior correctly. In positive gamma, the dealer hedging suppresses realized vol, which is the income trader's friend.

**Scenario C — Hot PCE print + technical exhaustion triggers flush below 7483:**
A move through S1 (7483) toward the Put Wall (7475) warrants monitoring for a more aggressive vol spike. If VIX breaks above the low-to-mid 18 range, reassess income structures for gamma risk. Defined-risk put spreads below 7475 would be early, but worth having in the contingency toolkit if the Put Wall shows signs of failing.

**Structural note on RSI:** With SPY at RSI 74, mean-reversion premium is elevated. For traders running short-gamma income structures, the current positive gamma regime is mechanically supportive, but the stretched technical backdrop means the *direction* of the next significant move — when it comes — will likely be swift. Keep expiries short, stay within the expected move, and treat the 7530 Call Wall as the level that tells you whether tomorrow is a chop day or a breakout day.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today delivered exactly what positive gamma promised — a contained, directionless session absorbed by dealer hedging flows. Tomorrow, the regime is on the knife's edge at 7530, and a triple-header of Core PCE, PCE, and Personal Income hits at 08:30. The data, not the tape structure, will determine which scenario we're trading. Size accordingly.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy*

# Evening Derivatives Recap — Thursday, May 28, 2026

*Prepared for Professional Distribution | Head of Derivatives Strategy*

---

## 1. Session Flow: What Drove Today's Tape

Today's +0.58% advance in SPX (close: 7,563.63) was macro-driven at its core, not a 0DTE or gamma-mechanical session. The primary catalyst was geopolitical: reports of a US-Iran ceasefire extension and progress toward a broader deal materially reduced the geopolitical risk premium embedded in oil and risk assets. Equity futures had been under pressure earlier in the session as traders monitored the conflict and positioned ahead of inflation data, but the ceasefire headlines shifted sentiment decisively risk-on intraday.

The sector rotation confirmed the narrative. Healthcare (+1.6%) and Industrials (+1.2%) led — consistent with a relief-driven, "remove the tail risk" rotation rather than a growth or momentum-chasing session. The defensive laggards, Consumer Defensive (-1.0%) and Utilities (-1.2%), underperformed sharply, reflecting a classic flight *out* of safety proxies as geopolitical fear receded. This is a textbook geopolitical de-risking unwind, not a tech-led momentum tape.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Positive Gamma, But a Trend Day

The morning called for **HIGH VOLATILITY** with **70% confidence** under a **positive gamma regime** — and the session did not fully cooperate with that framing. What actually printed was a **moderate trend day**, with the session range coming in at just **0.81%** (High: 7,568.72 / Low: 7,508.04). That is a relatively contained directional move, not a high-volatility expansion.

This is a partial contradiction worth unpacking carefully. Positive gamma regimes are associated with dampened, mean-reverting sessions — dealers long options, hedging by selling rallies and buying dips. By that mechanic, a contained range is *consistent* with positive gamma. However, the moderate trend character — sustained directional drift rather than a choppy mean-reverting session — suggests the macro catalyst (ceasefire headlines) was strong enough to override some of the gamma dampening effect, pushing price in one direction without triggering significant mean-reversion flows.

**Assessment:** The positive gamma regime held structurally (range was not explosive), but the macro news provided enough directional impulse to produce a trending rather than oscillating session. The regime was validated in *magnitude* but not fully in *character*.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Confirmation Across the Board

VIX closed at **15.74, down -3.38%**, which is fully consistent with the SPX directional advance and the geopolitical risk reduction narrative. The VIX decline confirms that the session's bid was genuine risk-on sentiment — not a short-covering squeeze that leaves vol elevated — and that dealers were not forced into disorderly hedging activity. The orderly vol compression alongside a contained range is precisely what you would expect with dealers in a positive gamma posture: absorbing order flow, smoothing price action, and not amplifying directional moves.

The 10Y yield falling 3bp to **4.45%** is notable in this context. A rates rally alongside an equity advance — both happening simultaneously — suggests the market read the Iran deal as reducing macro uncertainty broadly, allowing both bonds and stocks to price in a lower risk premium. This is not a stagflation signal; it is a cleaner geopolitical relief trade.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Levels, Regime, and Catalysts

**Closing Gamma Regime: POSITIVE | No GEX Flip Level Active**

With no GEX flip level in play, regime framing must rely entirely on structural walls and technical pivots. The closing positive gamma environment carries into tomorrow's setup, meaning dealers remain in a position where their hedging activity *dampens* directional moves — selling strength into resistance and supporting dips near support.

**Key Levels for Friday, May 29:**

| Level | Price | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| R2 | 7,639.0 | Upper extension — unlikely in positive gamma without a major catalyst |
| **Call Wall** | **7,600.0** | Primary upside ceiling; dealer supply likely emerges here |

| S2 | 7,488.0 | Secondary support; loss of this level would be structurally significant |

**Expected Move: ±75 points** — framing a fair value range of approximately **7,489 – 7,639** for Friday's session.

**Tomorrow's Catalyst Schedule:**
- **02:00 ET — Fed Kashkari Speech [Medium]:** Overnight risk. Any hawkish surprise could pressure futures before the domestic open.
- **06:50 ET — Fed Schmid Speech [Low]:** Pre-market noise; unlikely to move markets meaningfully.

In a positive gamma environment, that ceiling will be sticky. A gap toward 7,600 at the open should be treated as a fading opportunity unless macro data or Fed commentary provides a strong directional impulse sufficient to break dealer supply.

**Technical Overlay:** SPY RSI at **76 (overbought)**, with price running **+7.8% above the 50-SMA** and **+11.0% above the 200-SMA**. These are levels historically associated with elevated mean-reversion risk. The positive gamma regime reinforces this — the mechanical backdrop *supports* containment near current levels rather than further extension.

## 5. Strategy Implications: Structure Fits the Regime

**Regime: Positive Gamma | Catalyst Load: Moderate | Overbought Technicals**

In a positive gamma environment, dealer hedging activity dampens directional moves — dealers sell into rallies and buy into dips, compressing range. Combined with a hard technical ceiling at the Call Wall (7,600) and overbought RSI, tomorrow's session sets up as a **range-compression, mean-reverting environment** — not a trending one.

**Favored Structures:**
- **Iron Condors / Credit Spreads:** The 7,550 Put Wall to 7,600 Call Wall range defines a natural short-premium corridor. Premium sellers with strikes positioned at or beyond those walls capture the structural support/resistance mechanics with gamma working in their favor.
- **Call Spread Shorts / Bear Call Spreads:** Given the proximity to the Call Wall at 7,600 and overbought technicals, defined-risk bearish call structures above 7,600 offer a compelling asymmetry. A bear call spread using the 7,600/7,640 strikes captures the regime ceiling with limited upside risk.
- **Put Spreads for Tail Hedging:** S1 at 7,526 and the Put Wall at 7,550 should provide dealer-supported dip-buying. However, given the triple Fed speaker risk in the morning window and the goods trade data at 8:30, a modest put spread hedge below the Put Wall is prudent portfolio management — not a directional bet.

**What to Avoid:** Long premium / long volatility structures are structurally disadvantaged in positive gamma with VIX at 15.74 and compressing. Buying straddles or strangles into a dampened regime bleeds theta without the catalyst load to justify it. If tomorrow's Fed speakers deliver a genuinely hawkish surprise, reassess — but that is not the base case.

**Week-Level Risk Caveat:** The risk outlook for the week remains **elevated** with JOLTs Job Openings (Apr) still ahead. Position sizing should reflect that tail risk is not fully resolved. Keep structures sized to allow adjustment if the macro narrative shifts.

---

*Risk is not a direction. It is a dimension. Trade accordingly.*

# Evening Derivatives Recap
### Friday, May 29, 2026 | Close of Session

---

## 1. Session Flow: What Drove Today

Today's session was macro-led, not gamma-driven. The dominant catalyst was geopolitical de-escalation — specifically, reports of a 60-day U.S.–Iran memorandum of understanding, the reopening of the Strait of Hormuz, and an end to U.S. naval blockade posture in the region. Crude oil dropped sharply, from roughly $95/bbl to sub-$90/bbl, stripping pressure from inflation expectations and freeing risk appetite. Technology led the charge (+0.5%), with the Nasdaq-100 posting a record close. Communication Services (-1.1%) and Consumer Defensive (-1.7%) were the session's notable laggards — a classic risk-on rotation pattern.

The inflation data that landed today — April PCE at 3.8% y/y headline, core PCE at 3.3% — was objectively hot. Under normal conditions, that print would have driven rates volatility and equity pressure. Instead, it was absorbed with minimal disruption as the geopolitical narrative dominated. The 10Y yield ended unchanged at 4.45%, which itself tells a story: bond markets acknowledged the inflation data but did not reprice materially, likely because the oil collapse embedded a forward disinflationary signal. Month-end flows likely provided an additional stabilizing bid through the close, helping SPX finish May on a firm note.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Positive Gamma Confirmed

The morning regime call was **Positive Gamma**, with a day-type forecast of **Mixed/Chop** at 55% confidence. Today's actual session printed as **Range Bound** — a tight 0.47% range (7563.55 – 7599.38) on a +0.22% close. This is textbook positive gamma behavior.

Dealers, positioned long options, hedged by selling into strength and buying weakness throughout the session, compressing realized volatility and anchoring price action. The session never developed momentum in either direction despite a genuinely meaningful macro catalyst. That the market could absorb both a hot PCE print and a major geopolitical headline — and still only move 36 points range — validates how powerful dealer hedging flows can be in a deeply positive gamma environment. Regime confirmed. No contradiction to note.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay

VIX closed at **15.32, down 2.67%**, consistent with the SPX grind higher and the compression of realized vol. The VIX/SPX relationship today was directionally correct and internally coherent: risk-on flows drove equity gains, options sellers were rewarded, and the implied volatility surface compressed as the macro uncertainty around Middle East supply disruptions receded.

Dealer flows were clearly a factor in the session's *character*. The macro catalyst was large enough that in a negative gamma environment, today could have been a 1.5–2%+ trending day. Instead, positive gamma mechanics kept the session capped and contained. The high of 7599.38 notably stopped just short of the 7600 Call Wall — not a coincidence. That level represents significant dealer short-call positioning, and the natural headwind from that structural overhang capped intraday upside. This is a clean example of options structure constraining what the tape wanted to do on a geopolitical catalyst.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Overnight and Next Session

**Closing regime: Positive Gamma. No GEX flip level in play** — there is no discrete flip point to anchor around. Key structural levels are the **Put Wall at 7575** and **Call Wall at 7600**, with tomorrow's pivot levels at **R1: 7618 / R2: 7656** on the upside and **S1: 7542 / S2: 7504** on the downside.

Critically, with SPX closing at **7580.06**, the market is currently **sandwiched between the Put Wall (7575) and Call Wall (7600)** — a 25-point compression zone. In a positive gamma regime, this structure is magnetic. The default assumption for the session is mean-reversion and chop within that band, with dealers continuing to sell toward 7600 and support near 7575.

**Fed Chair Powell speaks at 20:30 ET** — that's an after-hours event tonight, not a next-session catalyst in the traditional sense, but any substantive commentary on the PCE data or rate path could set the overnight tone heading into Monday. Given today's hot PCE print, any hawkish acknowledgment from Powell could apply pressure. Watch for gap-risk in Sunday night futures.

Tomorrow's headline risk level is assessed as **low** — light calendar, normal trading conditions expected as a standalone session. However, the **weekly risk outlook is elevated**: Non-Farm Payrolls (May) and the Unemployment Rate are ahead next week. Positioning into month-end/quarter-start should account for elevated gamma compression unwinding as we roll into a new expiry cycle next week.

**Technical note:** SPY RSI sits at **76 — overbought territory** — with price **+7.8% above the 50-SMA and +11.1% above the 200-SMA**. Mean-reversion risk is elevated on a multi-day horizon. This does not imply an imminent reversal in a positive gamma regime, but it is a material backdrop consideration. Extended positioning into next week's NFP event should be sized with this in mind.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

**Regime: Positive Gamma → Range-Bound / Mean-Reverting Environment**

In a positive gamma environment, dealers are long options and hedge by selling rallies and buying dips. This behavior mechanically dampens moves and rewards range-bound structures. For tomorrow — a light-catalyst session within a compressed Put Wall/Call Wall window — the structural setup is well-suited to:

- **Iron Condors** centered around current price (7580), with short strikes near 7600 (Call Wall resistance) and 7542 (S1 / expected move boundary). The 7575–7600 compression zone reinforces the thesis that price will struggle to sustain moves beyond this band.
- **Credit Spreads** (call-side, given overbought technicals): selling the 7600–7620 call spread captures the structural overhead and aligns with dealer hedging flows that create natural resistance at the Call Wall. Risk/reward is skewed to the short side given RSI at 76 and price well extended above moving averages.
- **Avoid naked long premium / long gamma structures for tomorrow's session**. Positive gamma regimes punish premium buyers through realized vol compression. Unless Powell tonight delivers a material hawkish surprise that re-sets the tape, single-session long directional structures are fighting the structural flow.

**Looking ahead to next week:** As we approach NFP, implied volatility should begin to reprice higher into the event window. That means income structures entered today or Monday will face expanding premium — be disciplined about defined-risk positioning and do not carry wide short spreads through the number. The week's elevated risk designation is a regime warning: what is a positive gamma, sell-the-move environment today may not be so by Thursday morning.

---

**Bottom Line:** A well-behaved end to May. Positive gamma did its job, the Call Wall at 7600 held as structural overhead, and macro news delivered a bid without unleashing a breakout. Tomorrow is structurally similar — range-compression between 7575 and 7600 is the base case — with Powell's evening remarks as the primary overnight swing variable. Size with next week's data calendar in view.

*Good weekend. Trade the regime, not the narrative.*

# Evening Derivatives Recap — Monday, June 2, 2026

*Prepared for Professional Circulation | Post-Close | Head of Derivatives Strategy*

---

## Session Verdict

**Macro-led, range-bound grind. Positive gamma contained the tape — but the regime is on a knife's edge heading into a high-impact data week.**

---

## 1. Session Flow

Today's session was driven primarily by **macro and cross-asset momentum** rather than a single catalyst or technical gamma event. Crude oil strength (+3%+), modestly firmer front-end yields, and a constructive earnings backdrop provided the bid under equities from the open. The result was a **+0.27% SPX close at 7,599.96** — respectable on the surface, but the **0.73% session range** (7,562.61 – 7,617.66) tells the real story: this was a compression session, not a trend day.

Sector dispersion was notably wide at **4.1%**, with **Technology (+2.4%)** and **Energy (+0.8%)** leading while **Utilities (-1.7%)** and **Real Estate (-1.0%)** lagged materially. That spread reflects **rotational, not broad-based conviction** — capital recycling into growth and away from rate-sensitive defensives as the 10Y yield nudged to **4.47% (+2bp)**. This is not the profile of a market making a clean directional statement; it is one sorting itself internally.

---

## 2. Regime Validation

The morning call was **Positive Gamma / MIXED-CHOP** at 55% confidence, and the session delivered exactly that — a **range-bound** outcome. The regime call was validated.

However, the validation comes with an important asterisk: **spot closed at 7,599.96 — within 10 points of the GEX flip level at 7,590.** This is not a comfortable positive gamma confirmation; it is a market that spent the bulk of the session straddling the line between dealer hedging regimes. The modest positive close reflects that dealer mean-reversion flows helped absorb the intraday volatility, but the margin is thin. The regime should be treated as **borderline, not firmly positive**.

---

## 3. VIX / Gamma Interplay

The VIX close at **16.05 (+4.77%)** deserves attention. Implied volatility rising alongside a positive — if marginal — SPX close is a **divergence signal**. In a clean positive gamma environment, we would expect VIX to drift lower as realized volatility compresses. The fact that VIX expanded nearly 5% on a session where SPX gained less than a third of a percent suggests:

- **Options buyers are active** at these elevated index levels, likely hedging into the macro catalyst window ahead.
- The market is **not pricing complacency** at record-adjacent levels — a modest structural positive for risk management discipline.
- The divergence is consistent with the **borderline regime** — dealer gamma support is present but not dominant, and the options market is beginning to re-price the week's event risk.

With **SPY RSI(14) at 73 (overbought)**, price **+7.8% above the 50-SMA** and **+11.4% above the 200-SMA**, technical mean-reversion risk is elevated. Stretched conditions plus a VIX bid into a data-heavy week is a combination that warrants defensive positioning sizing regardless of directional view.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup

The structural setup heading into Tuesday is **asymmetric and catalyst-dependent**.

**Key levels in focus:**

The **Call Wall at 7,615** is the immediate ceiling. SPX closed at 7,600 — just 15 points below that level. Any early strength that pushes into 7,615–7,638 is likely to encounter **dealer hedging resistance** in a positive gamma regime, where dealers sell into rallies to remain delta-neutral. Sustained acceptance above 7,638 (R1) would be the first signal that the tape has genuine momentum.

On the downside, **7,562 (S1) and the 7,550 Put Wall** form a double support cluster. A break below 7,550 on volume would be a regime-change warning, as it would likely push SPX decisively below the GEX flip — transitioning dealer behavior from dampening to amplifying.

### Primary Catalyst: JOLTs Job Openings (April) — 10:00 ET [HIGH IMPACT]

This is the key event for Tuesday. The labor market narrative is the fulcrum for Fed rate path expectations, and with **Fed speakers Kashkari (01:50 ET) and Hammack (08:30 ET)** framing the overnight and pre-market session, JOLTs lands into an already-sensitized rates backdrop.

- **Stronger-than-expected JOLTs:** Re-ignites "higher for longer" concern. 10Y yield likely pushes higher. Rate-sensitive sectors (Utilities, Real Estate — already today's laggards) under additional pressure. SPX likely tests the 7,562–7,550 support zone.

## 5. Strategy Implications

### Regime Context (Conditional)

The closing regime is **borderline positive gamma**, with spot within 10 points of the flip level. **Do not treat this as a firmly supportive dealer environment.** Conditional framing applies throughout.

**If the borderline positive gamma regime holds through Tuesday's session:**
- Dealers remain **long options** and will **sell into strength and buy weakness** to maintain delta neutrality.
- This behavior **dampens moves** and keeps price gravitating toward the range midpoint.
- The 7,562–7,615 range is the structural battleground. The regime supports fading outlier moves toward either boundary.
- **Income structures are the regime-appropriate expression:** consider **iron condors** or **defined-risk credit spreads** centered on the 7,562–7,615 range, using the put wall (7,550) and Call Wall (7,615) as your outer strike anchors.

**If spot breaks below 7,590 (GEX flip) and regime transitions to negative gamma:**
- Dealer behavior reverses materially — they would **buy strength and sell weakness**, amplifying moves rather than dampening them.
- In that environment, directional structures become appropriate: **debit spreads or put verticals** toward the 7,524 (S2) target.
- The regime shift trigger is a **clean, sustained break below 7,562–7,550**. A brief dip and recovery holds the positive gamma structure. A close below 7,550 does not.

### RSI / Technical Overlay

With RSI at 73 and the price extended materially above both moving averages, **upside structures should be sized conservatively**. The risk/reward of chasing calls into overbought conditions against a Call Wall at 7,615 is asymmetric to the downside. The better risk-adjusted play on the upside is **defined-risk call spreads capped at R1 (7,638)** rather than naked directional exposure.

US-Iran geopolitical noise (sanctions on Iran's crypto exchange) was absorbed without incident. None of this was volatility-generating on net; it was narrative confirmation of an already-extended rally.

## 2. Regime Validation: Positive Gamma Won, But the Forecast Didn't

The **morning gamma regime call was POSITIVE** — and that ultimately proved correct in terms of session character. Dealers long gamma hedged dynamically by selling into intraday strength and providing support on dips, compressing the range and preventing any directional resolution. This is the classic positive gamma fingerprint.

However, the **high-volatility day-type forecast (70% confidence) was contradicted.** The disconnect likely stemmed from an overweighting of the geopolitical headline risk (US-Iran) and the streak-extension narrative as potential catalysts. In practice, with no hard macro catalyst intraday and dealer positioning acting as a structural dampener, the volatility potential never found a trigger. The regime did its job. The forecast did not account sufficiently for how effectively borderline-positive gamma suppresses realized vol even against elevated headline risk.

**Critical caveat:** SPX closed at **7,609.78** with the GEX flip at **7,600.00** — a separation of just **9.8 points**. The regime is **borderline positive, not firmly positive.** A move through 7,600 on any catalyst can flip dealer behavior materially.

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Confirmation, With a Technical Asterisk

VIX closed at **15.77 (-1.75%)**, declining on a modestly positive close — standard and confirmatory. No dislocation between implied and realized vol, no skew blow-up, no hedging urgency. This is consistent with dealers absorbing flow rather than amplifying it.

That said, the macro backdrop carries a quiet technical warning that deserves attention:

## 5. Strategy Implications

### If Borderline Positive Gamma Holds (SPX > 7,600):

Dealer hedging continues to **dampen moves and support mean-reversion.** In this environment, income structures that benefit from compression are appropriate:

Dealer hedging behavior **reverses materially.** In a negative gamma environment, dealers would be **short gamma**, meaning they buy rallies and sell dips to hedge — **amplifying moves in both directions.** This is not a range-bound environment. Structures should shift to:

- **Debit Spreads / Directional Verticals:** Define your risk, pick a direction on catalyst resolution, and let momentum carry the trade. ADP or ISM Services can provide the trigger.
- **Long Puts / Put Spreads toward S1 (7,572) or S2 (7,534):** If the flip confirms, dealer selling pressure accelerates moves lower, and S2 at 7,534 becomes a realistic same-day target.

Any long-gamma income structures initiated tomorrow should carry **Thursday expiry or earlier** to avoid NFP vol exposure unless explicitly sized for it. Do not carry undefined-risk short vol through Friday's print.

Tomorrow's catalyst cluster hits a market that is technically stretched, narrowly above a regime flip, and priced for continued calm. Respect the levels. The 7,600 flip is the number that matters most.

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap

## 1. SESSION FLOW — What Actually Drove Today

Today's session did **not** play out as anticipated. The morning called for a **Mixed/Chop** day underpinned by a **positive gamma** environment — instead, the market delivered a **moderate trend lower**, with SPX finishing at **7,553.68 (-0.74%)** on a session range of just **0.72%** (High: 7,605.35 / Low: 7,551.22).

The primary drivers were **macro and geopolitical**, not the AI/semis narrative that had been in focus earlier in the week. Headlines linking an **oil surge and Iran tensions** hit risk sentiment hard, with the Dow reportedly dropping ~620 points intraday at peak pressure. Critically, the **sector rotation confirmed this macro shift**: **Healthcare (+1.8%) and Energy (+1.5%)** led — a textbook geopolitical/risk-off rotation into defensives and commodity-linked names — while **Technology (-1.5%) and Utilities (-1.4%)** lagged. The 3.2% sector dispersion is notable: this was not a broad-market de-risking event but a **sharp rotational repricing** away from the growth/AI trade that had dominated recent sessions.

The nine-day S&P 500 winning streak ending today adds weight to the narrative that this was a momentum exhaustion event catalyzed by a macro shock, rather than a random mean-reversion day.

---

## 2. REGIME VALIDATION — The Morning Call Broke Down

This was a **clear regime contradiction**. The morning forecast called for **Positive Gamma / Mixed-Chop** at 55% confidence — a low-conviction call to begin with. What we got instead was a **trending, directional session to the downside**, and by the close, the gamma regime had **flipped to NEGATIVE**.

The sequence matters here: early session likely saw the positive gamma framework hold (compressed range, dealer hedging dampening initial moves), but the geopolitical catalyst — Iran tensions and oil shock — was sufficient to overwhelm dealer stabilization flows and push the market through levels where the regime transitioned. Once negative gamma took hold intraday, **dealer hedging behavior reversed**: rather than selling into weakness to stabilize, dealers were forced to **sell additional exposure as SPX declined**, amplifying the move toward the session lows near **7,551**. The intraday trend character and the closing regime of **negative gamma** are entirely consistent with this mechanical dynamic.

Low-conviction morning calls (55%) deserve wider contingency framing — this session is a reminder that in transitional gamma environments, the catalyst load governs.

---

## 3. VIX/GAMMA INTERPLAY — Vol Confirmation

VIX closed at **16.06 (+1.84%)**, directionally confirming the SPX decline and consistent with a **negative gamma environment**. The VIX move was measured rather than explosive — a 1.84% rise on a -0.74% SPX session suggests the market is pricing incremental risk, not panic. That said, **16 on VIX is no longer the complacent sub-15 environment** that has characterized recent weeks.

The negative gamma closing regime is the key variable to carry into tomorrow. With dealers now **net short options**, their hedging posture has flipped: on further weakness, they must **sell into declines**, and on any bounce, they must **buy into strength**. Both dynamics act as **amplifiers**, not stabilizers. The range-bound comfort of the prior positive gamma regime is gone — at least until proven otherwise.

The rates backdrop provides limited offset: the **10Y yield at 4.46% (-1bp)** barely moved, and the normal 2Y-10Y spread of +41bp offers no fresh signal. Rates are not the story today. The **Iran/geopolitical premium** in oil is the vol driver to watch.

**Regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA | Risk: Moderate-to-Elevated**

In a negative gamma environment, **dealers are short options**. Their hedging requires them to **buy into rallies and sell into declines** — flows that **amplify directional moves** in both directions. This is not an environment for income structures that require range-bound behavior.

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Thursday, June 04, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## 1. SESSION FLOW: WHAT DROVE TODAY'S TAPE

Today's move was predominantly **flow- and technically-driven** rather than a clean macro or earnings catalyst. SPX closed at **7,584.31 (+0.41%)** — a new record high and reportedly the ninth consecutive week of gains — but the session's character was more grind than gap. The 1.09% intraday range (High: **7,598.19**, Low: **7,516.54**) tells the real story: the market probed both directions before settling modestly higher, consistent with positioning and options flow mechanics rather than a decisive macro impulse.

Sector rotation was notably **rotational, not broad-based**. Technology led at **+1.8%** and Industrials at **+1.5%**, while Consumer Cyclical (-0.5%) and Consumer Defensive (-1.5%) lagged. The 3.3% spread between leaders and laggards confirms money moving *within* equities rather than a risk-on/risk-off tide lifting or sinking all sectors uniformly. The defensives underperformance is structurally notable — it reinforces a market that continues to shed its defensive allocation in favor of growth and cyclical exposure, consistent with the AI/agentic narrative dominating recent headlines.

Rates provided no headwind: the 10Y yield closed at **4.47% (-2bp)** on a normally-shaped curve (+42bp 2s/10s), offering equities a supportive backdrop heading into tomorrow's macro event.

---

## 2. REGIME VALIDATION: MORNING CALL vs. ACTUAL

This is where today's session requires honest post-mortem. **The morning call was not validated.**

- **Morning regime:** NEGATIVE GAMMA, Trend Down at 75% confidence
- **Actual session type:** MIXED
- **Closing regime:** POSITIVE GAMMA

The expected trending, amplified downside did not materialize. Instead of dealers chasing moves lower — the behavior expected in a negative gamma regime — the session traded in a contained range that ultimately resolved higher. Several factors likely explain the regime contradiction:

1. **Gamma regime migration intraday.** SPX began the session near levels where the gamma environment was borderline. As the tape held and drifted higher toward **7,598**, the market appears to have exited the negative gamma zone and crossed into positive gamma territory during the session. Dealer hedging behavior would have shifted accordingly — from amplifying to dampening — contributing to the compressed range and mean-reversion character observed in the afternoon.

2. **The absence of a macro catalyst.** A negative gamma regime requires a directional impulse to express itself; without a news shock or forced repositioning, the underlying flow wasn't there to trigger the trending behavior the model anticipated.

3. **Residual options positioning.** With Friday NFP looming, participants appear to have been reluctant to press directional bets aggressively into a major data event, muting the kind of momentum that validates a negative gamma trend day.

**Net assessment:** The morning regime was technically correct at the open, but the session migrated into positive gamma as SPX lifted, and the forecast did not account for a rapid regime transition at these compressed levels.

---

## 3. VIX / GAMMA INTERPLAY

VIX closed at **15.4 (-4.11%)**, behaving in textbook confirmation of the SPX advance. The implied vol compression is meaningful context: a VIX below 16 on a record SPX close signals the market is not reaching for protection. Participants are not hedging tails — they are either complacent or, more charitably, positioned long gamma themselves through calendar structures heading into tomorrow's payrolls print.

The RSI at **64** and price sitting **+6.7% above the 50-SMA** and **+10.9% above the 200-SMA** means technicals are stretched. That does not preclude further extension, but it does mean the margin for error on a disappointment is asymmetric — mean-reversion snapbacks from these levels can be swift.

The closing positive gamma regime is consistent with today's muted intraday swing. In a positive gamma environment, dealers are **long options and hedge by selling into strength and buying dips**, acting as a natural shock absorber. The 82-point session range, tight by recent standards, fits this dynamic cleanly.

---

## 4. TOMORROW'S SETUP: NFP FRIDAY IN A BORDERLINE REGIME

**This is the critical caveat for overnight positioning:** SPX closed at **7,584.31**, only **15.7 points below the GEX flip level at 7,600.** The regime is positive gamma — but only barely. Do **not** treat this as a firmly anchored positive gamma environment. It is borderline and highly susceptible to flipping on tomorrow's open depending on the NFP print.

**Key levels for tomorrow:**
| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,600 | GEX flip level — regime hinge |
| R1 | 7,622 | First resistance above flip |
| R2 | 7,660 | Extension target if NFP hot |
| Put Wall | 7,550 | Dealer support zone |
| S1 | 7,546 | First technical support |
| S2 | 7,508 | Breakdown zone |
| Expected Move | ±74pts | Implies ~7,510 – 7,658 range |

**The 7,600 level is the session's central pivot.** Here is why it matters structurally: on a **strong NFP print**, SPX is likely to gap toward or through 7,600. If the market clears and holds that level, the regime flips negative — dealers would then be **short gamma and hedge by buying the rally**, amplifying the move toward R1 (7,622) and potentially R2 (7,660). That is a very different tape character than today.

On a **weak or disappointing NFP print**, expect the Put Wall at **7,550** to become the battleground. The positive gamma regime (if it holds below 7,600) would see dealers provide natural support there — buying the dip mechanically. A failure of 7,550 on heavy volume would open S1 at **7,546** and S2 at **7,508**, the latter aligning closely with the lower bound of the expected move.

**Tomorrow's macro calendar is heavily loaded:**
- **Non-Farm Payrolls (May) — 08:30 ET [HIGH IMPACT]**
- **Unemployment Rate (May) — 08:30 ET [HIGH IMPACT]**
- **NFP Private (May) — 08:30 ET [HIGH IMPACT]**
- Government & Manufacturing Payrolls also at 08:30 ET [low impact]

The 08:30 cluster means the first 15–30 minutes of the session will be critical for regime determination. Pre-market positioning is the key risk window.

Additionally, the **weekly risk outlook flags CPI (May) and Inflation Rate MoM (May) later this week** as further elevated-risk events. Position sizing should reflect a multi-event risk environment, not just a single NFP trade.

---

## 5. STRATEGY IMPLICATIONS

**The regime is borderline positive gamma — and that ambiguity is itself the strategy input.**

**Scenario A — SPX holds below 7,600 post-NFP (positive gamma preserved):**
The dealer hedging dynamic continues to dampen moves. Mean-reversion and range compression favor **income structures** — iron condors anchored around the 7,550/7,600 range, or **credit spreads** positioned with the Put Wall (7,550) as the short strike floor. The expected move of ±74 points sets the outer boundary; premium selling within that range has a logical mechanical underpinning.

**Scenario B — SPX breaks and sustains above 7,600 (regime flips negative gamma):**
Dealers flip to **short gamma, hedging by buying the rally** — momentum amplifies. In this scenario, income structures face an adverse environment. **Defined-risk directional structures** — call debit spreads targeting R1 (7,622) and R2 (7,660) — become appropriate. The momentum feed-forward from dealer re-hedging could drive a fast, sustained extension. Do not fade that break with short premium.

**Scenario C — Weak NFP drives SPX toward 7,550 and below:**
If the Put Wall breaks, dealers in positive gamma territory will initially absorb — but a sustained move through 7,546 (S1) shifts the calculus. Consider **put debit spreads** using 7,550/7,508 as strike wings if the tape confirms downside follow-through in the first 30 minutes post-print.

**Risk Management Overlay:**
- Avoid naked short vol into NFP. The event risk is real and the borderline regime means no mechanical stabilizer is guaranteed.
- Size down to account for the multi-event week ahead (CPI follows).
- The 08:30 print is a binary event in a borderline regime — wait for the first 15-minute candle to resolve before initiating delta exposure.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today was a regime migration session — negative gamma in the morning, positive by the close — that produced a contained, technically-driven grind to new highs. Tomorrow's NFP print lands 15 points below the regime flip, making the 7,600 Call Wall the most important level on the board. Manage size, respect the flip, and let the data resolve the regime before committing directional risk.

*Good luck tomorrow. Stay disciplined.*

---
*This recap is for institutional use only and does not constitute investment advice.*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Friday, June 6, 2026 | SPX: 7,383.74 | VIX: 21.51

---

## 1. Session Flow — What Drove Today

Today's tape was macro-authored, not a gamma or technical event. The **May nonfarm payrolls print of 172,000** — nearly double the 88,000 consensus — detonated a rate-shock repricing across equity markets. The 10-year yield surged **+8 basis points to 4.55%**, and the rate-sensitive, duration-heavy corners of the market bore the brunt: **Technology fell 4.1%**, dragging broad indices with it, while a chip-specific selloff reportedly erased **over $1 trillion in market capitalization** across the semiconductor complex.

The session's character was not a broad risk-off flush, however — it was **rotational and selective**. Financial Services gained **+1.0%** and Consumer Defensive added **+0.6%**, consistent with a "higher-for-longer" rate repricing rather than outright panic. The 5.1% sector dispersion between the leaders and laggards reinforces this: money moved, it didn't simply leave. The defensive bid absorbed flows that exited technology, a pattern characteristic of institutional repositioning rather than retail fear.

---

## 2. Regime Validation — The Morning Call vs. Reality

This is where the day demands accountability. The morning regime was called **POSITIVE GAMMA** with a **HIGH VOLATILITY** day prediction at 70% confidence. The actual session delivered a **strong TREND day**, with SPX printing a **2.35% intraday range** and closing near session lows at **-2.65%**. The closing gamma regime flipped to **NEGATIVE**.

The regime call was contradicted, and the mechanism is clear: the payrolls shock was large enough in magnitude to overwhelm whatever dampening effect positive gamma positioning may have provided early in the session. In positive gamma environments, dealer hedging behavior — selling into strength and buying into weakness — creates a natural mean-reversion force. That force was present, but the macro impulse from the jobs data generated selling pressure that exceeded the gamma cushion. As the market moved lower, strikes were breached, gamma flipped, and by the close dealers had transitioned to a **short gamma posture** — buying as prices fell, amplifying rather than dampening the move into the close. The regime did not hold. Note the distinction: the morning call was reasonable given the positioning data available at open, but macro surprises of this magnitude are precisely the scenario where gamma-based forecasts face their highest override risk.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay

VIX confirmed the narrative with authority. A **+39.68% single-session spike to 21.51** is not noise — it is a volatility regime signal. The SPX down / VIX sharply up pairing is the textbook rate-shock response, and the magnitude of the VIX move tells us that the options market repriced tail risk materially, not just near-term directional uncertainty.

The gamma regime transition amplified this dynamic. As negative gamma took hold into the afternoon, dealer hedging shifted character: rather than providing stabilizing counter-flows, dealers were forced to **sell into declining prices** to maintain delta neutrality — a pro-cyclical feedback loop that accelerated the move toward the session low of **7,368.63**. The VIX spike is both a consequence of that dealer behavior and a forward signal: at 21.51, implied volatility is now pricing a meaningfully more uncertain environment heading into next week.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup — Levels and Overnight Framework

**Closing regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA. No GEX flip level exists — rely entirely on structural walls and technical pivots.**

This is a critical framing point for the weekend and Monday morning. With the market in **absolute negative gamma** and no identifiable flip level, there is no natural dealer-imposed gravitational center to anchor price action. Moves can extend in either direction without the mean-reversion dampening that positive gamma provides.

**The structural landscape for tomorrow:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|-------|-------|--------------|
| R2 | 7,458 | Upside extension target |
| R1 | 7,421 | First overhead resistance |
| **Call Wall** | **7,400** | Dealer short-call concentration; supply above |
| **Put Wall** | **7,380** | Dealer short-put concentration; support below |
| SPX Close | 7,383.74 | Current price — sandwiched in the wall zone |
| S1 | 7,347 | First meaningful support |
| S2 | 7,310 | Deeper support; negative gamma acceleration risk |
| Expected Move | ±100 pts | ~7,283 to 7,483 range |

The most important observation here: **SPX closed at 7,383.74 — directly between the Put Wall (7,380) and the Call Wall (7,400).** This is a compressed 20-point zone. In a negative gamma environment, this proximity is not stabilizing — it is unstable. A break below 7,380 removes Put Wall support and, with dealers short gamma, their hedging flows would add **selling pressure** as price declines, potentially accelerating toward S1 at 7,347 and S2 at 7,310. Conversely, a reclaim above 7,400 could see dealer hedging **add buying pressure** into a recovery, with R1 at 7,421 as the first target.

**Tomorrow's sole catalyst is Fed Governor Barr speaking at 12:00 ET (medium impact).** Given today's payrolls shock, any commentary that validates a "higher-for-longer" interpretation — or more pointedly, signals comfort with fewer cuts — has the potential to be an additional headwind. The market is already digesting the rate repricing; Barr's tone matters at the margin but is unlikely to be the primary driver unless he explicitly pushes against current pricing.

**The light calendar tomorrow limits event risk, but the weekly outlook is elevated.** Next week brings **Core CPI and CPI YoY (May)** — the next critical data point in the post-payrolls narrative. Given today's labor data, a hot inflation read would complete the "higher-for-longer" thesis and likely test negative gamma acceleration below current supports. Traders should be sizing accordingly heading into the weekend.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

**Regime: Negative Gamma. Environment: Post-shock, elevated VIX (21.51), no floor from GEX.**

In a **negative gamma** regime, dealer hedging is **pro-directional** — they buy into rallies and sell into declines, amplifying moves. This is not a credit-spread or income-structure environment. Selling premium with VIX at 21.51 and no gamma stabilization is structurally disadvantaged: the premium collected does not compensate for the tail exposure when dealer flows add fuel to directional breaks.

**Favored structures for the current environment:**

- **Defined-risk directional (debit spreads / verticals):** With negative gamma amplifying moves and VIX elevated, directional structures with defined risk capture the potential for extended trends without unlimited exposure. Given the downside bias from rates and the proximity to Put Wall support, **bear put spreads targeting S1 (7,347) / S2 (7,310)** on a break below 7,380 offer a defined-risk way to position with the regime.

- **Long vol / long gamma positioning (straddles / strangles — sized carefully):** VIX at 21.51 is elevated but not extreme by historical standards. If you believe CPI next week delivers another surprise, owning volatility over the weekend has a credible catalyst-based rationale. The ±100 expected move for tomorrow is the near-term anchor; the weekly CPI event is the larger vol opportunity.

- **Avoid naked short premium and iron condors until the regime clarifies.** The absence of a GEX flip level means there is no identifiable point at which positive gamma would reassert. Writing condors in a regime where dealer flows amplify breakouts is a structurally poor risk/reward proposition.

**Risk management note for the week ahead:** The RSI at 45 is neutral and not technically oversold despite today's decline. Price remains **+8.0% above the 200-SMA**, which provides a longer-term technical cushion but also means the market has room to correct further before reaching structural support. Do not mistake the lack of technical oversold conditions for a floor — in negative gamma, price can move to levels that create the oversold condition, not wait for one.

---

**Bottom line:** A macro-driven regime flip on a jobs shock. The morning positive gamma call was overwhelmed by the data surprise — a reminder that gamma provides a dampening force, not an absolute ceiling on volatility when macro impulse is strong enough. Heading into the weekend, SPX sits in a narrow and unstable zone between two structural walls in a negative gamma environment. Position sizing, not conviction, should be the dominant variable until the CPI data next week resolves the rate narrative.

*Manage size. Know your levels. Next week's inflation data is the session that matters.*

---
*This recap is prepared for professional market participants and does not constitute investment advice. All figures sourced from closing market data as of June 6, 2026.*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Monday, June 8, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## 1. Session Flow: Mixed Character Defies Downside Forecast

Today's session delivered a **MIXED outcome**, diverging from the morning's 75%-confidence **TREND DOWN** forecast. SPX closed at **7,405.73 (+0.30%)**, carving a relatively contained **0.97% session range** (High: 7,466.81 / Low: 7,395.13) — neither a clean trending day in either direction nor a textbook range-bound grind.

The primary driver appears to have been **macro/geopolitical crosscurrents colliding with a technology-led momentum bid**. Headlines pointing to fresh geopolitical tensions — notably Israeli strikes on Beirut and Iranian retaliatory missile fire — provided early risk-off pressure consistent with the downside bias, pushing the index to session lows near **7,395**. However, that selling was absorbed and reversed by a **chip/AI complex rebound**, with semiconductor names leading a broad tech recovery through the afternoon session. Ceasefire hopes provided incremental relief as the day progressed.

The net result: a session that opened under pressure, tested lower support, found buyers in growth/tech, and closed marginally positive — classic **indecision** rather than trend conviction in either direction.

**Sector rotation confirms this read:** Consumer Cyclical and Consumer Defensive each added **+0.4%**, suggesting a defensive/consumption tilt to institutional positioning rather than aggressive risk-on rotation. The laggards — **Real Estate (-1.4%)** and **Utilities (-1.7%)** — reflect rate sensitivity weighing on yield proxies, consistent with the **10Y yield holding at 4.56% (+1bp)**. This sector leadership pattern does *not* support a clean bull narrative; it reads more as selective repositioning than broad-based accumulation.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Negative Gamma — Framework Partially Confirmed, But Incomplete

The morning called **NEGATIVE GAMMA** with a TREND DOWN bias. The regime classification was partially validated, partially contradicted:

**What confirmed the regime:** The session *did* exhibit negative-gamma-consistent behavior in pockets. The early move to **7,395** (within 20 handles of the Put Wall at 7,375) showed directional momentum rather than mean-reversion, and the intraday swings had amplified, non-sticky characteristics — dealers short options were leaning into moves rather than dampening them.

**What contradicted the trend call:** The directional vector was wrong. Rather than a sustained downside trend, the market reversed off lows and spent the bulk of the afternoon in a compressed band between roughly **7,400–7,450**. In a negative gamma environment, the absence of a clean trend in *either* direction suggests that neither directional catalyst was strong enough to trigger a self-reinforcing dealer hedging cascade today. Geopolitical uncertainty created noise; chip sector strength created a counter-bid. The two forces essentially neutralized each other's momentum potential.

**Bottom line on regime validation:** Negative gamma *character* (amplification potential, episodic sharp moves) was present. The *directionality* call missed. The regime itself was not invalidated — it simply lacked a sufficiently dominant catalyst to drive one-sided flow.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: VIX Provides the Clearest Signal of the Day

VIX's **-12.05% collapse to 18.92** is arguably the most important data point of this session. A **12-handle decline in implied volatility on a day when SPX gained only 0.30%** is a significant divergence — and it is directionally informative.

Several interpretations deserve attention:

- **Short-volatility supply returned aggressively.** Participants were willing to sell premium into a geopolitical headline environment, suggesting the market viewed today's risk events as transitory rather than systemic escalation risks.
- **The VIX decline is consistent with the chip rebound narrative** — if the primary uncertainty this year has been AI/semiconductor disruption, and that cohort rallied sharply, options market participants may have priced out a layer of tail risk specific to that sector.
- **However, this VIX move in a negative gamma regime warrants caution.** When dealers are short gamma and VIX compresses rapidly, the resulting increase in delta sensitivity sets up the potential for sharper moves on the next catalyst. Lower VIX in a negative gamma regime is not stabilization — it is compression ahead of potential release.

The **2Y-10Y spread holding at +41bp** on a normal curve, combined with a stable but elevated 10Y at 4.56%, reinforces that rates are not the immediate volatility source — but inflation headlines expected later this week represent a known risk that the options market may be underpricing at 18.92.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Contained But Not Calm — Know Your Walls

**Closing gamma regime: NEGATIVE** — no GEX flip level exists; the market sits in an environment of absolute negative gamma. All meaningful structure reference must derive from **Put/Call Walls and Technical Pivots** rather than a flip point.

**The key architecture for Tuesday:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| R2 | 7,480 | Extended upside — above expected move |
| R1 | 7,443 | First meaningful resistance; just below Call Wall |
| **Call Wall** | **7,450** | Dealer short-call concentration; upside speed bump |
| SPX Close | 7,405.73 | Anchor |
| **Put Wall** | **7,375** | Dealer short-put concentration; critical downside support |
| S1 | 7,369 | Structural support below Put Wall |
| S2 | 7,332 | Meaningful breakdown zone |

**Expected Move:** ±88 points, defining a Tuesday range of approximately **7,318–7,494**.

**The overnight and open setup:** With SPX closing at 7,405, the market sits essentially mid-range between the Put Wall (7,375) and the Call Wall (7,450) — a **75-handle corridor**. In a negative gamma regime, this is not a comfortable equilibrium; it is an unstable one. Any directional catalyst has the potential to drive the market toward one of these walls with self-reinforcing dealer hedging flows behind it.

**Catalyst load for Tuesday is moderate:**
- **06:00 ET — NFIB Business Optimism (May):** Small business sentiment has been politically and tariff-sensitive this cycle. A miss here could reinforce stagflation concerns ahead of this week's CPI.
- **08:30 ET — Trade Balance, Imports, Exports (Apr):** Tariff distortions have made this print volatile and difficult to model. A widening deficit could reignite trade-policy concerns; a narrowing could be interpreted as demand destruction. Either way, volatility potential at the open is non-trivial.
- **10:00 ET — Existing Home Sales MoM (May):** Low impact on its own, but read in context of rate levels, this confirms or denies the housing market's resilience narrative.

**Directional bias:** No strong directional lean — but the asymmetry worth monitoring is to the **downside**. The Put Wall at 7,375 sits just 31 handles below Friday's close, and a negative trade print at 08:30 combined with residual geopolitical anxiety could test that level quickly. A break *below* 7,375 in a negative gamma regime would trigger dealer selling pressure that could accelerate toward S1 (7,369) and potentially S2 (7,332) within the session.

**Week risk note:** **CPI (YoY, May)** and **Inflation Rate MoM (May)** are on the calendar this week. With VIX compressing to 18.92 and negative gamma in place, any hot inflation print has the potential to be a volatility shock. Position sizing should reflect this elevated weekly risk, not just tomorrow's moderate-impact slate.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Respect the Regime — Directional Structures, Defined Risk

**Regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA — Dealer Behavior Context:**

In the current negative gamma environment, dealers are **short options** and must hedge by **buying into rallies and selling into declines** to maintain delta neutrality. This behavior *amplifies* directional moves rather than dampening them. Traders should expect that momentum begets momentum — breakouts above the Call Wall at 7,450 or breakdowns below the Put Wall at 7,375 are more likely to *accelerate* than to reverse immediately.

**This is explicitly not a range-trade/income environment.** Iron condors and credit spreads are structurally disadvantaged when dealers are adding fuel to directional moves. Selling premium into a negative gamma regime with elevated week-level risk (CPI incoming) is a low-reward, high-risk proposition.

**Structures that align with the regime:**

- **Defined-risk directional spreads (debit verticals):** If the trade data at 08:30 or NFIB at 06:00 provides a directional catalyst, debit spreads targeting a move toward 7,450 (bullish) or 7,332 (bearish) align with the negative gamma amplification dynamic. Defined risk is essential given binary catalyst uncertainty.
- **Downside put verticals targeting 7,375–7,332:** The Put Wall at 7,375 is the first line; a breach opens the door to S2 at 7,332 — a 43-handle move that fits neatly within the ±88 expected move. A put spread structured around this range captures the negative gamma downside acceleration scenario.
- **Avoid undefined-risk short premium:** Short strangles, naked puts, and similar structures carry material risk in this regime. A negative CPI surprise this week in a negative gamma environment could produce a move that rapidly exceeds premium collected.
- **Sizing discipline:** Keep positions sized for the weekly risk window, not just Tuesday. VIX at 18.92 may feel "calm," but it is still elevated relative to the 13–15 range that would represent genuine complacency. Respect the compression; do not extrapolate it.

---

**Summary:** Today's session was a geopolitical-versus-tech-momentum draw that left the negative gamma regime intact but directionality unresolved. Tomorrow's trade data and NFIB print represent the next opportunity for the regime to assert itself. The Put Wall at 7,375 and Call Wall at 7,450 are your bookends. In a negative gamma environment, those walls are magnets when approached and accelerants when breached — trade accordingly.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy | Evening Recap Desk*

# Evening Derivatives Recap
### Tuesday, June 10, 2026 — Prepared for Distribution After Close

---

## 1. Session Flow: What Actually Drove Today

Today's session delivered a decisive **contradiction of the morning's TREND UP call** — and the driver sits squarely at the intersection of sector rotation and macro positioning ahead of tomorrow's CPI print.

The tape told a clear story: **Tech and Energy sold off hard** (Technology -2.1%, Energy -2.0%) while capital rotated defensively and opportunistically into **Real Estate (+0.9%) and Basic Materials (+0.8%)**. The session range of **3.39%** — spanning from a low of **7,237.85 to a high of 7,483.15** — is wide by any standard, but the SPX net close of **7,386.65 (-0.26%)** obscures the violence underneath. This was not a low-volatility drift. It was a high-amplitude chop session driven by rotation out of the growth complex.

The proximity to tomorrow's CPI data package is the most logical explanation for the rotational character. Traders unwound momentum longs in megacap Tech and repositioned into rate-sensitive real estate and materials — a classic "de-risking into an inflation event" posture. The 10-year yield easing 3bp to **4.53%** helped the real estate trade but didn't rescue tech, suggesting the selling there was positioning-driven, not purely rates-driven.

The 2Y-10Y spread holding at **+40bp** (normal curve) tells us the bond market is not pricing a hard landing — but at 4.53% on the 10Y, there's enough rate sensitivity to justify caution into a live CPI print.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Forecast vs. Reality

**The morning call was: TREND UP, 75% confidence, Negative Gamma regime.**

**What happened: Range-bound, with a net -0.26% close inside a violent 3.39% intraday range.**

The regime call was **technically validated on mechanics but wrong on direction.** Let me unpack that carefully.

Negative gamma environments *should* produce amplified, trending moves — dealers are short options and must buy rallies and sell dips to hedge, which accelerates directional moves. The 3.39% intraday range confirms that amplification was present. The mechanics worked as advertised. What failed was the directional call. Sector rotation created an internally offsetting dynamic: heavy Tech and Energy selling suppressed index-level momentum even as individual sector moves were substantial. The rotation was **wide but self-canceling at the index level**, producing a near-flat SPX close inside an enormous range.

The takeaway: **Negative gamma amplified the intraday moves**. The TREND UP thesis failed not because gamma mechanics were wrong, but because the rotational structure of the selling neutralized the index-level trend. With CPI looming, directional conviction was absent at the aggregate level — traders were moving within the market, not out of it.

---

## 3. VIX / Gamma Interplay

VIX closing at **19.87 (+5.03%)** on a session where SPX ended roughly flat is a notable dislocation. Ordinarily, a flat-to-down 0.26% SPX day would produce a modest VIX uptick — but a +5% VIX move on a near-unchanged close signals something more structural: **the market is buying protection ahead of tomorrow's CPI, not reacting to today's realized move.**

This is a textbook pre-event vol expansion. Dealers who are short options in a **negative gamma regime** are not in a position to dampen vol — they are pro-cyclical. As CPI protection demand rises, implied vol expands further, and the negative gamma dynamic deepens. The closing regime remaining **uniformly negative** (with no GEX flip level anywhere in the observable chain) confirms this: there is no gamma support structure anywhere in the current strike landscape. Dealers remain short gamma across the board and will continue to amplify moves in either direction tomorrow.

The 3.39% intraday range today, combined with a +5% VIX expansion into the close, is the market telling you clearly: **tomorrow will not be a quiet session.**

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: CPI Day in a Negative Gamma Vacuum

### The Structural Setup

The closing gamma regime is **uniformly negative with no flip level anywhere in the chain.** This is the most unambiguous negative gamma configuration possible — there is no level at which dealer behavior shifts from amplifying to dampening. Every point of movement tomorrow, in either direction, will be met with **dealer flows that accelerate the move, not absorb it.**

Tomorrow's **expected move is ±92 points** around the 7,386.65 close. That puts the probabilistic range at approximately **7,295 to 7,479.**

### Key Levels That Matter

| Level | Type | Why It Matters |
|---|---|---|
| **7,525** | Call Wall | Upper structural ceiling; dealer short-call exposure concentrates here |
| **7,461** | R2 | Second resistance; upper bound of expected move sits near here |
| **7,424** | R1 | First resistance; reclaiming this level post-CPI would signal bullish tone |
| **7,355** | Put Wall | Critical floor; this is where put protection is heaviest — a break here is structurally significant |
| **7,350** | S1 | First support; aligns tightly with Put Wall — a convergence zone |
| **7,313** | S2 | Second support; breach would confirm downside momentum |

The **convergence of the Put Wall at 7,355 and S1 at 7,350** is the most important level cluster on the board for tomorrow. In a negative gamma environment, if CPI prints hot and SPX breaks below 7,350, **dealers will sell into that move** — not buy it. The Put Wall provides no mechanical stabilization in this regime the way it would in a positive gamma environment. A break of 7,350/7,355 opens the door toward 7,313 and potentially beyond, with no structural dealer support to slow the decline.

To the upside, **7,424 (R1) is the first meaningful test.** A cooler-than-expected CPI print could ignite a fast move through R1 toward R2 at 7,461. Given the ±92 expected move, a full push to the Call Wall at 7,525 would represent an *above-expected* outcome and would require a materially dovish surprise.

### CPI Catalyst Framing

Tomorrow's 08:30 ET data drop is the week's highest-impact event. The market is pricing elevated uncertainty (VIX 19.87, +5% today). The key metrics to watch:

- **Core CPI MoM (May):** Consensus will be the immediate reaction driver. Any print above +0.3% MoM risks a hawkish re-rating.
- **CPI YoY:** The year-over-year headline will dominate media framing and retail flow response.
- **Core CPI YoY:** The Fed's preferred inflation lens — this drives rate expectations and therefore the duration trade.

A **hot print** in a negative gamma vacuum = dealers selling into SPX weakness, rates spiking, and tech/growth under renewed pressure. The 7,350/7,355 zone becomes the immediate line of defense.

A **cool print** triggers the mirror image — dealers buying into SPX strength, rate-sensitive sectors (real estate, utilities) catching a bid, and potentially a rapid re-test of R1/R2.

There is no "moderate" gamma cushion here. The negative regime guarantees that **whatever direction the first 30-minute post-CPI candle commits to, it will likely extend.**

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

**Regime: Uniformly Negative Gamma | Catalyst: High-Impact CPI (08:30 ET)**

In a **negative gamma environment**, dealers are short options and hedge by buying rallies and selling dips — this amplifies directional moves and makes mean-reversion strategies hazardous. Do not structure for range-bound outcomes tomorrow. The setup favors **defined-risk directional structures** that profit from a sustained move in one direction.

### Structures to Consider

**Pre-CPI (Tonight/Pre-Market):**
- Defined-risk directional exposures are appropriate if you have a macro view on the CPI print. Debit spreads (call or put) allow you to participate in a large post-CPI move while capping the vol-crush risk that will follow the 08:30 release.
- **Avoid selling premium naked or in wide structures going into 08:30.** The +5% VIX expansion today inflated option prices — selling now seems attractive, but the negative gamma regime means a large directional move tomorrow could overwhelm the premium collected.

**Post-CPI (Reaction Play):**
- **If SPX gaps above 7,424 (R1) on a cool print:** Bullish call debit spread targeting R2 (7,461) or the Call Wall (7,525) as upper strike. The negative gamma environment will help the trend extend. Structure with a next-day or same-day expiry to minimize theta risk.
- **If SPX breaks below 7,350/7,355 (S1/Put Wall) on a hot print:** Bearish put debit spread targeting S2 (7,313). Again, negative gamma works in your favor — dealer hedging will accelerate the downside, not absorb it.
- **In either case, do not fade the initial post-CPI move.** Negative gamma regimes punish counter-trend positioning on catalyst days. The dealer community is not positioned to buy dips or sell rallies — they will pile on.

**Iron Condors / Credit Spreads:** Not appropriate for tomorrow. The expected move of ±92 points combined with a uniformly negative gamma regime and a binary macro catalyst makes range-bound income structures high-risk. The session today — 3.39% range, net flat — previews what CPI day can look like without even having a print. Avoid.

---

## Bottom Line for Tomorrow

SPX closed at **7,386.65**, sitting between S1 (7,350) and R1 (7,424) in a **uniformly negative gamma regime** with no structural support from dealer flows anywhere in the chain. VIX at 19.87 and rising signals the market is already pricing a volatile open. CPI hits at 08:30 ET across five simultaneous readings — the most information-dense macro moment of the week.

**The setup is binary and the gamma regime amplifies both outcomes.** The 7,350/7,355 cluster is the line in the sand to the downside; 7,424 is the first real test to the upside. Structure accordingly, size for volatility, and respect the regime.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy*
