# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Wednesday, June 10, 2026 | After the Close

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro-Led Risk-Off, Dealer Flows Amplified the Move

Today's tape was unmistakably macro-driven. The session opened under pressure as geopolitical risk — centered on renewed Iran/war fears and rising oil prices — collided with a CPI print that reinforced sticky inflation concerns. The dual macro shock set the tone early, and the market never mounted a credible recovery. SPX closed at **7,266.99**, down **1.62%** on the session, trading within a **1.80% range** from a high of **7,396.56** to a low of **7,265.93**.

Sector rotation confirmed the risk-off read: **Consumer Defensives (+0.7%) and Financial Services (+0.2%)** led the tape while **Industrials (-2.7%) and Utilities (-2.1%)** lagged badly. Sector dispersion of **3.3%** is notable — this was not a uniform index flush. Rather, capital rotated defensively while cyclicals and rate-sensitive names bore the brunt. The breadth story aligns: the selling was broad across the index, not concentrated in a handful of mega-cap names.

*A note on the news feed:* Some external sources cited a much more modest SPX decline (~0.37%). We are treating the **market data as the authoritative source** — SPX closed -1.62%, and the session character (strong trend, VIX +11.83%) is fully consistent with a materially larger move than those reports suggested.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Morning Call Confirmed — Negative Gamma Delivered

The morning regime call of **NEGATIVE GAMMA** with a **HIGH VOLATILITY** day type (70% confidence) was validated in full. The session printed as a **strong trend day**, with price grinding lower throughout the session without meaningful mean-reversion bounces. This is textbook negative gamma behavior.

In a negative gamma environment, dealers are **short options** and must hedge dynamically by **buying rallies and selling dips** — which acts as a **momentum accelerant**, not a dampener. That is precisely what the session delivered: the intraday reversals that did occur were shallow and quickly failed, consistent with dealer hedging activity reinforcing directional pressure rather than absorbing it.

No regime flip occurred during the session. The closing gamma regime remains **uniformly negative**, with no observable GEX flip level across the entire option chain. There is no structural support from dealer positioning — the environment remains one of amplified, momentum-driven price action.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Full Confirmation

VIX closed at **22.22**, up **+11.83%** on the session. The magnitude of the VIX expansion is consistent with — and confirmatory of — the negative gamma regime. When dealers are short gamma, realized volatility spikes alongside implied volatility as hedging flows chase the move rather than offset it. The 22-handle on VIX represents a meaningful elevation from recent complacency levels and signals that the options market is pricing a sustained uncertainty premium, not a one-day anomaly.

The 10Y yield edging up **+2bp to 4.55%** — with the curve maintaining a normal shape at **+42bp (2Y-10Y)** — does not indicate a rates-driven panic, but it does reinforce the inflationary macro narrative that pressured risk assets. Rates were not the primary accelerant today; geopolitics and inflation repricing were. However, a 4.55% 10Y remains a meaningful headwind for equity multiples, particularly in a session where SPY RSI(14) is now sitting at **40** — approaching but not yet at oversold territory.

The technical backdrop is mixed: SPY remains **+1.4% above its 50-SMA** and **+6.0% above its 200-SMA**, so the trend structure has not been broken. Today's move is a stress test of the intermediate trend, not yet a breakdown.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: No Floor from Dealer Positioning

**Closing Regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA — uniformly negative across the full chain. No flip level exists.**

This is the most important structural fact heading into Thursday. With no GEX flip level in the observable chain, there is no point at which dealer hedging mechanics shift from momentum-amplifying to momentum-dampening. The risk of continued directional extension — in either direction — remains elevated.

**Key levels for Thursday:**

| Level | Price | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,350 | Structural ceiling; dealer short-covering resistance |
| R2 | 7,340 | Confluence resistance near Call Wall |
| R1 | 7,303 | First intraday resistance on any recovery attempt |
| **Close** | **7,266.99** | Reference point |
| S1 | 7,231 | First downside support; watch for acceleration through |
| Put Wall | 7,220 | Key structural floor — concentration of put open interest |
| S2 | 7,194 | Secondary support; if Put Wall fails, next meaningful level |

**Expected move: ±102 points**, implying a Thursday range of approximately **7,165 – 7,369** based on the close.

The **Put Wall at 7,220** is the critical level to monitor. In a negative gamma regime, a clean break below 7,220 is not self-correcting — dealers would continue to sell into weakness, potentially accelerating a move toward S2 at **7,194**. Conversely, any recovery bid needs to clear **R1 at 7,303** to have credibility, with the **Call Wall at 7,350** acting as a ceiling on any intraday rally.

**Overnight and pre-market:** The OPEC Monthly Report at **6:00 ET** is the first catalyst of consequence. Given that rising oil was a cited driver of today's selloff, any hawkish OPEC tone (supply cuts maintained, demand upgrades) risks additional pressure on equities through the inflation channel heading into the **8:30 ET macro cluster**.

**8:30 ET is the primary risk window:** Core PPI MoM, Headline PPI MoM, Jobless Claims (4-week average), and Continuing Claims all print simultaneously. In the context of today's CPI-driven inflation anxiety, a hot PPI print could extend the risk-off move, potentially testing the Put Wall at 7,220 in the pre-market or early cash session. A soft PPI, by contrast, could trigger a meaningful short-cover — but in a negative gamma regime, any rally would need to be treated as a potential fade until dealer flows shift.

**Weekly risk context:** FOMC Economic Projections and Michigan Consumer Sentiment remain on the calendar later this week. The risk horizon is elevated, and position sizing should reflect that stacking of catalysts.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Negative Gamma Demands Directional or Hedged Structures

The closing negative gamma regime — with no flip level and VIX at 22 — is not an environment that rewards income-oriented, range-bound structures. Iron condors and credit spreads require mean-reversion; this regime delivers momentum and trend extension. Deploying those structures into Thursday's session carries meaningful assignment risk if PPI surprises.

**Structures aligned with the current regime:**

- **Defined-risk directional (debit spreads/verticals):** The negative gamma environment means dealer hedging will amplify moves. If PPI comes in hot and the tape breaks below the Put Wall at 7,220, a put debit spread (e.g., 7,220/7,150) captures directional exposure with defined risk. Strike selection should reference S2 at 7,194 and the ±102 expected move floor near 7,165.

- **Downside protection / tail hedges:** With VIX at 22 and the regime uniformly negative, long put structures remain reasonable as portfolio hedges — particularly ahead of a compressed catalyst schedule (PPI Thursday, FOMC projections later in the week). Implied volatility has moved up, but at 22 it is not yet at levels that make outright long vol prohibitively expensive for tactical hedges.

- **Short gamma / income structures: AVOID into Thursday's print window.** The 8:30 ET macro cluster creates binary gap risk that is fundamentally incompatible with short vega positions. If the regime were positive and VIX were compressing, that calculus would change — but it is not.

- **Recovery scenario:** Should PPI undershoot expectations and the tape recover above R1 at 7,303, a bull call spread targeting the Call Wall at 7,350 (e.g., 7,305/7,350) offers defined-risk participation with the ceiling already structurally defined by dealer positioning. But this is a reactive, data-dependent structure — not a pre-positioning thesis.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today confirmed the negative gamma regime with a strong trend day driven by macro shock. Tomorrow's session carries a stacked macro catalyst load at 8:30 ET with no structural dealer support below current price. The Put Wall at 7,220 and S2 at 7,194 define the downside risk corridor; the Call Wall at 7,350 caps recovery upside. Trade directionally, size for volatility, and do not fade momentum without confirmation.

*Stay nimble. Manage risk before thesis.*

# Evening Derivatives Desk Recap
### Thursday, June 11, 2026 | SPX 7394.3 | VIX 19.44

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro-Driven Risk-On Reversal

Today's move was unambiguously **macro/geopolitical in origin**, not an earnings or technical story. The primary catalyst was a sharp de-escalation in U.S.-Iran tensions — reports that Trump halted planned strikes and signaled a deal was near triggered an immediate repricing of the geopolitical risk premium that had been embedded in the tape. Secondary support came from easing crude prices as the Iran premium deflated, and softer yields (10Y -10bp to 4.45%) provided additional ballast for duration-sensitive growth names.

The sector rotation confirmed the narrative cleanly: **Technology +3.3% and Industrials +3.1% led**, while **Energy -1.6% and Consumer Defensive -0.7% lagged**. The 4.9% dispersion between the top and bottom sectors is notable — this was a *rotational* move out of safety and commodities and into risk, not a broad-based lift. Oracle's weak print was a notable individual name headwind, but it was absorbed without derailing the index tape, which speaks to how strong the macro tailwind was on the day.

The session range of 2.14% (Low: 7257.33 → High: 7412.68) with a strong close at 7394.3 (+1.76%) reflects a trend session with clear directional conviction from the open.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Forecast Contradicted on Direction, Confirmed on Character

The morning regime call was **NEGATIVE GAMMA, TREND day, directional bias DOWN** at 75% confidence. The trend day forecast was **correct** — the session delivered a clean, sustained directional move. However, the **direction was wrong**; the tape trended sharply higher rather than lower.

This is an important distinction. In a negative gamma environment, dealers are **short options** and must hedge by **buying into rallies and selling into dips** — which amplifies moves in whichever direction the macro catalyst drives. This morning's regime correctly identified the *mechanism* (trend, amplification), but the geopolitical shock that materialized was bullish rather than bearish, catching the directional forecast off-side.

Had the Iran escalation continued, the downside trend call would likely have been validated. Instead, the de-escalation news overrode the prior-session bearish tape, and negative gamma dealer mechanics amplified the upside move rather than a downside one. The regime was correct; the catalyst direction was not.

**Closing regime remains NEGATIVE.** No flip level exists across the observable chain — there is no in-range GEX zero-cross. The uniformly negative GEX structure persists into Friday's session.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Textbook Confirmation

VIX closed at **19.44, -12.52%** on the session — a substantial compression that confirmed the SPX rally was legitimate and not a low-conviction short-covering drift. A VIX decline of that magnitude alongside a +1.76% SPX move signals genuine demand for risk exposure and meaningful put unwind, not simply delta-hedging noise.

The VIX/SPX relationship behaved exactly as expected in a macro de-escalation: as the Iran risk premium was removed from the term structure, short-dated implied volatility collapsed, which in turn put downward pressure on the vol surface. This dynamic would have reinforced dealer negative gamma hedging — as SPX rallied and puts lost value rapidly, dealers were forced to buy SPX futures to maintain delta neutrality, further amplifying the upside trend. The session's 2.14% range is consistent with this feedback loop operating in full force.

RSI(14) sitting at 50 on the daily tells us the rally from session lows absorbed prior weakness without creating immediate overbought conditions. The price is now +2.6% above the 50-SMA and +7.6% above the 200-SMA — technically constructive, but the 50-SMA proximity suggests the recent consolidation/selloff had brought the market to a meaningful mean before today's reversal.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Navigating Sentiment Data in a Negative Gamma Tape

**Closing Regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA** — uniformly negative across the observable chain with no flip level. Dealer behavior tomorrow remains in short gamma mode: they will **buy strength and sell weakness**, meaning moves in either direction are prone to extension rather than reversion.

**Key Levels for Friday:**

| Level | Price | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| R2 | 7468.0 | Upper boundary of expected range |
| R1 | 7431.0 | First resistance above close |
| **Close** | **7394.3** | — |
| S1 | 7357.0 | First support; near put wall cluster |
| Put Wall | 7350.0 | Structural floor; heavy put concentration |
| S2 | 7320.0 | Secondary support; loss here accelerates |
| Call Wall | 7450.0 | Resistance ceiling; above R1 |
| **Expected Move** | **±91.0** | Range implies ~7303–7485 |

The **Put Wall at 7350** and **S1 at 7357** form a closely aligned support cluster that represents the critical downside reference tomorrow. A breach of 7350 in a negative gamma tape would be consequential — dealers short puts through that level would need to increase their selling to hedge, mechanically amplifying any downside. Conversely, the **Call Wall at 7450** sits just above R1 at 7431 and represents the logical ceiling for tomorrow's session absent a further bullish catalyst.

**Tomorrow's Catalyst — Michigan Consumer Sentiment (10:00 ET):**

The University of Michigan June preliminary print is the sole scheduled catalyst of consequence. The **1-Year Inflation Expectations** sub-component carries the most market-moving potential in the current macro context — recall that prior prints showing elevated inflation expectations contributed to the earlier selloff this week. A soft or in-line read could sustain today's risk-on momentum and push SPX toward R1/R2. A hotter-than-expected inflation expectations print, however, would reintroduce the rates-tightening narrative at a moment when the market is technically extended from the session low — and in a negative gamma environment, that downside could move quickly.

**Overnight consideration:** The geopolitical situation remains fluid. Today's rally was predicated on a deal or de-escalation being "near" — any reversal of that signal overnight would re-inject Iran risk premium and could gap the open below S1/Put Wall, triggering amplified selling given the negative gamma regime.

**Week-ahead context:** The **Fed Interest Rate Decision and FOMC Economic Projections** remain the dominant medium-term risk event. Position sizing should reflect the possibility that Friday's sentiment data, combined with the open FOMC risk, could create event-driven vol spikes early next week. Do not let today's VIX compression lull positioning into complacency — 19.44 is still an elevated absolute level.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

**Regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA → Dealer behavior is to BUY rallies and SELL dips → Moves extend.**

Income structures (iron condors, short straddles, credit spreads) are **not suited** to this regime. The negative gamma environment means the tape is prone to trending and breaking rather than mean-reverting within a range. Short premium strategies carry the risk of being on the wrong side of an amplified move.

**Preferred structures for Friday:**

- **Defined-risk directional debit spreads** remain the regime-appropriate vehicle. If the Michigan print is soft and SPX is pushing toward R1 (7431), a **bull call spread** structured in the 7400/7450 range (targeting the Call Wall) captures upside with defined risk inside the expected move.

- **Put debit spreads as hedge:** For those carrying long equity exposure, a **bear put spread** centered around the 7350 Put Wall (e.g., 7350/7300) provides cost-effective protection against a sentiment miss that breaks the put wall and triggers amplified negative gamma selling. The tight proximity of S1 (7357) and the Put Wall (7350) makes this a logical hedge anchor.

- **Avoid short vol / undefined risk structures** heading into the Michigan print and with FOMC on the calendar next week. The vol surface has compressed sharply today (-12.5% in VIX), but single-name and index vol could reprice quickly if the geopolitical or inflation narrative re-accelerates.

- **Width and sizing discipline:** With the expected move at ±91 points and the regime uniformly negative, structure widths should respect the full move envelope. Spreads too narrow around the current close risk being run through in either direction.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today's session was a textbook negative gamma trend day — just in the unexpected direction. The macro catalyst (Iran de-escalation) overrode the directional bias, and dealer hedging mechanics amplified the upside throughout the session. Tomorrow, the regime remains negative with no structural floor until the 7350 Put Wall. Michigan sentiment at 10:00 ET is a binary event in the current inflation-sensitive environment. Approach Friday with directional flexibility, defined risk, and respect for the amplification dynamics that this regime produces in both directions.

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Friday, June 12, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## LEAD STORY: REGIME FLIP — NEGATIVE TO POSITIVE GAMMA INTO THE CLOSE

The headline structural development tonight is a **confirmed gamma regime flip**. The morning opened under a **negative gamma** classification, but the session's directional bid — anchored by macro tailwinds — carried SPX through a regime transition, and the market closed in **positive gamma** territory. This is not a minor calibration; it represents a material shift in the dealer hedging landscape that reshapes the setup heading into next week.

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro and Event-Driven Risk-On

Today's move was not a technical or earnings story — it was **macro and event-driven**. Two primary catalysts defined the tape: the high-profile **SpaceX market debut** injected sentiment energy into the broader equity complex, while **declining oil prices** — underpinned by emerging expectations of a U.S.-Iran agreement — removed a key inflation and margin concern from the calculus. The combination produced a straightforward risk-on bid, with SPX closing at **7,431.46 (+0.51%)** on a session range of **1.27%** (High: 7,456.4, Low: 7,363.01).

Sector rotation confirmed the macro narrative rather than contradicting it. **Utilities (+2.0%)** and **Basic Materials (+0.8%)** led — an atypical combination that suggests rotation into defensives alongside reflation proxies, consistent with a "lower oil, softer rates fear" read. **Healthcare (-1.0%)** and **Communication Services (-0.8%)** lagged, reflecting neither a pure growth nor a pure defensive rotation, but rather idiosyncratic flow. The 10Y yield edged modestly higher to **4.48% (+3bp)**, which did not materially pressure equities — a mild positive for the risk narrative.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Morning Call Contradicted — Flip Confirmed

The morning forecast called **negative gamma** with a **70% confidence HIGH VOLATILITY** day type. The actual session came in as a **moderate TREND** — not the amplified, momentum-accelerating environment negative gamma typically produces. The day type forecast missed.

Here is the important nuance: a moderate trend in a negative gamma regime is not entirely inconsistent — negative gamma *amplifies* directional moves, and dealers hedge by **buying into rallies and selling into dips**, which can generate trending character. The move from the session low of 7,363 to the high of 7,456 is directionally coherent with that mechanic. However, the amplitude was contained relative to what a high-volatility negative gamma session typically produces, and crucially, **the regime flipped to positive by the close**.

The flip is the defining event. The session's bid was strong enough, and dealer positioning shifted sufficiently, that we cross the close in a **uniformly positive gamma** environment with **no observable GEX flip level across the chain**. Dealers are now positioned **long gamma** — meaning they will be inclined to **sell strength and buy weakness** to delta-hedge, creating a mean-reverting, range-dampening dynamic for Monday's session.

---

## 3. VIX / Gamma Interplay: Clean Confirmation

VIX closed at **17.68, down a significant -9.06%** against SPX's +0.51% gain. This is a notable vol crush relative to the equity move — the implied volatility decline was far larger than the spot move would typically justify on a percentage basis. This tells us the session was not merely directional; it was a **volatility-selling / regime normalization event**. Options sellers were active, premium was compressed, and the market's fear gauge repriced materially lower.

This VIX behavior is fully consistent with the regime flip narrative: as spot moved into zones where dealer gamma positioning turned net long, the structural bid for downside protection faded, VIX collapsed, and the regime transitioned. SPY RSI at **53** and price sitting **+2.9% above the 50-SMA** and **+8.2% above the 200-SMA** place us in technically constructive but not overbought territory. The technical backdrop does not argue against the positive gamma regime holding near-term.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Positive Gamma, Compressed Range, Light Catalyst Load

**Closing Regime: Positive Gamma — No GEX Flip Level in Chain**

Tomorrow's risk outlook is rated **Low** — the calendar is light, and normal trading is expected. This dovetails cleanly with the positive gamma regime. With dealers now **long gamma**, their hedging activity will work to **dampen volatility and suppress directional extension** — they will sell into strength and buy into weakness, anchoring the tape toward mean-reversion.

**Key Levels for Saturday/Monday context:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,450 | Dealer-sold call concentration; resistance ceiling |
| Put Wall | 7,425 | Structural support floor; gamma support zone |
| R1 | 7,469 | First upside resistance pivot |
| R2 | 7,506 | Extended upside; requires fresh catalyst |
| S1 | 7,394 | First downside support pivot |
| S2 | 7,357 | Secondary support; session low proximity |
| Expected Move | ±83 pts | Implied range: ~7,348 – 7,514 |

The **Put Wall at 7,425** and **Call Wall at 7,450** define a narrow 25-point corridor that will be the gravitational center of dealer hedging activity. In a positive gamma environment, this zone acts as a magnetic range — expect price to oscillate within and around these walls rather than break decisively in either direction on light volume. Today's close at 7,431 is already nestled between these walls, reinforcing the range-compression thesis.

A sustained move above **7,469 (R1)** would require either fresh catalyst or significant weekend development. A break of **7,394 (S1)** would need macro deterioration, and even then, the uniformly positive gamma structure suggests dealers would be buyers into that weakness, providing a cushion.

**Looking further out — the week is elevated risk.** The upcoming **Fed Interest Rate Decision and FOMC Economic Projections** (later in the week) are the dominant macro events on the horizon. This is Kevin Warsh's first FOMC meeting as Fed Chair, a moment of institutional significance the market will price carefully. Position sizing should reflect this elevated weekly risk, even if tomorrow's session is expected to be quiet.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Income Structures in Focus for Monday

**Regime: Positive Gamma → Mean-Reverting, Range-Dampening Environment**

With dealers long gamma — **selling rallies and buying dips to hedge** — the structural bias is toward a contained, range-bound tape. This is the environment that rewards **income-generating, non-directional structures**. The strategy toolkit for this regime:

- **Iron Condors** around the 7,425–7,450 corridor: Selling the wings against a market anchored by dealer hedging flows is structurally coherent. The 25-point Put/Call Wall corridor is your tent pole.
- **Credit Spreads (both sides):** Given the expected move of ±83 points, there is meaningful premium available at the R2/S2 extremes (7,506 / 7,357) that a positive gamma regime argues are unlikely to be reached on a low-catalyst Friday/Monday setup.
- **Short-dated premium collection:** VIX at 17.68 — down sharply today but not at suppressed extremes — still offers workable premium for disciplined sellers. The vol crush suggests the market is no longer pricing elevated weekend risk aggressively.

**What to avoid:** Aggressive directional debit structures that require sustained trending. In a positive gamma regime, dealers are *working against* momentum — rallies get sold, dips get bought. A debit spread premised on a clean breakout above 7,469 or below 7,394 is structurally fighting the dealer hedging flow on what is projected to be a light-catalyst session.

**The important caveat for the week ahead:** As the FOMC approaches, the gamma regime may come under pressure. Fed uncertainty events have a history of pulling dealers toward shorter hedges and flipping regimes toward negative. Do not carry heavy short-vol/income structures into Wednesday without reassessing the regime. Scale accordingly.

---

# Evening Derivatives Desk Recap
### Monday, June 15, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## HEADLINE: Regime Flip to Positive — Iran Deal Triggers Macro Squeeze, Dealers Now Absorb Rather Than Amplify

---

## 1. SESSION FLOW: Geopolitical Catalyst Overwhelms Structural Backdrop

Today's move was unambiguously macro-driven. Reports of a U.S.-Iran peace deal — and the accompanying collapse in oil prices — served as the primary ignition source for a broad risk-on squeeze that lifted SPX +1.66% on the session (range: 7516.75 – 7577.92, a 0.81% band). This was not an earnings-driven move, nor a technical 0DTE grinding session — it was a headline event that repriced geopolitical risk premium in a single session.

**Critically, the morning gamma regime was NEGATIVE**, meaning dealers were short options heading into the day. When the Iran headlines broke and the tape accelerated higher, dealers were forced to **buy the rally to hedge their short gamma exposure** — precisely the dynamic that amplifies directional moves. The 1.66% close on a 0.81% intraday range suggests the bulk of the move occurred in bursts, consistent with negative gamma momentum feeding. The day type call — TREND (UP), 75% confidence — was validated cleanly.

Sector rotation complicates the "simple risk-on" narrative, however. Per today's sector data, **Industrials led at +3.3%** while Consumer Cyclicals (-0.4%) and Basic Materials (-0.6%) actually lagged. This is not a textbook "Iran deal = growth trade" rotation — it reads more like an industrial/defense policy re-rating overlaid on the geopolitical shift, not a pure tech/growth surge as some news wires suggested. Defer to the sector data over the narrative.

---

## 2. REGIME VALIDATION: Morning Called Negative — Session Delivered, Then Flipped

The morning regime call was **NEGATIVE GAMMA**, and today's session validated it emphatically. Dealers were short options, and the Iran catalyst gave them no choice but to chase the move — buying into strength as SPX pressed higher. Momentum was self-reinforcing, range expansion was expected, and that is precisely what printed.

**The structural story for tomorrow, however, is materially different:** the closing regime has **flipped to POSITIVE GAMMA**. This is the lead story going into Tuesday's session. The sharp intraday rally — combined with options market repricing as IV collapsed (VIX -8.38% to 16.2) — has repositioned the dealer book. Dealers are now net long options at the closing mark.

**One important nuance:** the GEX calculation for the closing regime was derived from ES futures at **7618.75** due to a stale SPX cash print of 7554.29. The regime classification reflects where futures are trading, not the cash close. The ~65-point gap between the two is non-trivial — traders should anchor structural levels to the futures-implied GEX, not the cash index print.

---

## 3. VIX/GAMMA INTERPLAY: Volatility Confirmation Was Clean

VIX closing at 16.2, down 8.38%, is fully consistent with the session's character. A geopolitical risk premium — previously embedded in vol — was rapidly unwound once the Iran deal became legible to the market. This was not a case of VIX diverging from price action; it confirmed the directional move.

The vol crush matters structurally: the collapse in implied volatility is partly responsible for the gamma regime flip. As options repriced lower across the chain, dealer net positioning shifted from short gamma to long gamma. **The VIX decline didn't just reflect the rally — it mechanically contributed to the regime change.** That's a feedback loop worth tracking. If the Iran deal frays or new geopolitical noise emerges overnight, a VIX snapback could reassert negative gamma conditions faster than the current positioning implies.

---

## 4. TOMORROW'S SETUP: Positive Gamma, Light Catalyst Load — Range Compression Expected

**Regime:** POSITIVE GAMMA (closing basis, GEX anchored to ES ~7618.75)
**Key Levels:** R1: 7592 | R2: 7630 | S1: 7517 | S2: 7479
**Structural Walls:** Put Wall: 7500 | Call Wall: 7650
**Expected Move:** ±77 points (roughly 7477 – 7631 from cash close)

With no observable GEX flip level across the chain, the regime is **uniformly positive** — there is no proximity-to-flip concern to flag. In this environment, dealers are long options and will **sell strength and buy weakness to delta-hedge**, acting as a structural dampener on directional moves. Sessions in this configuration tend to be range-bound and mean-reverting.

**Key levels for Tuesday:**
- **7592 (R1)** is the first meaningful upside test. A push here will likely encounter dealer selling pressure in a positive gamma regime.
- **7630 (R2) / 7650 (Call Wall)** represents the upper structural cap. The expected move ceiling of ~7631 coincides almost precisely with R2 and the Call Wall — this cluster deserves respect as a hard ceiling unless a new macro catalyst emerges.
- **7517 (S1)** is the near-term downside pivot — notably close to today's session low of 7516.75. A breach of this level on volume would be technically significant.
- **7500 (Put Wall)** provides the structural gamma cushion below. Dealers will be buyers of weakness approaching this level, reinforcing support.

**Catalyst Load:** Light. Housing Starts, Building Permits, and Import/Export Prices at 08:30 ET are all low-impact data points in isolation. None of these individually move markets. However, given that the Iran deal's macro implications include an oil price shock reversal, the import price data carries marginal relevance as a tariff/inflation read — don't ignore it entirely.

**Week-level risk is elevated:** FOMC Economic Projections and the Fed Rate Decision are on the horizon. Traders should treat Tuesday as a positioning session ahead of heavier risk mid-to-late week. Overnight risk remains asymmetric — any Iran deal deterioration or geopolitical headlines could reverse today's vol compression quickly.

---

## 5. STRATEGY IMPLICATIONS: Positive Gamma Favors Income Structures — But FOMC Week Demands Caution

In a **positive gamma** regime, dealer hedging behavior acts as a ceiling on volatility and a dampener on directional follow-through. Dealers will sell into rallies and buy dips, compressing the range and supporting mean-reversion. This environment structurally favors:

- **Iron Condors** anchored within the 7500–7650 structural walls (Put Wall / Call Wall), which align closely with the ±77-point expected move.
- **Credit Spreads** — both call-side (7620–7650) and put-side (7480–7517) — structured to capture premium in a range-bound tape with dealer support at the wings.
- **Short-dated premium selling** remains viable given VIX at 16.2 and the positive gamma suppression dynamic.

**Critical overlay — FOMC risk this week:** Any income structure initiated Tuesday must account for the potential for rapid vol re-expansion ahead of the Fed decision. Do not carry undefined risk into the FOMC window. Use defined-risk structures, keep notional sizing disciplined, and ensure positions can be managed if VIX reverses sharply from 16.2 back toward 19-20+ on any policy surprise or geopolitical relapse.

**The base case for Tuesday is a quieter, more controlled session** — positive gamma suppressing volatility, light data, and the market digesting today's geopolitical repricing. But the week's tail risk is real. Play Tuesday for compression; preserve capital and flexibility for what comes later in the week.

---

*Levels to watch overnight: ES 7618 (GEX anchor), 7650 (Call Wall), 7500 (Put Wall). Any material gap in either direction pre-market should be evaluated against the positive gamma structural bias before fading or following.*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Wednesday, June 18, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## METHODOLOGICAL NOTE

With SPX printing a stale 7,420.10 close from the prior session, this morning's dealer gamma was recalculated at the ES-implied opening price of 7,538.5 rather than the prior cash close. At that implied level, the regime read **positive** at the open, with dealer hedging structured around a higher moneyness distribution. The session's subsequent price action materially changed that picture — more on that below.

---

## 1. SESSION FLOW — MACRO CATALYST DOMINATES

Today's tape was unambiguously **macro-driven**. Chair Warsh's inaugural FOMC decision delivered the hawkish surprise the market had not fully priced: rates held steady, but the statement and press conference shifted the rate path distribution meaningfully toward further tightening, with multiple officials now signaling the possibility of rate hikes this year. The CNBC headline framing it as the worst "Fed Day" S&P 500 performance for a new chair since 1994 tells you everything about the magnitude of the sentiment shock.

The selloff was not indiscriminate. **Communication Services (-2.0%) and Industrials (-3.0%)** led the downside, with the semiconductor and broader tech complex remaining under sustained pressure — a continuation of weakness that had been building ahead of today's event. Meanwhile, **Utilities (+1.5%) and Financial Services (+0.1%)** outperformed, the latter consistent with news reports of JPMorgan hitting intraday records and financials holding relative strength in a hawkish rate environment. Sector dispersion of **4.6%** confirms this was a rotation trade, not a uniform de-risking flush. The wide dispersion also suggests active positioning shifts rather than passive outflows.

The 10-year yield closing at **4.49% (+6bp)** and the curve steepening slightly to **+29bp** on the 2Y-10Y spread provides the rates confirmation: the bond market validated the hawkish read, and equity multiples — particularly in rate-sensitive growth — repriced accordingly.

---

## 2. REGIME VALIDATION — MORNING CALL CONTRADICTED; REGIME FLIPPED TO NEGATIVE

This is the structural story of the session, and it deserves direct treatment.

**The morning call was POSITIVE gamma at the ES-implied open of 7,538.5.** In a positive gamma regime, dealers are net long options and hedge by selling into strength and buying weakness — a mechanical stabilizer that compresses realized volatility and tends to produce range-bound, mean-reverting sessions.

**That is not what happened.** SPX printed a session high of **7,532.17** early and then sold off with conviction through the afternoon, ultimately closing at **7,420.10** — a **1.75% range** and a **strong trend session**, not a controlled mean-reversion. The day type forecast was HIGH VOLATILITY at 70% confidence; the actual session type was TREND (strong). The directional read on volatility was correct; the gamma regime's dampening expectation was not.

What changed: as SPX broke lower through the morning, the open interest distribution shifted against the positive gamma assumption. The macro shock from Warsh's remarks accelerated the move below key strike concentrations, eroding the positive gamma buffer that had been estimated at the ES-implied open. Once the market moved through those strikes, dealer hedging flows that had been stabilizing flipped in character. **The closing regime is now NEGATIVE gamma**, confirming the regime transitioned intraday — a direct consequence of both the directional magnitude and the speed of the post-Fed repricing.

**Bottom line:** The morning positive gamma read was methodologically sound at the ES-implied open. The macro catalyst overwhelmed the mechanical support, and we close in a different structural regime than we opened in.

---

## 3. VIX / GAMMA INTERPLAY

VIX closed at **18.44, up +12.38%** on the session — a sharp, directionally coherent move alongside SPX's -1.22%. This is the expected relationship; VIX behaved normally relative to equity direction.

What's notable is the *magnitude* of the VIX move relative to the SPX decline. A ~12% VIX expansion on a ~1.2% SPX drop implies a vol/spot sensitivity well above the recent baseline, consistent with the negative gamma environment that crystallized intraday. In a positive gamma regime, dealer hedging absorbs realized vol and suppresses implied vol expansion. The size of today's VIX move is, itself, empirical evidence that the gamma buffer was not present — or was removed — during the afternoon session.

At **18.44**, VIX is elevated but not in a stress regime. It remains below the 20 threshold that historically triggers systematic vol-targeting de-risking. That said, the directional trajectory matters: a VIX trending from 16 toward 20+ while the gamma regime is negative is a compounding risk factor, not a stabilizing one.

---

## 4. TOMORROW'S SETUP

**Closing Regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA — No Flip Level in Observable Chain**

This is the critical structural input for Thursday's session. With no flip level anywhere in the observable options chain, the regime is **uniformly negative** across the distribution. There is no mechanical level at which dealer hedging behavior reverses. This means:

- Dealers are **net short options (short gamma)**.
- Their hedging behavior **amplifies directional moves**: they buy into rallies and sell into further weakness.
- There is no embedded stabilizer in the structure.

**Key Levels for Thursday:**

- **Put Wall: 7,400** — SPX closed at 7,420.10, putting the put wall just **20 handles below the close**. This is the most critical level for overnight and early-session framing. A break and hold below 7,400 removes the near-term structural support and opens the path toward **S1 at 7,383** and **S2 at 7,346**.
- **S1: 7,383 / S2: 7,346** — These are the first two meaningful downside references below the put wall.
- **R1: 7,457 / R2: 7,494** — Resistance levels for any attempt at a recovery bid. R2 at 7,494 aligns roughly with the morning's session high vicinity and represents a meaningful gamma-weighted overhead.
- **Call Wall: 7,550** — Far overhead; not a realistic near-term target given current positioning, but marks the ceiling of dealer short-call concentration.
- **Expected Move: ±86 handles** — Thursday's expected range is **7,334 to 7,506**. Note that the lower bound of the expected move at 7,334 sits **below S2 (7,346)**, flagging tail risk to the downside in a negative gamma environment where moves can overshoot.

**Catalyst Load (Thursday 08:30 ET):**

The morning is front-loaded with **labor market and regional manufacturing data**:
- Philadelphia Fed Manufacturing Index (Jun) — medium impact
- Initial Jobless Claims (Jun/13) — medium impact
- Continuing Jobless Claims (Jun/06) — medium impact
- Jobless Claims 4-Week Average — medium impact
- Philly Fed Employment — low impact

In isolation, these are medium-tier releases. In the context of **Warsh's hawkish pivot yesterday**, however, they carry asymmetric interpretive risk. A strong Philly Fed or a meaningful drop in claims would be read as validation of the "no cuts needed, possibly hike" scenario — reinforcing the bearish rate narrative from today. A weak claims print or a soft manufacturing read could provide a tactical relief bid, but the burden of proof for a sustained recovery is now higher given the regime and narrative backdrop.

**Overnight framing:** The proximity of SPX to the **7,400 put wall** is the single most important overnight variable. Watch whether futures hold above 7,400 into the 8:30 data window. A gap below 7,400 pre-market, in a negative gamma environment with no flip level, could accelerate quickly toward 7,383–7,346.

---

## 5. STRATEGY IMPLICATIONS

**Regime: Negative Gamma | Catalyst Load: Moderate | Risk Outlook: Moderate**

In a negative gamma environment, dealer hedging **amplifies directional moves** — they buy into strength and sell into further weakness, providing momentum rather than friction. This structural backdrop favors **directional structures over income structures**.

**Structures to consider:**

- **Defined-risk bearish verticals (put debit spreads)** — With SPX 20 handles above the put wall and a uniformly negative gamma regime, directional downside exposure with defined risk is the structurally coherent expression. The 7,400/7,346 zone (put wall to S2) is a natural spread anchor. The ±86 expected move supports strike selection in that range.
- **Long puts / bear verticals on continuation names** — Communication Services and Industrials, which lagged materially today (-2.0% and -3.0% respectively), remain the highest-beta expressions of the Warsh hawkish repricing theme.
- **Avoid short volatility / income structures** — Iron condors, credit spreads, and naked short vol are structurally misaligned with a negative gamma regime. With no flip level in the chain and VIX trending toward 18.44, dealers will not be dampening moves. Gamma-driven range compression is not the base case.
- **Gamma scalping / long vega plays** — If VIX continues its trajectory toward 20+, long vega structures gain additional tailwind from both directional exposure and vol expansion. Straddles anchored near current spot are expensive at these levels but the convexity is real.

**Tactically:** The 08:30 data cluster tomorrow is the binary event. Size exposure accordingly heading into the print. A soft labor read that pushes back on the hawkish Warsh narrative could produce a sharp relief rally — in a negative gamma environment, that rally would also be amplified. The two-sided risk is real; the regime does not favor one direction, it favors **larger moves in either direction**.

---

**Desk Summary:** Today's session was a macro-driven regime flip event. Warsh's hawkish pivot overwhelmed the morning positive gamma structure, transitioning us to a uniformly negative gamma closing regime with no flip level in the chain. The put wall at 7,400 is the overnight line in the sand — 20 handles from the close. Thursday's labor data is the next catalyst, interpreted through a newly hawkish lens. Negative gamma means size and direction matter more than usual. Remain disciplined on risk definition.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Brief
**Thursday, June 18, 2026 | Post-Close**

---

## Methodological Note

With SPX carrying a stale prior close of 7500.58 while ES futures were pricing at **7564.75** overnight, we recalculated dealer gamma at the ES-implied opening price using Black-Scholes rather than the stale cash print. This is standard practice when a meaningful gap separates the prior cash close from futures-implied spot — presenting the regime off a stale level would misrepresent true dealer moneyness and skew wall readings. All GEX values, regime classifications, and structural levels below reflect the ES-implied recalculation.

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro Catalysts Overrode the Gamma Playbook

Today's move was **unambiguously macro-driven**. Three interlocking catalysts stacked into a coherent risk-on narrative: the **U.S.-Iran interim agreement** created a geopolitical relief valve, pulling energy risk premiums lower; **10-year yields softened 3bp to 4.46%** after Wednesday's hawkish Fed-driven spike (with new Chair Kevin Warsh's debut FOMC signaling rattling the bond market mid-week); and **mega-cap technology** surged on the reported **Intel-Apple chip partnership**, injecting genuine fundamental momentum into the Nasdaq's +1.9% session.

The result was a **strong trend session** — SPX +1.09%, session range a modest 42 points (7468.32–7511.07), and VIX collapsing **-11.07% to 16.4**. The breadth of the rally, however, was notably **narrow and growth-skewed**: Technology led at +1.7%, Utilities followed at +1.3% (a rates-sensitive defensive bid on the yield pullback), while Healthcare lagged -1.2% and Real Estate -1.0%. The Dow's +0.1% advance against the Nasdaq's +1.9% gain underscores the concentration: this was a mega-cap growth trade, not a broad cyclical rotation.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Positive Gamma, But the Session Character Ran Hot

The morning regime call was **POSITIVE GAMMA**, with a predicted day type of **MIXED/CHOP at 55% confidence**. The actual session delivered a **strong trend** — a meaningful forecast miss that warrants explanation.

The regime classification itself was not wrong; dealer positioning in positive gamma means dealers are **long options, selling into strength and buying weakness** to maintain delta neutrality. That hedging flow should have acted as a structural dampener. And in isolation, the 0.57% session range *was* contained relative to the macro catalyst load — a 1.09% directional move on a geopolitical/tech catalyst day without a VIX spike is arguably a positive-gamma session that simply ran into macro force majeure.

What overwhelmed the dampening mechanism was the **stacking of three macro drivers simultaneously** (Iran deal + yield relief + Apple-Intel chip headline) into a market where positioning was already skewed toward growth recovery. Dealer hedging flows were a real but secondary force today; the primary driver was **net new buying pressure** from macro catalyst resolution. The regime did not flip — it held positive — but the magnitude of the directional move slightly exceeded what a well-anchored positive gamma environment typically permits. Consider this a **regime-consistent session with an asterisk**: positive gamma contained the *tail*, but could not fully suppress a macro-driven trend impulse of this character.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Textbook Confirmation

VIX's **-11.07% close to 16.4** is a clean confirmation signal. A falling VIX into a rising SPX is the standard signature of a **positive gamma, realized volatility compression environment** — dealers long options are not panic-hedging, and the options market is pricing *less* forward uncertainty as spot moves higher. The absence of any VIX divergence (i.e., VIX spiking as SPX rallied, which would signal structural stress) validates the regime read.

The **2Y-10Y spread at +27bp (normal curve)** and the 10-year at 4.46% after Wednesday's Warsh-driven jump suggests bond markets are stabilizing near a new equilibrium rather than repricing aggressively. That yield stability removed a key overhang and allowed equity multiples, particularly in rate-sensitive tech, to expand without the headwind of a rising discount rate.

At **SPY RSI(14) of 48**, the tape is technically neutral — not overbought, not oversold — meaning today's rally does not yet carry the overhead exhaustion risk that often accompanies stretched readings above 70. Price sits **+2.5% above the 50-SMA and +8.6% above the 200-SMA**: a constructive trend posture with room before the longer-term trend becomes a crowded trade.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Light Calendar, Positive Gamma Structure

**Closing Regime: POSITIVE — no GEX flip level present across the observable chain.** The regime is uniformly positive. There is no strike where cumulative GEX crosses zero within the observable range, meaning there is **no structural cliff** that a momentum move could push through to flip dealer behavior. Frame tomorrow as a range-anchored session in the absence of exogenous catalyst.

**Key Levels:**

| Level | Price | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | **7600** | Hard ceiling; maximum dealer short-gamma exposure above |
| R2 | **7576** | Second resistance; likely intraday exhaustion zone |
| R1 | **7538** | First resistance; initial upside target on continuation |
| Current Close | **7500.58** | Pivot reference |
| S1 | **7463** | First support; dealer buy flow likely strengthens here |
| S2 | **7426** | Secondary support; 0.57% below S1, meaningful drawdown |
| Put Wall | **7400** | Structural floor; heavy dealer long positioning below |

**Expected Move:** ±78 points. This frames a 1-sigma band of approximately **7422–7578**, neatly bracketed by S2 and just inside R2. The expected move is consistent with positive gamma containment — the market itself is pricing a rangebound session.

**Overnight/Catalyst Watch:** Tomorrow's risk outlook is rated **Low** — light calendar, normal trading expected. There are no macro releases of consequence on Friday's docket. The near-term elevated risk window is the **following week**, when **Personal Income MoM (May) and Core PCE Price Index YoY (May)** are due. Core PCE, as the Fed's preferred inflation gauge, will carry added weight given Warsh's hawkish debut this week. Position sizing ahead of that print deserves attention even if tomorrow itself is quiet.

The primary overnight risk remains **headline reversion** — specifically, any deterioration in the U.S.-Iran interim agreement or a fresh Warsh/Fed communication that re-steepens yields. Neither is in the base case, but both are tail risks given the geopolitical and monetary policy uncertainty that framed this week.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Positive Gamma Favors Income Structures

In a **positive gamma environment with no observable flip level**, dealer hedging behavior acts as a natural range enforcer. Dealers are **long options** and will **sell into rallies and buy dips** to stay delta-neutral — this is the suppression mechanism that makes the session range structurally mean-reverting. With an expected move of ±78 points and VIX at 16.4 (still elevated enough to offer reasonable premium but not stressed), the setup favors **income and range structures**.

**Preferred structures for tomorrow's session:**

- **Iron Condors / Credit Spreads:** The 7400 Put Wall and 7600 Call Wall define the structural boundaries. Selling premium between these walls — particularly via an iron condor with wings near 7400P / 7600C — captures the positive gamma range-reversion dynamic. The absence of a flip level across the chain means the dampening structure is consistent across strikes, reducing the risk of a sudden dealer behavior reversal mid-session.

- **Put Credit Spreads (bullish bias):** If one has a directional lean following today's macro-driven rally, a put credit spread centered near S1 (7463) allows participation in continued positive gamma support while defining downside risk. The 7463/7426 strike zone offers a natural spread construction anchored to tomorrow's key support levels.

- **Avoid naked directional debit structures:** A debit spread or directional vertical banking on sustained momentum runs against the dealer flow. In a positive gamma regime, sustained trends require macro catalysts to overpower the hedging dampener — with a light calendar tomorrow, that catalyst is not in the base case.

**Regime Watch for Next Week:** As Core PCE approaches, monitor GEX trajectory closely. A VIX expansion above 18–19 ahead of the print, or a meaningful SPX break below S2 (7426), could signal a regime transition that would shift strategy posture from income to defined-risk directional. Flag that inflection point now, not after it moves.

# Evening Derivatives Recap — Thursday, June 25, 2026

---

## Session Flow: Macro Headwinds, Rotational Character

Today's session was macro-driven in narrative but structurally rotational in execution. The primary overhangs — AI expenditure concerns and elevated inflation (PCE running at a 4.1% annual rate) — weighed on growth-sensitive names, while Micron's earnings beat provided a partial offset that limited downside follow-through in tech. The net result: SPX essentially flat on the day (-0.01%, closing at **7,357.49**) but with meaningful dispersion beneath the surface.

Sector rotation told the real story. **Basic Materials (+1.0%) and Energy (+0.9%)** led, while **Technology (-2.2%) and Financial Services (-1.3%)** lagged — a wide **3.1% dispersion spread** consistent with a rotational, risk-redistribution session rather than a broad-based directional move. Oil's sharp decline (Brent -4%) paradoxically pressured Energy sector names in equities despite being a macro relief valve elsewhere, illustrating the complexity of today's cross-asset flows.

---

## Regime Validation: Negative Gamma, But Session Didn't Cooperate

The morning call was **negative gamma, high volatility (70% confidence)** — a setup that implied dealer hedging behavior would *amplify* moves, with dealers buying into rallies and selling into dips, creating trending, momentum-driven price action.

The session delivered a **range-bound outcome**, not a trending one. The session high/low range of **1.31%** (High: 7,419.08 / Low: 7,323.50) was wide enough to suggest instability, but the *close* at 7,357.49 — nearly mid-range — reflects mean reversion, not amplification. In a true negative gamma environment, we would typically expect directional resolution, not a round trip.

The most likely explanation: competing forces offset dealer gamma amplification. The macro uncertainty around PCE (priced via elevated VIX9D proximity to spot) created two-sided hedging demand, and the rotational nature of the session dispersed flow across sectors rather than concentrating it directionally in index products. The absence of a GEX flip level anywhere in the observable chain meant there was no structural level to trigger a momentum cascade — dealers remained in negative gamma but without a catalyst sufficient to force the cascade that regime implied. The high volatility forecast was technically validated by the session range, but the directional follow-through was not.

---

## VIX/Gamma Interplay: A Divergence Worth Watching

VIX closed at **18.89 (+1.40%)** against a near-flat SPX — a mild divergence from the typical inverse relationship, and directionally opposite to what the news narrative might suggest. This is notable. A rising VIX against a flat index signals that *options markets are repricing tail risk* even as realized price movement stays contained. In a negative gamma regime, this is a yellow flag: dealers short options into a VIX that is quietly creeping higher face increasing mark-to-market pressure on their short books, which can accelerate hedging behavior on a catalyst.

SPY RSI at **39** — approaching oversold on a 14-period basis — adds context. The index is holding +0.2% above its 50-SMA and +6.5% above its 200-SMA, so structural damage is limited. But the RSI compression, combined with rising VIX and negative gamma, creates a fragile equilibrium heading into Friday.

---

## Tomorrow's Setup: Negative Gamma, Walls in Place, Moderate Catalyst Load

**Closing regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA.** With no GEX flip level anywhere in the observable chain, the regime is uniformly negative across tomorrow's range. This is the baseline structural condition: dealer flows *amplify* directional moves rather than dampen them. If price trends, dealers buy into strength and sell into weakness — reinforcing momentum in either direction.

**Note:** ES futures are trading at **7,407.5** vs. the SPX close of **7,357.49**, implying a **~0.7% upside gap** heading into the open. Gaps of this magnitude in a negative gamma regime warrant careful monitoring — they can fill rapidly if early flows lack follow-through, and a failed gap open in this regime can accelerate to the downside as dealer hedging reinforces the move. Do not anchor to the gap; let the cash open confirm.

**Key structural levels for Friday:**

| Level | Price | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| R2 | 7,431.0 | Upper technical resistance |
| Call Wall | 7,400.0 | Dealer short-strike concentration — supply zone |
| R1 | 7,394.0 | First resistance pivot |
| **SPX Close** | **7,357.49** | **Overnight anchor** |
| S1 | 7,321.0 | First support pivot |
| Put Wall | 7,325.0 | Dealer short-strike floor — demand zone |
| S2 | 7,284.0 | Secondary support / downside extension target |

The **call wall at 7,400** and **put wall at 7,325** define the structural corridor. These are the concentrations of dealer short-strike exposure — price approaching either wall will face natural resistance as dealers adjust hedges. In a negative gamma environment, a *clean break* through either wall removes that resistance and accelerates the move; price does not mean-revert through walls in this regime.

**Tomorrow's expected move: ±88 points**, defining an effective range of approximately **7,269 to 7,445** from close.

**Catalysts (Friday, June 27):**
- **Goods Trade Balance (Advance, May) — 08:30 ET [Medium]:** An early-session data point that could influence USD and rate sentiment at the open. A significantly wider deficit could weigh on growth expectations; a narrower print would be benign.
- **Fed's Kashkari Speech — 11:30 ET [Medium]:** Mid-session Fed speak carries asymmetric risk in the current inflation context. With PCE printing at 4.1%, any hawkish nuance from Kashkari on the rate path could reignite the tech/growth rotation and push VIX higher. Watch for language on the pace of normalization.

**Week-level risk remains elevated.** Next week brings **Nonfarm Payrolls (Private)** and the **Unemployment Rate (June)** — these data points will dominate positioning into month-end and the holiday period. Expect structural hedging demand to build through next week, keeping VIX elevated.

---

## Strategy Implications: Respect the Regime, Manage the Gap

**Regime is negative gamma — uniformly so.** Dealer behavior is pro-directional: they buy into strength, sell into weakness. Income structures that depend on range-bound behavior (iron condors, credit spreads) carry meaningful risk if a catalyst triggers a clean move through either wall.

**Appropriate structures for this regime and catalyst profile:**

- **Defined-risk directional (debit spreads / vertical spreads):** The correct structural fit for negative gamma. If Kashkari signals hawkish, a put debit spread with strikes anchored near the 7,325 put wall and targeting S2 at 7,284 captures the amplification dynamic with defined risk. If the gap holds and buyers take out the call wall at 7,400, a call debit spread targeting R2 at 7,431 benefits from the same dealer flow reinforcement.

- **Long premium / straddle bias:** VIX at 18.89 is not cheap, but with negative gamma uniformly in place and no flip level to dampen moves, long gamma structures benefit from the regime directly. The ±88 expected move sets the breakeven threshold.

- **Avoid short premium outright:** Selling iron condors or naked credit spreads into a uniformly negative gamma regime with a VIX trending higher and a gap open is structurally misaligned. The dealer flow profile does not support the mean-reversion thesis those structures require.

**Position sizing:** Moderate catalyst load tomorrow, elevated risk heading into NFP next week. Size accordingly. The 7,400 call wall and 7,325 put wall are the two levels that matter most — respect them as inflection points, not targets.

---

*Risk is not the presence of uncertainty — it's the failure to price it correctly. Tonight's structure says the regime is working against complacency. Manage accordingly.*

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy*

## 1. Session Flow: Earnings Overhang, Not Macro

Today's session was driven by **semiconductor/earnings spillover**, not a macro catalyst. The -0.05% SPX print masked meaningful intraday turbulence — a 1.35% session range (7294.18 to 7392.95) that saw the index test both extremes before closing nearly flat. The primary culprit was continued memory sector fallout: Micron -7% and Sandisk -10%+ as post-earnings volatility cascaded through the semi complex. That pressure was isolated enough that it didn't metastasize into broad-based risk-off — the index held, but the damage was concentrated.

Notably, a headline crossing late in the session — **U.S. strikes on Iran following an alleged ceasefire violation in the Strait of Hormuz** — introduced a geopolitical risk premium that is likely a partial driver of the +0.6% ES gap overnight. This is a developing story with the potential to reprice energy, shipping, and volatility markets early next week. It should not be dismissed as noise.

Separately, **Fed's Kashkari projecting one rate hike this year** adds a hawkish lean to the rates backdrop, though the 10Y yield at 4.38% (-2bp today) and a normal 2Y-10Y spread of +31bp suggest the market has not yet repriced aggressively for that scenario.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Negative Gamma — Predicted Trend, Got Range

The morning call was **TREND POTENTIAL at 60% confidence** within a negative gamma regime. The session delivered **RANGE BOUND** — a contradiction worth examining.

In a negative gamma environment, dealers are **short options** and hedge by buying into strength and selling into weakness, which amplifies directional moves. The structural setup supported trending behavior. What it got instead was a 1.35% range that opened low, ripped to session highs, and then faded back to nearly unchanged — effectively two competing directional impulses that canceled each other out.

The likely explanation: the semiconductor-specific selling created downside pressure early, but the absence of a macro catalyst and the narrow scope of the damage (tech/semis, not broad market) meant the selling exhausted itself intraday. Rotation into Healthcare (+2.8%) and Consumer Cyclical (+1.6%) provided an offsetting bid that arrested the directional momentum negative gamma would otherwise amplify. This was a **sector rotation session masquerading inside a trend-prone regime**, not a regime failure per se — but the forecast miss is real and worth noting. The 3.2% sector dispersion tells the story: this market moved within, not broadly.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: VIX Confirmed, Dealer Flows Contained

VIX closed at 18.41 (-2.55%), which is **directionally consistent** with an SPX session that held losses to five basis points. The vol compression is notable: despite a 98-point intraday range, implied volatility declined. That tells you protection sellers were active and the intraday moves — while real — did not register as regime-threatening to the vol market.

SPY RSI at 36 places the index in **approaching-oversold territory**, which historically creates a technical backdrop where bounces become more likely, though not guaranteed. The -0.7% gap below the 50-SMA is a headwind that the index has not yet resolved. Price remains 5.6% above the 200-SMA, so the larger structural trend remains intact.

With no GEX flip level present anywhere in the observable chain, the negative gamma regime is **uniformly embedded across strikes**. There is no gamma cushion to dampen moves — dealers remain short options across the board, meaning **any directional conviction from Monday's open carries amplification risk, not suppression**.

---

## 4. Tomorrow Setup: Light Calendar, But Geopolitics in the Overnight

**Tomorrow (Monday, June 30)** carries a **low near-term risk rating** — a light calendar with no scheduled macro events. However, the **weekly risk rating is elevated** given the approach of **Nonfarm Payrolls (Private and Headline) for June**, which will be the primary macro catalyst later in the week. - A **confirmed break above 7392–7400** opens the path toward R2 at 7428. In negative gamma, dealers will be buying that rally to hedge their short exposure — amplifying the move higher.
- A **rejection at R1** that pulls cash back below the call wall (7360) and toward the put wall (7350) creates a potential pinning zone. ## 5. Strategy Implications: Negative Gamma, Light Calendar, Geopolitical Overhang

With the regime **uniformly negative** and no structural flip level in range, Monday's session is set up for **directional amplification in whichever direction cash confirms**. The light scheduled calendar removes a macro dampener, leaving tape action and geopolitical flows as the primary drivers.

**Applicable structures:**

**Directional Debit Spreads** remain the appropriate vehicle in a negative gamma environment. Dealers are short options and will hedge by buying into rallies and selling into weakness — amplifying momentum. Credit structures that rely on mean-reversion and range-bound behavior (iron condors, short strangles) are **misaligned with this regime** and should be avoided or sized very conservatively given the absence of any gamma flip to constrain a directional break.

- **If fading the gap or hedging geopolitical downside:** A put debit spread using S1 (7317) and S2 (7280) as targets is structurally supported — a break of the 7350 put wall in negative gamma carries no cushion.
- **Avoid naked short volatility into NFP week.** Even with today's VIX decline, carrying uncapped short vol positions into a payrolls week in a negative gamma tape is asymmetrically dangerous.

## Bottom Line

Today's session was a sector rotation story inside a technically challenging negative gamma tape. The morning trend forecast didn't materialize because semi-specific selling and Healthcare/Consumer rotation created offsetting flows that pinned the index. The regime remains negative and uniformly so — no flip, no cushion, no mean-reversion support from dealer positioning.

Monday's primary risk is the **gap open at R1 into a geopolitical headline**, in a regime where any directional move gets amplified, not dampened. The NFP overhang building through the week adds a vol premium that should keep premium buyers modestly favored over sellers heading into the holiday-shortened stretch.

**Manage size. Know your levels. The tape has no shock absorbers.**

# Evening Derivatives Recap
### Monday, June 29, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## LEAD STORY: Regime Flip — Negative to Positive at the Close

The structural headline tonight is a confirmed **regime shift**. The morning opened in **negative gamma** and closed in **positive gamma** — a meaningful transition that reframes the dealer hedging dynamic heading into Tuesday's session. This flip is the primary story, and traders should reset their structural assumptions accordingly.

---

## 1. Session Flow

Today's move was macro-driven at its core. Two discrete catalysts broke the five-session losing streak in risk assets: **easing Middle East tensions** as US-Iran hostilities de-escalated over the weekend, and the **Supreme Court's dismissal** of the Trump administration's attempt to remove Fed Governor Lisa Cook — a ruling that preserved near-term Fed independence and removed a significant tail risk for rates markets. Both catalysts hit a market that was already technically oversold and positioned defensively, creating the conditions for a sharp unwind.

The technical setup amplified the macro impulse. After five consecutive down sessions, crowded short positioning in technology and quarter-end rebalancing flows added fuel to the reversal. The result was a **strong trend day** — SPX +1.18%, session range 1.30%, with the low of 7348.88 established early and directional follow-through to the session high of 7444.32. The day type forecast of **TREND (UP) at 75% confidence** was validated precisely by these dynamics.

---

## 2. Regime Validation

The morning's **negative gamma** call was structurally correct and operationally relevant. In negative gamma environments, dealers are **short options** and hedge by **buying into rallies and selling into dips** — behavior that amplifies directional moves in whichever direction the market breaks. Today's session followed that script exactly: once the macro catalysts cleared early hesitation and price broke above the early range, dealer hedging flows reinforced the upside momentum rather than dampening it. The strong trend character — tight early low, progressive extension toward the close — is the signature of negative gamma in action.

What changed by the close was the **magnitude of the rally itself**. SPX's 1.18% gain and the associated VIX compression pushed the market into call wall territory (7450) and rebuilt enough net dealer positioning to flip the closing GEX reading to **positive gamma**. The regime did not flip because the thesis was wrong — it flipped because the move consumed the negative gamma inventory. That is a healthy and interpretable transition.

---

## 3. VIX / Gamma Interplay

VIX confirmed the session's directional character cleanly. The **4.13% decline to 17.65** is consistent with both the macro relief (geopolitical de-escalation + Fed independence preserved) and the structural shift from negative to positive gamma. In negative gamma regimes, dealer short-gamma hedging can exacerbate volatility on both sides; the fact that VIX compressed as aggressively as it did alongside a 1.18% SPX rally suggests the relief trade was genuine rather than a mechanically driven short squeeze.

Rates provided no friction — the 10Y yield held at **4.38%** with zero basis point movement, preserving the equity discount rate narrative. The 2Y-10Y spread at +28bps reflects a normal curve, offering no systemic signals to complicate the read.

Sector dispersion at **3.6%** is worth flagging. Technology (+2.4%) and Consumer Cyclical (+1.9%) leading while Consumer Defensive (-0.6%) and Basic Materials (-1.2%) lagged is a classic risk-on rotation, not a broad-based advance. When sector dispersion is this wide, the index headline number flatters the underlying breadth. The advance was real, but concentrated.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup

**Closing regime:** POSITIVE GAMMA | Call Wall: 7450 | Put Wall: 7400 | No in-range flip level

The structural backdrop for Tuesday shifts materially from today. With dealers now **long gamma** at the close, their hedging behavior reverses: they will **sell into strength above 7450 and buy weakness toward 7400**, creating a gravitational pull toward the range midpoint. The prior session's trend-following dynamic is no longer structurally supported in the same way.

**Key levels to watch:**

- **7450 (Call Wall):** SPX closed at 7440.43, printing essentially at the call wall. This level now acts as near-term resistance. ES futures at 7497.75 imply roughly a **0.8% gap up** at the open, which would place cash SPX near **7500** — above R1 (7478) and approaching R2 (7515). However, the gap does not guarantee follow-through; positive gamma dealer behavior above the call wall could work against gap continuation.

- **7478 (R1) / 7515 (R2):** These are the resistance reference points if the gap holds. Given the expected move of ±83 points, the upside range extends to approximately **7523** from Friday's close, which aligns with R2. Momentum traders watching overnight should be aware that positive gamma above 7450 introduces mean-reversion pressure.

- **7400 (Put Wall) / 7403 (S1):** These are the structural support cluster. In positive gamma, any intraday pullback toward this zone should attract dealer buying, reinforcing the floor.

**Catalyst load tomorrow:**
The morning session (9:00 ET) brings the **S&P/Case-Shiller Home Price data (April)** and the **FHFA House Price Index** — medium-to-low impact individually, unlikely to shift index direction absent a significant surprise. However, the **risk outlook is explicitly elevated** due to **JOLTs Job Openings (May)** — a labor market read that carries meaningful implications for the Fed's rate path. Position sizing should reflect that a JOLTs surprise in either direction could challenge the positive gamma containment, particularly if the print spooks rates.

Looking further ahead, **Nonfarm Payrolls (June)** later this week represents the highest single-event risk on the calendar. Traders should be cautious about adding directional risk early in the week that would require unwinding ahead of Friday.

**SPY RSI at 44 (neutral)** and price sitting +0.9% above the 50-SMA suggest the technical picture is neither overbought nor providing strong contrarian signal. The 200-SMA at +7.3% below current prices confirms the longer-term uptrend remains intact.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

With the closing regime now **positive gamma**, the dealer hedging dynamic shifts to dampening: **dealers are long options and will sell strength / buy weakness** to maintain delta neutrality. This creates a range-bound, mean-reverting environment — the structural opposite of today's trend session.

**Structures that align with this regime:**

- **Iron condors or credit spreads** centered on the 7400–7450 range are the natural fit. The put wall at 7400 and call wall at 7450 define the mechanical boundaries within which dealer flows are working in your favor.

- Given the overnight ES gap (~7498), **call spreads with short strikes above 7515 (R2)** or **put spreads with short strikes near 7366 (S2)** offer defined-risk structures that respect the expected move (±83 points) and lean on positive gamma containment.

- **Avoid naked or undefined-risk directional structures** unless a catalyst (JOLTs, Thursday/Friday payrolls) forces a regime re-evaluation. The positive gamma environment punishes premium buyers who are fighting the mean-reversion dynamic.

- **If JOLTs prints materially hot or cold tomorrow**, re-evaluate whether the positive gamma regime can absorb the rates reaction. A significant upside JOLTs miss triggering a rates rally could erode GEX support and bring the regime back toward neutral — at which point directional premium structures become more defensible.

The week's elevated risk posture (Payrolls Friday) argues for **keeping net theta exposure moderate** and maintaining defined risk on both sides. Size down relative to a clean, low-catalyst week.
