# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Tuesday, June 30, 2026 | Quarter-End Close

---

## Session Flow: Macro-Driven Trend on Quarter-End Catalysts

Today's session was defined by macro momentum rather than technical gamma mechanics or earnings flow. The primary catalyst was geopolitical — President Trump's announcement of a U.S.-Iran diplomatic meeting in Qatar eased a layer of tail-risk premium that had been sitting in the vol surface heading into quarter-end. That headline, layered onto sustained AI/tech spending optimism, provided the fundamental justification for a broad risk-on close to Q2.

The SPX printed a **+0.80% gain** (7438.04 → 7508.29 range, 0.94% session width), with **Technology +3.3%** and **Industrials +2.0%** leading. Healthcare and Real Estate lagged materially at **-1.1%** and **-1.4%** respectively — a sector dispersion reading of **4.7%** that confirms today's move was rotational and thematic, not a broad-based risk-lift. The equal-weight S&P 500 reaching a new all-time high does suggest breadth expansion, but the sector spread makes clear that capital was actively repositioning into growth/cyclical and out of defensives, not simply levitating the tape.

The session character was a **moderate trend** — not the high-volatility, two-sided action the morning forecast anticipated with 70% confidence. The actual print was directional but contained, with the session range of 0.94% well within the expected move framework. Worth noting: quarter-end flows, window dressing, and rebalancing tend to mechanically smooth intraday turbulence even when macro catalysts are live, which likely contributed to the orderly trend character rather than the high-vol whipsaw the model had flagged.

---

## Regime Validation: Positive Gamma Held — And Showed

The morning's **positive gamma** call was confirmed and, in fact, was the structural backstop that kept today's rally orderly. With dealers net long options heading into the session, their hedging behavior ran counter to the move: as SPX pushed higher, dealers were systematically **selling into the rally** to rebalance delta, which acted as a natural ceiling on the pace of the advance. That same mechanic provided a bid on any dip attempt — the session low of **7438.04** found support cleanly, and the tape recovered without extended selling pressure.

This is precisely the mean-reverting, range-dampening character that positive gamma environments produce. The session did not fragment or trend violently — it drifted upward with measured cadence. The **call wall at 7540** capped the intraday high at **7508**, providing clean resistance that held without a test of the upper bound. The **put wall at 7450** served as the support anchor, and the session low of 7438 briefly undercut it before buyers absorbed. That's the regime working as designed.

The forecast contradiction — HIGH VOLATILITY called, TREND delivered — is largely a function of quarter-end mechanics compressing realized vol within a directional tape. The gamma read was correct; the vol amplitude estimate was off.

---

## VIX / Gamma Interplay: Clean Confirmation

**VIX closed at 16.45, down -6.80%** on the session. This is a textbook confirmation of the SPX directional move — vol compressed as equities rallied, with no divergence signal. In a positive gamma environment, dealer flows inherently dampen realized volatility, and today's VIX decline is consistent with that dynamic. There was no vol-bid creeping in against the rally, no term structure inversion worth flagging, and no hidden hedging demand suggesting the market was fading its own strength.

Rates deserve a mention: the **10Y yield moved +6bp to 4.44%**, with the 2Y-10Y spread holding at **+30bp**. A steeper curve on a risk-on day is consistent with growth optimism pricing, not a stress signal. Equity vol and rate vol are moving in the same directional narrative today.

The **SPY RSI at 55** keeps the tape in neutral territory — not overbought, not extended on a momentum basis. Price sitting **+1.6% above the 50-SMA** and **+8.1% above the 200-SMA** reflects a healthy trend structure without the kind of overextension that typically precedes mechanical vol mean-reversion.

---

## Tomorrow's Setup: Positive Gamma Baseline, Labor Data the Pivot

**Closing gamma regime: POSITIVE.** The settled structural read heading into Wednesday is that dealers remain net long options, with the hedging mechanic continuing to favor a dampened, range-contained tape. There is no in-range GEX flip level — the cumulative GEX profile remains uniformly positive across the observable chain. This is an important detail: there is no identifiable level at which dealer behavior flips from dampening to amplifying. Regime transitions, if they occur, would require a discrete repricing event, not a gradual drift through a flip point.

**Key levels for Wednesday:**
- **Call Wall / R1 Zone: 7537–7540** — This band is the primary upside cap. R1 sits at 7537, the call wall at 7540. ES futures are currently trading at **7546.5**, implying a **+0.6% overnight gap** above this zone. That gap is the first structural question of the overnight session. If cash opens above 7540, the call wall becomes a test of whether the positive gamma regime can continue to suppress the advance, or whether a breakout toward R2 at **7574** develops.
- **Put Wall / S1 Zone: 7450–7462** — S1 at 7462, put wall at 7450. This is the critical downside anchor. As long as SPX holds above 7450 on any pullback, the positive gamma regime is intact and dealer flows provide a structural bid. A sustained break below 7450 would be the first meaningful signal that the regime is softening.
- **Expected Move: ±78 points** — This frames the full day range as roughly **7421–7577** from the close. The put wall at 7450 sits inside the lower bound, reinforcing its relevance as the key support line rather than a tail event level.

**Catalysts:**
- **ADP Employment Change (Jun), 08:15 ET** — Medium impact. This is the primary Wednesday morning risk event. A materially hot or cold read will set the pre-open tone for Thursday's NFP framing. Consensus expectations matter here — a significant beat could push yields higher and test whether equity vol reprices ahead of the full labor report.
- **ISM Manufacturing PMI + Prices + Employment (Jun), 10:00 ET** — Three simultaneous manufacturing reads. The prices component carries the most cross-asset implication given the current rate and inflation narrative. A hot prices print on top of a strong ADP could steepen the yield curve further and introduce some intraday vol that the positive gamma regime would otherwise suppress.
- **Challenger Job Cuts, 05:30 ET** — Low impact, pre-open. Useful for directional framing but unlikely to move markets meaningfully.

**Overnight context:** The ES gap to 7546.5 places the futures print squarely above the call wall. In a positive gamma regime, this is not inherently a breakout signal — dealers selling into a gap-up open is precisely the expected behavior. Watch the first 30 minutes of cash trade to assess whether the gap fills back toward 7510–7520 (mean-reversion, regime confirming) or whether volume and macro catalysts sustain a hold above 7540 (potential call wall breach, watch R2 at 7574).

**Week-level risk:** Thursday's **Nonfarm Payrolls and Unemployment Rate** for June are the tail-risk event of the week. Position sizing should reflect that Wednesday's data represents a preview, not a resolution. The week-level risk outlook is elevated, and any significant ADP/ISM surprise Wednesday could trigger pre-positioning flows that introduce volatility ahead of the cash report.

---

## Strategy Implications: Positive Gamma Favors Income, But Mind the Labor Sequence

With the **closing regime confirmed positive** and no in-range flip level, Wednesday's structural default remains range-bound and mean-reverting. Dealer hedging behavior — selling strength, buying weakness — provides a mechanical buffer against extended directional moves. That environment is structurally favorable for **income-oriented structures** that benefit from vol compression and range containment: **iron condors, credit spreads, and short premium structures** with defined risk parameters are the regime fit.

However, the catalyst sequence demands respect. Wednesday carries medium-impact labor data in ADP and the ISM complex, and Thursday brings the full NFP release. This is not a week to deploy wide, static income structures and walk away. The practical approach:

- **Intraday / short-dated structures:** The 7450–7540 band is the natural range anchor for Wednesday. Credit spreads positioned within that band, with defined downside at or below the put wall and defined upside at or below the call wall, align with positive gamma mechanics.
- **If gap holds above 7540 at the open:** The call wall becomes dynamic resistance. The regime does not break simply because price touches the wall, but sustained hold above it with volume would warrant caution on short-call exposure and may favor tightening upside strikes toward 7537.
- **Avoid undefined risk heading into Thursday's NFP:** Regardless of Wednesday's regime character, the week-level elevated risk outlook argues strongly against naked or uncapped structures. Defined-risk on both sides is the appropriate posture.
- **Vol surface note:** VIX at 16.45 is not elevated in absolute terms, but Thursday's NFP should be carrying an event premium in the near-term term structure. If Wednesday's data prints benign and the tape drifts, any vol compression into Wednesday's close would make front-month premium attractive from a buyer's perspective ahead of NFP — a potential setup for defined-risk directional positioning into Thursday.

---

**Bottom line:** Q2 ended on a clean macro-driven bid, positive gamma confirmed, and vol compressed. Wednesday's setup is structurally range-friendly, but the labor data sequence — ADP Wednesday, NFP Thursday — means the regime's dampening effect will be tested. Respect the 7450–7540 band, watch the gap resolution at the open, and keep structures defined heading into the full jobs report.

*Good evening.*

## Session Flow

Today's session played out largely as a rotational, technically-driven grind rather than a macro or earnings-led directional move. SPX settled at **7,483.23 (-0.22%)** with a session range of **0.97%** (high: 7,521.81 / low: 7,449.63) — a profile consistent with the morning's MIXED/CHOP call, which printed as RANGE BOUND in practice.

The primary driver of today's character was **sector rotation**, not an index-level catalyst. Financial Services (+1.3%) and Communication Services (+1.0%) led, while Technology (-2.1%) and Industrials (-2.3%) were the clear laggards. The **3.6% sector dispersion** is notably wide and signals capital reallocation within equities rather than a broad risk-on or risk-off posture — this is not a tape that's responding to a single macro theme, it's a tape repositioning ahead of tomorrow's payroll print.

The Fed's Kevin Warsh was in the news dodging questions on rates while emphasizing AI's impact on labor markets — headline noise that added uncertainty without providing actionable guidance. Net effect: equities drifted modestly lower as the market refuses to extend without clarity.

---

## Regime Validation

The morning's **positive gamma regime call held.** The 0.97% range and mean-reverting character — the session tested both the 7,521 print near the call wall and the 7,449 print near the put wall — is textbook dealer behavior under a positive gamma environment. Dealers, being **long gamma**, sold into intraday strength and bought into weakness, compressing the range and dampening follow-through in either direction.

That said, a critical caveat is warranted: **spot closed at 7,483.23, only 16.8 points below the call wall at 7,500.** The regime is **borderline, not firmly positive.** The session ended within the positive regime, but the proximity to the structural boundary means the regime's stabilizing influence is thinner than normal. Do not treat this as a robust cushion.

---

## VIX / Gamma Interplay

VIX closed at **16.59 (+0.86%)**, a mild uptick on a down session — directionally consistent but not alarmist. The move in VIX is proportional to the SPX decline and does not signal a volatility regime change. More importantly, the VIX move is occurring against a backdrop where dealers remain net long options (positive gamma), meaning the hedging flows continue to work against sustained momentum in either direction.

The tech selling is worth watching from a vol surface perspective — Technology -2.1% with dealer flows absorbing index-level dislocations creates a bifurcated tape where single-name vol can diverge sharply from index vol. That dispersion is not fully captured in a VIX reading near 16.

---

## Tomorrow Setup

**Non-Farm Payrolls (June) prints at 08:30 ET.** This is the dominant event risk for the session and, given where spot sits, the structural implications are significant.

**Key levels:**
- **Call Wall: 7,500** — This is the ceiling that rejected the session high (7,521.81 tested, failed to hold). A strong NFP print could drive price back through this level. - **R1: 7,521 / R2: 7,558** — Natural targets on an upside surprise.
- **Put Wall: 7,450 / S1: 7,446** — The structural floor. Today's low of 7,449.63 nearly tagged this level precisely. A weak payrolls print risks a clean break below 7,450, removing the put wall as a support anchor.
- **S2: 7,408** — The next meaningful support if 7,450 gives way.
- **Expected Move: ±78 points** — This places the market's implied range at approximately **7,405 to 7,561**, which brackets all key levels and confirms that tomorrow's print has the range to test every meaningful structural reference point.

**Overnight context:** ES futures are trading at **7,534.5**, implying a **0.7% gap higher** relative to the 7,483.23 cash close. This gap sits squarely above the call wall at 7,500. The gap should be treated as unconfirmed until the cash open — gaps of this magnitude into key gamma levels ahead of high-impact catalysts frequently fade or fill as position-squaring precedes the number.

**Regime sensitivity:** Given the borderline positive regime and the proximity of ES futures to the call wall, **the opening print in cash relative to 7,500 will be the most important early signal.** If cash opens above 7,500 and holds, watch for a potential regime shift dynamic intraday. If it fails to hold, the positive gamma stabilization mechanism reasserts and contains the range.

## Strategy Implications

**Catalyst load tomorrow is elevated.** NFP is a high-impact, binary-outcome event. This creates a specific strategic framework:

**Positioning note ahead of NFP:** The wide sector dispersion today, combined with VIX at 16.59 and a borderline gamma regime, argues for **reduced gross exposure heading into 08:30 ET.** Structures with defined, limited risk are appropriate. Undefined-risk short vol positions are poorly suited to a high-impact data event at a gamma inflection point.

**FOMC Minutes** are also on the calendar this week — an additional vol event that argues against aggressive premium decay strategies as the tail of the week approaches.

### Thursday, July 02, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## Regime Alert: Morning Call Contradicted — Regime Closes Negative

The lead structural story tonight is a **regime shift**. This morning's call was **positive gamma** with a HIGH VOLATILITY day type at 70% confidence. Neither held. The session closed in **negative gamma** with a range-bound character — a direct contradiction that warrants a clean-slate read into Friday's session.

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro-Driven Rotation, Not Gamma-Driven Trend

Today's primary driver was the **soft June jobs report**, which triggered a sharp rotational response rather than a directional SPX move. The index closed essentially flat at **7483.24 (+0.00%)** despite a 1.52% intraday range (High: 7540.75 / Low: 7427.55) — a wide swing that resolved into nothing on a closing basis.

The mechanics underneath the surface were anything but calm. Technology and semiconductors absorbed severe selling pressure — chip stocks were off sharply, with the SOX down 5.4% — while Healthcare (+2.1%) and Consumer Defensive (+1.8%) absorbed the rotation. The soft payroll data cooled near-term rate-hike expectations, pulling defensives and large-cap industrials higher while punishing rate-sensitive growth. The result: a flat SPX print masking **5.1% sector dispersion**, confirming this was a rotational, not broad-based, move.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Morning Call Contradicted

The morning framework anticipated **positive gamma** — a regime in which dealers are long options and hedge by selling rallies and buying dips, naturally compressing intraday moves. The day type was flagged HIGH VOLATILITY at 70% confidence, which sits in tension with that regime call on its own, but the closing outcome resolves the contradiction clearly: **the session did not confirm positive gamma mechanics**.

What actually happened: the 1.52% range with a full retracement to flat is superficially consistent with range-bound behavior, but the path — from 7427.55 to 7540.75 and back — shows intraday momentum and sector stress that is more characteristic of a **negative gamma environment**, where dealer hedging amplifies rather than dampens moves. The jobs report was the catalyst that exposed the true regime; the soft labor data generated a sharp risk-off impulse in tech that dealers in a short-gamma posture had to chase, contributing to the amplified intraday swing before the session settled.

**Closing regime: NEGATIVE gamma.** This is the structural baseline heading into Friday.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: VIX Partially Confirms, Dealer Flows Complicate

VIX closed at **16.15 (-2.66%)**, a meaningful decline on a day where SPX printed flat. On the surface, a falling VIX alongside a flat index is not obviously negative-gamma behavior — vol compression typically accompanies dealer long-gamma hedging. However, the context matters: the VIX decline likely reflects the **reduction in tail risk** following the jobs print (no rate-hike panic, no hard landing signal), not a structural vol-selling bid from dealers in a long-gamma posture.

The intraday amplitude — a 113-point SPX range that fully round-tripped — is more consistent with dealers in a **short-gamma position amplifying flows** as the market whipsawed on the labor data. Sector dispersion of 5.1% with sharp tech underperformance reinforces that today's vol was directional and rotational, not dampened and mean-reverting. The VIX print is an imperfect signal here; the intraday structure tells the more honest story.

10Y yields ticked up 1bp to **4.49%** with the 2Y-10Y spread at +35bp — a normal curve that does not introduce additional systemic stress. Rates were not the amplifier today.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Negative Gamma Into a Holiday-Shortened Close

**Closing regime: NEGATIVE gamma**
**Call Wall: 7485.0 | Put Wall: 7450.0 | Expected Move: ±76.0**
**No in-range GEX flip level — regime is uniformly negative across the chain.**

There is no GEX flip level within the observable chain, which means there is no structural pivot where the regime becomes self-stabilizing. This is a fully committed negative gamma environment heading into Friday — the pre-holiday July 4th session.

ES futures are trading at **7537.25**, implying a **+0.7% overnight gap** above the SPX close at 7483.24. Critically, this puts ES **above the call wall at 7485** and approaching R1 at **7521** and R2 at **7558**. In a negative gamma regime, dealers are **short options** and must hedge by **buying into rallies and selling into declines** — meaning any opening strength above the call wall risks being amplified, not faded. This is not a regime that naturally caps upside at resistance.

**Key levels to watch:**
- **7485 (Call Wall):** The overnight gap already clears this level in futures. Whether cash confirms matters. A hold above 7485 in the cash open keeps the directional pressure bullish and dealer flows pro-trend.
- **7521 (R1):** First meaningful resistance on a confirmed gap-hold. In negative gamma, a break above R1 is not capped by dealer selling — momentum structures become relevant.
- **7558 (R2):** Upper bound of the expected move. A reach toward R2 in a negative gamma session would carry momentum, not mean-reversion.
- **7450 (Put Wall / S1 zone):** The critical downside anchor. A failure of the gap and a reversal below 7485 toward 7450 would be the negative gamma tail risk — dealers short puts would amplify selling through this level.
- **7408 (S2):** Extended downside scenario. Low probability in a low-catalyst session, but without a flip level, there is no structural floor between 7450 and technicals alone.

**The week-level risk is ELEVATED** with FOMC Minutes on the horizon — but that is a next-week positioning story, not a Friday driver.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Negative Gamma, Light Catalysts, Pre-Holiday Tape

In a **negative gamma regime**, dealers are short options and hedge by buying strength and selling weakness — flows that **amplify directional moves**. This is the environment where income structures bleed and directional structures earn.

**Structures that align with the regime:**

- **Debit call spreads (7485/7521 or 7521/7558):** If the overnight gap holds on the cash open and price sustains above the call wall, the momentum bias is upward with dealer flows providing a tailwind rather than a headwind. Defined-risk directional long exposure is appropriate.
- **Directional verticals with defined risk:** Given the wide session range potential (±76 expected move) and amplification risk in negative gamma, uncapped directional exposure is not warranted. Keep risk defined.
- **Avoid short premium / income structures:** Iron condors and credit spreads are structurally misaligned. In a negative gamma session, the dampening mechanics that income structures require are absent. Dealers are not selling rallies or buying dips — they are adding to moves.

**Key risk to the bullish gap thesis:** Pre-holiday thinning cuts both ways. If the gap fails at the open and price retreats below 7485 toward 7450, negative gamma mechanics flip the amplification to the downside. Stops matter more than usual in this regime.

**Position sizing note:** With FOMC Minutes on the weekly horizon and the regime uniformly negative with no structural flip point, carry risk into the long weekend with awareness that unhedged directional exposure faces an amplified return distribution in either direction.

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Monday, July 7, 2026 | Post-Close Edition

---

## Regime Shift: The Lead Story Tonight

The most important structural development from today's session is a **regime flip from negative to positive gamma**. The morning opened in a negative gamma environment — where dealers are short options and hedge by buying rallies and selling dips, amplifying directional moves. By the close, the regime has rotated to **positive gamma**, with dealers now long options, selling into strength and buying weakness to stay hedged. Tomorrow's session character should shift meaningfully toward **range compression and mean-reversion** unless a catalyst disrupts the structure.

This transition is the primary framing lens for Tuesday.

---

## 1. Session Flow

Today's move was fundamentally **macro-driven**, with the primary catalyst being softer June labor data that reinforced expectations for a Fed pause. The SPX added **+0.73%**, closing at **7,537.43** on a session range of just **0.67%** (High: 7,551.31 / Low: 7,500.97). The tight range is notable — despite a trending outcome, the market moved in a controlled, non-explosive fashion.

For narrative context, the news feed referenced strong leadership in Communication Services and Financials alongside broad risk-on sentiment. **Our source of truth for sector rotation is the closing data: Financial Services (+0.6%) and Technology (+0.6%) led, while Healthcare (-0.5%) and Real Estate (-1.0%) lagged.** The defensive sectors underperforming in a soft-landing macro narrative is internally consistent. The rates backdrop supported the move — the 10-year yield dipped 1bp to 4.48%, keeping the curve constructively normal at a +35bp 2s/10s spread.

---

## 2. Regime Validation

The morning called **NEGATIVE gamma, TREND UP at 75% confidence**. That call was validated. In a negative gamma regime, dealers are short options and must **buy as prices rise and sell as prices fall** to maintain delta neutrality — this mechanically amplifies trending moves. Today's session delivered exactly that: a moderate upside trend from the 7,500 low to the 7,551 high, with the macro catalyst providing the initial directional impulse and dealer hedging activity amplifying follow-through to the upside.

The critical development is what happened **at the close**: the regime flipped to **positive gamma**. The market's move higher — specifically the close above 7,537 — appears to have shifted the net dealer positioning to long options. This is the defining structural pivot heading into Tuesday.

---

## 3. VIX / Gamma Interplay

VIX closed at **15.57, -1.52%**, confirming the directional SPX move. A falling VIX alongside a rising SPX is the expected co-movement in a risk-on environment — short volatility positioning is consistent with dealers absorbing premium and the market trending into a more stable structure. The VIX print at 15.57 is not elevated; it sits in a zone consistent with a positive gamma regime and low realized volatility. SPY RSI at 58 is neutral and non-extended, leaving room for either continuation or consolidation without a technical exhaustion signal. Price sitting **+1.9% above the 50-SMA and +8.5% above the 200-SMA** reflects a well-supported trend structure, though it also means dip buyers have a meaningful cushion beneath them.

---

## 4. Tomorrow Setup

**The closing positive gamma regime is the settled structural baseline.** ES futures are showing a **+0.7% gap to 7,591.75** versus the SPX close at 7,537.43 — a notable overnight bid. However, gaps frequently fade at the open, and no assumptions should be made about its persistence until the cash market confirms.

**Key levels for Tuesday:**
- **Call Wall: 7,550** — This was today's session high (7,551.31). In a positive gamma regime, this level acts as a gravitational ceiling. Dealer hedging (selling rallies) intensifies as SPX approaches this wall. A clean break and hold above 7,550 would be structurally significant and require reassessment.
- **R1: 7,575 / R2: 7,613** — If the overnight gap holds and SPX opens above 7,550, R1 at 7,575 is the first meaningful resistance pivot. R2 at 7,613 sits near the upper bound of tomorrow's expected move (+74 points from close = ~7,611).
- **Put Wall: 7,500 / S1: 7,500** — These levels converge, making 7,500 the critical downside anchor. In a positive gamma regime, dealer buying increases near the put wall, providing a mechanical cushion. This is tomorrow's defining support zone.
- **S2: 7,462** — A close below 7,500 would expose this level and would likely signal a regime re-evaluation.

**Overnight/opening framing:** The gap open above 7,550 places the market immediately at the call wall at the cash open. Positive gamma dynamics would suggest initial selling pressure against that level. If the 8:30 ET trade data (Exports, Imports, Trade Balance) surprises materially in either direction, that could be the catalyst to either extend through 7,575 or fade back toward 7,500. The Expected Move of ±74 points brackets the session between approximately 7,463 and 7,611 — well-aligned with S2 and R2.

**Week-level risk:** FOMC Minutes are on the calendar this week. Position sizing should reflect that elevated weekly risk designation — the minutes could reprice the Fed pause narrative that drove today's rally.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

With the regime now **positive gamma** at the close, the structural setup for Tuesday favors **range-bound, mean-reverting price action** within the 7,500–7,550 corridor. Dealers are long options and will mechanically dampen moves by selling into strength above 7,550 and buying weakness toward 7,500.

**Structures that align with this regime:**

- **Iron Condors / Defined-Range Credit Spreads:** The natural positive gamma play. The 7,500 put wall and 7,550 call wall bracket a well-defined range. Premium sellers benefit from dealers actively compressing moves within this zone. With VIX at 15.57 and IV not elevated, credit is thin — be selective on strikes and ensure defined risk.
- **Call Credit Spreads (if opening gap fades):** An open above 7,550 that stalls immediately is a textbook positive gamma fade setup. A 7,575/7,600 call credit spread captures the R1-to-R2 zone with the regime working in your favor.
- **Avoid naked directional delta:** The positive gamma environment is hostile to unhedged directional premium buyers. If you have conviction on a catalyst-driven breakout through 7,575, use a defined-risk debit vertical rather than naked calls to control for the dampening regime.

**Caveat for elevated catalyst risk:** The 8:30 ET trade data cluster (three prints simultaneously) and the 10:00 ET Economic Optimism Index carry medium impact. A significant surprise — particularly a trade deficit widening that reignites tariff/macro concerns — could temporarily override positive gamma dynamics. If SPX breaks below 7,500 on volume, reassess regime assumptions immediately, as that level is both the put wall and S1.

The week-level risk from FOMC Minutes argues for **reduced position sizing** and preference for structures with clearly defined maximum loss.

---

*Risk is managed, not predicted. Size accordingly.*

*— Derivatives Strategy Desk*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Tuesday, July 8, 2026 | Published After Close

---

## LEAD STORY: Regime Flip — Positive to Negative at the Close

The most significant structural development of today's session is not the 0.45% SPX decline — it is the **regime flip from positive to negative gamma at the close**. This is the primary story heading into Wednesday. The dampening mechanics that characterized the morning's framework have given way to an amplifying regime: dealers, now net short options, will **buy into rallies and sell into dips** to manage their hedge exposure. Tomorrow's session structure changes materially as a result.

---

## 1. Session Flow

Today's move was macro and earnings-sentiment driven, not a product of technical gamma flows. Two catalysts dominated the narrative:

1. **Geopolitical escalation in the Strait of Hormuz** — U.S. revocation of Iran's crude oil license introduced an energy supply-shock premium, creating cross-asset uncertainty and suppressing risk appetite.
2. **Semiconductor sentiment reversal** — Despite Samsung's optimistic AI-driven forecast, the market read the print as a sell-the-news event, triggering broad chip-sector liquidation and dragging the Nasdaq down 1.16%.

The result was a **defensive rotation**: Real Estate (+1.7%) and Energy (+1.0%) captured flows, while Industrials (-2.5%) and Basic Materials (-1.4%) bore the brunt of the selling pressure. Note that sector market data confirms Energy as a *net beneficiary on the day* — the geopolitical premium lifted crude-adjacent names even as broader energy sentiment remained complex. The 4.1% dispersion between sector leaders and laggards signals this was **rotational, not broadly risk-off** — a meaningful distinction when reading dealer hedging behavior.

---

## 2. Regime Validation

**The morning call was POSITIVE gamma, with a MIXED/CHOP day type at 55% confidence.** The session did deliver a mixed outcome — SPX oscillated across a 0.77% intraday range (7478.63 low to 7536.06 high), ultimately settling down 0.45%. That range behavior is broadly consistent with positive gamma's dampening mechanics being operative *during* the session, with dealers absorbing selling pressure and preventing a clean directional trend.

However, the critical development came **at the close**: the regime flipped to **negative gamma**. The macro catalyst combination — geopolitical risk premium, semiconductor-led tech weakness, and a VIX print of 16.13 (+3.60%) — eroded the options structure sufficiently to shift dealer positioning from net long to net short gamma. The morning's chop call was validated intraday; but the *closing regime* is what governs tomorrow's structural dynamics. That is now a different environment.

---

## 3. VIX / Gamma Interplay

VIX at 16.13, +3.60% on the day, confirmed the directional character of the session: risk aversion rising, even if the SPX decline was modest in absolute terms. The correlation held — SPX down, VIX up — which is the expected behavior when macro uncertainty (Hormuz, AI-trade skepticism) is the primary driver rather than a technical squeeze or liquidity event.

The 10-year yield rising 7 basis points to 4.55% added a rates overlay that pressured rate-sensitive growth names and supported the defensive rotation into Real Estate (counterintuitively benefiting from flight-to-quality flows in REITs) and Consumer Staples. The 2Y-10Y spread at +36bp (normal curve) indicates no imminent recessionary pricing, but the rates move does reflect re-pricing of macro risk — consistent with the geopolitical narrative.

Dealer flows were a factor at the margin: with the morning's positive gamma regime still in place through most of the session, the 7478.63 low likely found support from dealers buying the dip to hedge. That mechanical bid may have helped contain the session low. The regime flip at the close suggests that support mechanism is now removed.

---

## 4. Tomorrow Setup

**Closing Regime: NEGATIVE GAMMA**
**Call Wall: 7525 | Put Wall: 7475 | Expected Move: ±76 points**
**No in-range GEX flip level exists — the regime is uniformly negative across the entire observable chain.**

With no flip level to anchor dealer behavior, the walls are the only structural guardrails. The 7525 call wall and 7475 put wall define a 50-point range around the 7500 handle — but in a negative gamma environment, these are **resistance and support zones, not magnets**. Dealers are short options and will **buy into strength and sell into weakness**, adding momentum to moves rather than fading them.

Key levels for Wednesday:
- **7525 (Call Wall / R1: 7541)**: This cluster is the first meaningful resistance overhead. A sustained push through 7525 — particularly if accompanied by the ES gap holding (futures currently +0.6% at 7549) — could accelerate toward R2 at 7579, with dealer covering adding fuel.
- **7475 (Put Wall / S1: 7466)**: Failure to hold 7475 in a negative gamma regime is structurally dangerous. Dealer short-gamma hedging would amplify selling toward S2 at 7429.
- **ES Futures Gap (7549 implied)**: The overnight gap of ~0.6% above the 7503.85 cash close is notable. Do not treat it as confirmed — cash market must validate. If the gap fades at open, the 7525 call wall becomes the first test of whether negative gamma amplification kicks in to the downside.

**Catalysts Tomorrow:** Wednesday's scheduled data are uniformly low-impact (Used Car Prices, Wholesale Sales, Crude Oil Imports, Thomson Reuters PCSI). However, the **FOMC Minutes** — flagged in the risk outlook as capable of moving markets — represent the key event risk. The minutes will be parsed for any shift in the Fed's reaction function relative to the tariff/inflation backdrop. In a negative gamma environment, a hawkish reading of the minutes has the potential to amplify significantly.

**This week's CPI (Core CPI YoY and CPI s.a. for June) is the dominant macro tail risk for the balance of the week.** Position sizing should reflect elevated weekly risk, not just tomorrow's low-impact data slate.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

**Negative gamma demands directional, defined-risk structures.** The income/premium-collection logic of iron condors and credit spreads is structurally misaligned with a regime where dealer hedging amplifies moves rather than dampens them. Selling premium into a uniformly negative GEX environment — with FOMC Minutes on deck and CPI later in the week — is a low-probability setup.

**Appropriate structures in this regime:**
- **Debit spreads / vertical spreads** in the direction of the anticipated move. If the ES gap holds and price clears 7525 with conviction, a call debit spread targeting 7525–7579 offers defined risk with exposure to dealer-amplified momentum.
- **Defined-risk directional puts** if 7475 breaks: a put debit spread targeting the 7475–7429 corridor aligns with the negative gamma amplification dynamic — dealers would be selling into weakness, not buying it.
- **Avoid naked or undefined-risk short premium** — the absence of any in-range GEX flip means there is no structural backstop to dealer-amplified moves beyond the walls.

**Volatility context:** VIX at 16.13 is not elevated historically, but it has room to expand quickly in a negative gamma regime with binary catalyst risk (FOMC Minutes, CPI). Owning convexity through inexpensive debit structures is preferable to harvesting premium into a regime that punishes static short-vol positions.

---

**Bottom line:** The session confirmed the morning's chop forecast intraday, but the regime flip at the close resets the structural framework. Tomorrow opens in negative gamma, no flip level in range, with FOMC Minutes as the primary catalyst. Dealer mechanics will amplify — not contain — directional moves. Respect the walls at 7525 and 7475, size for elevated weekly risk, and favor directional defined-risk over income structures until the regime shifts back positive.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy*

# Evening Derivatives Recap — Wednesday, July 8, 2026

---

## Session Flow: Geopolitical Shock, Muted Structural Follow-Through

Today's session was macro-driven. President Trump's declaration that the Iran ceasefire is over triggered an immediate risk-off response — crude oil surged with WTI gaining more than 7% and Brent settling near $78/bbl — yet the equity damage was notably contained. The SPX closed at **7,482.71 (-0.29%)** against an intraday range of just **0.90%**, with a session low of **7,421.82** that was quickly reclaimed. The Dow bore the brunt of the rotation (-1.1%), weighed by Financials and Industrials, while the Nasdaq eked out a fractional gain (+0.20%) supported by a Technology recovery. Sector dispersion of **3.4%** confirms this was a rotational, not broad-based, liquidation event.

**Sector leadership per market data:** Technology (+2.3%) and Utilities (+0.8%) led on the close; Healthcare (-1.1%) and Basic Materials (-1.1%) lagged. This diverges from the early-session narrative of Energy leading and Tech lagging — by the close, the market data is the source of truth. The intraday rotation back into Tech is notable given the geopolitical backdrop and suggests dip buyers were active in large-cap growth.

---

## Regime Validation: Forecast Contradicted — NEGATIVE Gamma Damped Rather Than Amplified

The morning regime call was **NEGATIVE gamma**, with a **HIGH VOLATILITY** day type forecast at 70% confidence. The actual session printed **RANGE BOUND**. That is a meaningful divergence and warrants explanation.

In a negative gamma environment, dealers are **short options** and hedge by **buying rallies and selling dips** — mechanics that typically amplify directional moves and sustain trending behavior. The conditions were present for follow-through: a hard geopolitical catalyst, elevated oil, and a risk-off tone at the open. Instead, the session absorbed the Iran shock with a 0.90% range and closed near mid-range.

The most likely explanation is **competing forces at the structural level**: while the gamma regime remained negative, the absence of a GEX flip level — with cumulative GEX uniformly negative across the entire observable chain — likely meant there was no structural inflection point to accelerate a breakdown. Without a flip level to cross, dealer hedging pressure lacked a trigger to cascade. Simultaneously, the intraday Tech reversal and dip-buying appetite in large-cap growth acted as a mechanical offset. The negative gamma regime was **present but ineffective** in producing its characteristic amplification today, a session that should be noted as a regime confirmation failure against a live macro shock.

---

## VIX/Gamma Interplay: VIX Confirmed the Anxiety, Not the Panic

VIX closed at **16.9 (+4.78%)**, a meaningful single-session spike that confirms the market priced genuine uncertainty around the Iran development. However, a VIX at 16.9 is not consistent with sustained directional breakdown — it is an acknowledgment of risk, not a fear regime. SPY RSI sits at **43**, neutral and not yet oversold, with price holding **+0.9% above the 50-SMA** and **+7.5% above the 200-SMA**. The technical structure remains intact despite today's headline shock.

The VIX rise confirms the negative gamma regime was live — dealer short-gamma positioning would have contributed to the spike — but the magnitude of the VIX move (still sub-17) relative to the geopolitical severity of the catalyst suggests macro participants are not pricing an escalation scenario with conviction. The regime and the volatility surface are aligned, but both remain in a "watchful" rather than "distressed" state.

---

## Tomorrow's Setup: Uniformly Negative Regime, Compressed Range Between Walls

The closing gamma regime is **NEGATIVE**, with no GEX flip level anywhere in the observable chain. This is the structural baseline entering Thursday. With ES futures trading at **7,521.0** — implying a **0.5% overnight gap up of approximately 38 points** versus the SPX close of 7,482.71 — the market is attempting to recapture the **call wall at 7,500** and push through it before the cash open.

Critical levels for Thursday:

- **Call Wall: 7,500** — This is the primary structural ceiling. In a negative gamma regime, dealers are short calls and will **buy into rallies above this level** to hedge their short gamma exposure, which can paradoxically create momentum continuation if the level breaks cleanly. Watch whether the open sustains above 7,500 or gets rejected back toward the put wall.
- **Put Wall: 7,475** — The downside anchor. A close below this level increases pressure toward **S1 at 7,445** and ultimately **S2 at 7,408**.
- **R1: 7,520 / R2: 7,558** — ES futures are already trading at R1 equivalent overnight. If the cash market opens at or above 7,520, R2 at 7,558 becomes the next test, and in a negative gamma environment, a sustained break of R1 can see price run toward R2 without meaningful mean-reversion support.
- **Expected Move: ±80 points** — Defines the one-standard-deviation range as approximately **7,403 to 7,563**.

**Catalyst load tomorrow is medium:** Initial Jobless Claims at 8:30 ET is the primary data event, with the 4-week average and continuing claims providing context. Fed Williams speaks at 9:00 ET — any commentary reinforcing a patient stance on cuts would likely support the overnight bid. Existing Home Sales at 10:00 ET is low impact.

The **weekly risk is elevated**: CPI and Inflation Rate YoY (June) are on the horizon later in the week. Position sizing should reflect that Thursday's session exists within a broader catalyst window — do not carry excess directional exposure into a CPI print in a negative gamma environment.

The overnight gap is constructive but unconfirmed. The Iran situation remains unresolved, crude oil is elevated, and a negative gamma regime means **any re-escalation in geopolitical news flow can produce momentum-driven downside** without the structural buffer a positive gamma regime would provide. The gap should not be treated as a resolved directional signal.

---

## Strategy Implications: Negative Gamma, Medium Catalyst Load — Directional Structures with Defined Risk

In the current negative gamma regime, dealer hedging **amplifies moves in the direction of the trend** — they buy strength and sell weakness, which means trending sessions can extend further than they would in a dampened environment. This is not the setup for income structures attempting to collect premium in a stable range. The appropriate tactical frame is **defined-risk directional**.

**If the open sustains above 7,500 (call wall):**
Bullish debit spreads or call verticals targeting R1 (7,520) and R2 (7,558) have structural logic — a clean recapture of the call wall in a negative gamma environment can run without natural mean-reversion resistance. Keep width manageable given the CPI risk later in the week.

**If the open fades below 7,475 (put wall):**
The downside path toward S1 (7,445) and S2 (7,408) opens structurally. Put verticals or bearish debit spreads with strikes anchored to those support levels are consistent with the regime. A failure to hold the put wall in a uniformly negative gamma environment is a higher-conviction signal than it would be in a mixed or transitional regime.

**Avoid undefined-risk short premium strategies** until the regime shifts positive or we approach a confirmed flip level. The uniform negative GEX profile with no in-range flip means there is no structural brake on a trending day — selling naked optionality here is structurally misaligned.

**Sizing note:** With CPI on the weekly calendar and the Iran situation as an active, unresolved macro overhang, keep gross notional below full allocation. Tomorrow's jobless claims print and Williams commentary are the near-term risk events, but the tail risk is priced too cheaply at VIX 16.9 given the geopolitical backdrop. The options market may be underpricing the weekly distribution.

---

*Key levels to watch at Thursday's open: 7,500 (call wall), 7,475 (put wall), 7,520 (R1). Regime: uniformly negative. Catalysts: Jobless Claims 8:30 ET, Fed Williams 9:00 ET. Weekly tail: CPI.*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Thursday, July 9, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## Lead Structural Story: Regime Flip to Positive — The Tape's Character Just Changed

The dominant structural development heading into Friday is not the day's price action — it's what happened to the gamma regime underneath it. The morning opened in **negative gamma**, where dealer hedging amplifies moves and momentum tends to feed on itself. By the close, the regime had **flipped to positive**. Dealers are now net long options, and their hedging behavior reverses entirely: they sell strength and buy weakness, compressing realized volatility and creating a gravitational pull toward key levels. That is not a subtle change. Traders carrying directional exposure built for trending conditions are now operating in an environment that works against them.

---

## 1. Session Flow: What Drove Today

The session's primary engine was **AI-sector optimism and earnings narrative**, anchored by SK Hynix's US trading debut and continued institutional rotation into large-cap technology. Communication Services led the tape at **+2.7%**, Consumer Cyclical added **+1.4%** — both consistent with a risk-on, growth-tilted session. Defensive sectors were the clear casualty: Consumer Defensive shed **-1.0%** and Utilities gave back **-1.1%**, the classic signature of capital rotating out of safety and into growth.

The wide **3.8% sector dispersion** is the tell here. This was not a broad-based lift — it was rotational conviction. Investors were making deliberate sector choices, not simply buying beta.

Geopolitical noise from US-Iran tensions (US airstrikes on Iranian targets) created an intraday wobble and spiked energy — but diplomacy headlines allowed the market to look through it. That macro risk premium faded as the session progressed, and the AI narrative reclaimed the driver's seat.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: Morning Called Negative, Session Confirmed It — Then Shifted

The morning forecast carried **negative gamma** as the structural backdrop, with a **TREND (UP) call at 75% confidence**. That call was validated. In a negative gamma environment, dealer hedging flows amplify directional moves — buying into strength — and that's precisely what the session delivered. SPX printed a **+0.82% gain** with a session range of just **0.87%** (high 7546.89 / low 7481.73). The trend was moderate but directionally clean, consistent with negative gamma amplification on the upside.

VIX confirmed: it dropped **-6.28%** to **15.84**, moving sharply inverse to SPX — textbook behavior for a trending, directionally committed session rather than a pinned, range-bound one.

The critical development came at the close: the regime **flipped to positive**. The intraday accumulation of call positioning, combined with SPX pressing toward the 7550 call wall, shifted the net GEX profile into positive territory. What started as a negative gamma trend day ended as a positive gamma setup. Traders who don't account for that shift going into Friday will be misaligned.

---

## 3. VIX / Gamma Interplay

The VIX's **-6.28%** decline to **15.84** is the clearest confirmation of the day's character. Volatility collapsed as the session trended — that is the inverse behavior you expect when dealer hedging is amplifying a directional move (negative gamma) rather than suppressing it. Had positive gamma been in control from the open, the session would likely have been tighter and more mean-reverting; VIX would not have compressed this aggressively.

With the regime now **positive at the close**, tomorrow's VIX behavior should be watched closely. A stable-to-rising VIX against a flat or slightly positive SPX would be an early warning that the market is building optionality rather than releasing it — a potential sign that the positive gamma compression is doing its job at resistance.

At **15.84**, VIX remains at a level that does not price systemic concern. The geopolitical risk premium from US-Iran appears to be actively deflating. That said, **weekly risk is rated elevated** given next week's CPI print (Core Inflation Rate YoY and MoM for June). Complacency at current VIX levels heading into an inflation catalyst deserves scrutiny.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: Levels, Regime, and Catalysts

**Closing regime: POSITIVE.** Dealers are net long options. Their hedging behavior involves selling into rallies and buying into dips — **moves are expected to be dampened, and the session should exhibit mean-reverting characteristics**.

There is **no GEX flip level within the observable chain**. The regime is uniformly positive across the strike landscape, which means there is no nearby structural threshold where behavior would abruptly shift. Navigation relies entirely on walls and technical pivots.

**Key levels for Friday:**

| Level | Value | Context |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall / R-area | **7550** | Overhead resistance; dealers sell into this level |
| R1 | **7581** | First technical resistance above call wall |
| R2 | **7619** | Outer resistance; requires catalyst to reach |
| Put Wall / S-area | **7500** | Structural floor; dealer buying supports this level |
| S1 | **7506** | First technical support |
| S2 | **7468** | Downside extension if 7500 breaks |
| Expected Move | **±75pts** | Defines the 7469–7619 fair range for the session |

The SPX closed at **7543.64**, sitting directly beneath the **7550 call wall**. ES futures are pricing a **+0.6% gap** to approximately **7590**, which would open SPX above the call wall and into the R1 zone. That gap is notable: a cash open above 7550 in a positive gamma regime places price at a level where **dealer selling pressure is structurally present**. The gap does not need to hold — and in a positive gamma environment, it frequently doesn't. Watch whether the opening print above 7550 sustains or fades back into the 7500–7550 range. The mean-reversion tendency of the regime argues for the latter as the baseline.

**Tomorrow's catalyst: WASDE Report (12:00 ET, medium impact).** The World Agricultural Supply and Demand Estimates will move commodity-adjacent equities and agricultural names at midday, but is unlikely to be a macro market mover for SPX. The more important calendar context is the **elevated weekly risk** from CPI data (not this week, but building positioning dynamics already). Today's 10Y yield at **4.54% (-2bp)** and a normal yield curve (+38bp 2s-10s) provide no immediate rates shock to price in.

**Overnight:** Gap dynamics should be treated as noise until the cash market confirms. The positive gamma regime, uniformly distributed, argues against a sustained break above the call wall without a material catalyst that isn't currently on the Friday docket.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications

**Regime: POSITIVE GAMMA → Income and range-bound structures are the correct alignment.**

In a positive gamma environment, dealer hedging **dampens moves**. They sell strength and buy weakness, compressing realized vol and creating a range-bound, mean-reverting session character. Directional structures that require a sustained break — long deltas via debit spreads, naked calls, or momentum verticals — are swimming against the structural current.

**Structures that fit:**
- **Iron Condors / Credit Spreads:** The 7500 put wall and 7550 call wall define a natural range. A condor structured around these walls benefits directly from the regime's mean-reversion tendency. With the expected move at ±75 points, the 7469–7619 range gives meaningful room for short strikes to sit outside typical drift.
- **Put Credit Spreads (defined risk):** If price opens above 7550 on the gap and fades back, a put spread defending 7500 support with positive gamma beneath it captures the dealer bid dynamic at the lower wall.
- **Short Premium / Covered Positions:** With VIX at 15.84 and the regime compressing realized vol, near-dated premium sellers have a structural tailwind. Avoid unhedged short gamma given the elevated weekly risk profile.

**What to avoid:** Long debit spreads or directional momentum structures betting on a sustained break above 7550 or below 7500 without a catalyst that overrides the positive gamma dampening. The regime is working against you in those scenarios.

**Sizing note:** Tomorrow's session is rated **low risk** on a standalone basis — light calendar, normal trading expected. However, the **elevated weekly risk** from pending CPI data argues for keeping gross exposure measured. Build positions that can absorb Thursday-into-Friday vol expansion without requiring a stop.

---

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Friday, July 10, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## Session Flow: Positive Gamma Holds Structure, But Not Without a Test

Today's session delivered a **mixed outcome** against the morning's range-bound forecast — technically validating the positive gamma framework, but not before a sharp intraday dip challenged the structural thesis. SPX printed a 0.96% range (High: 7,579.93 / Low: 7,508.16), closing at **7,575.39 (+0.43%)**, essentially pinned against the call wall.

The primary session driver appeared to be a combination of **AI chip momentum** — SK Hynix's U.S. trading debut attracted capital flows into the semiconductor complex — and **broad risk-on positioning** into a light-calendar Friday. The move was not macro-led in the traditional sense; there was no scheduled tier-1 data. Instead, flow dynamics and sector rotation dominated the tape.

The intraday low of 7,508 deserves attention. That represents a roughly **67-point drawdown from the high**, a meaningful move within what should have been a contained, positive gamma session. The recovery to close near session highs suggests dealer hedging activity — buying on the dip, dampening the sell-off — did ultimately perform its structural role, but the magnitude of the flush warrants acknowledgment. Something hit the tape hard enough to briefly overwhelm the passive stabilizing pressure.

---

## Regime Validation: Call Confirmed, But With Friction

The morning's call was **positive gamma / range-bound at 70% confidence**. The session type came in as **mixed** — the regime held, but the path was messier than a clean range-bound print would imply.

Here is what the data confirms: SPX closed at 7,575.39, pinned to within five points of the **7,575 put wall and 7,580 call wall**. That degree of gravitational pull toward this narrow band is textbook positive gamma mechanics — **dealers long options are continuously delta-hedging, selling into strength and buying weakness**, creating a natural compression effect. The close precisely between the two walls is not coincidental.

What contradicted the clean range-bound call was the intraday excursion to 7,508. The session low punched below S1 (7,538), which is atypical in a firmly positive gamma regime. That suggests a momentum-driven or flow-triggered episode — potentially 0DTE put buying or a transient macro headline — temporarily disrupted the dealer stabilization. The regime ultimately reasserted, but traders who leaned too aggressively on a quiet Friday got a reminder that **positive gamma dampens moves, it does not eliminate risk**.

**Net assessment: Regime validated. Execution was rougher than the headline forecast implied.**

---

## VIX / Gamma Interplay: Clean Risk-Off Unwind

VIX closed at **15.03, down 5.12%** on a day where SPX gained 0.43%. That is directionally coherent — fear premium compressing as equities recover — but the magnitude of the VIX decline relative to the SPX gain is notable. A 5%+ move in VIX on less than a half-percent SPX rally implies:

1. **Short-dated premium was aggressively sold** into the close, consistent with a Friday expiry dynamic where 0DTE options collapse to zero and market makers are net receivers of premium as positions expire worthless.
2. The VIX compression reinforces the positive gamma narrative — dealers were not in a stressed hedging posture by day's end.

At 15.03, VIX sits in a benign, low-volatility regime. This is consistent with positive gamma suppressing realized volatility and the market's structural placement well above both the 50-SMA and 200-SMA (SPY +2.0% and +8.8% respectively, RSI at neutral 51). There is no technical stress signal in the underlying.

---

## Sector Rotation: Defensive Leadership Warrants Attention

Today's sector data presents a mildly contradictory signal worth flagging:

- **Leaders: Utilities (+1.3%), Basic Materials (+0.7%)**
- **Laggards: Financial Services (-0.7%), Healthcare (-1.7%)**

Utilities leading on a risk-on day is a classic defensive rotation signal. While SK Hynix's debut kept the AI/semiconductor narrative alive in headlines, the **underlying sector rotation was not unambiguously risk-on**. Financials underperforming on a day when the 10Y yield ticked up two basis points to 4.56% is counterintuitive — banks typically benefit from steeper curves, and the 2Y/10Y spread at +35bp is constructive. Healthcare's -1.7% decline is the session's sharpest sectoral move and may reflect specific stock-level pressure rather than a macro rotation signal, but it bears monitoring.

**Bottom line on rotation:** The surface narrative (AI chips, equity gains) and the sector tape are not fully aligned. Defensive leadership on a green day often signals that the rally lacked broad participation conviction.

---

## Tomorrow's Setup: Confined Range, Low Catalyst Load

**Gamma Regime at Close: POSITIVE — Uniformly so across the observable chain. No GEX flip level exists in range. Do not anchor to a flip level; the walls and technical pivots are the operative framework.**

**Key Structural Levels:**
| Level | Value |
|---|---|
| R2 | 7,651.0 |
| R1 | 7,613.0 |
| Call Wall | 7,580.0 |
| Put Wall | 7,575.0 |
| S1 | 7,538.0 |
| S2 | 7,500.0 |
| Expected Move | ±72 pts (7,503 – 7,647) |

**ES futures are carrying a ~50-point overnight gap higher (7,626.0 vs. SPX close 7,575.39).** This gap represents roughly 70% of the full daily expected move on its own. Do not treat the gap as a settled baseline — the cash open will determine whether buyers defend the gap or fade it into the call wall structure. Importantly, the call wall at 7,580 becomes the first friction point after the open. If ES gaps open near 7,626, the cash market will be opening **above the call wall**, which means the structural gravity that pinned SPX into the close today will have shifted. Watch whether dealers begin selling the rally above 7,580 to maintain delta neutrality — that is the positive gamma playbook, and it would create overhead resistance at the open.

**Overnight and open-session framework:**
- **Above 7,580 (call wall):** Expect resistance. Positive gamma mechanics mean dealers will be selling into strength above this level. R1 at 7,613 is the next meaningful resistance if buyers absorb the call wall supply. R2 at 7,651 marks the outer boundary of the expected move to the upside.
- **7,538 – 7,575 (core range):** If the gap fades, the put wall at 7,575 transitions to support. S1 at 7,538 is the first significant floor. A hold of this band would be consistent with continued positive gamma compression.
- **Below 7,538 (S1):** A break here reintroduces the intraday dynamics we saw today. S2 at 7,500 and the lower expected move boundary near 7,503 represent the structural support zone. A breach of 7,500 in a positive gamma environment would be an anomaly requiring reassessment of regime stability.

**Tomorrow's risk outlook is LOW.** The calendar is light, no tier-1 U.S. data is scheduled for Monday's session, and tomorrow presents a normal trading environment. This supports the positive gamma / range-bound thesis holding into the session.

**The week-ahead risk is ELEVATED.** **Core CPI MoM and CPI MoM (June)** are on the calendar this coming week. These are tier-1 macro catalysts with demonstrated capacity to dislocate volatility surfaces and shift gamma regimes rapidly. Traders should not allow a benign Friday to create complacency heading into inflation data. Position sizing into the week should account for the possibility that the positive gamma regime faces a regime-disrupting catalyst.

---

## Strategy Implications: Weekend and Next-Session Positioning

**Regime: POSITIVE GAMMA | Risk: LOW (Tomorrow) / ELEVATED (Week)**

Given positive gamma and a light Friday calendar, the structural playbook favors **premium-selling and range-defined structures**. Dealer behavior in this regime — buying dips and selling rallies to maintain delta neutrality — creates the mean-reversion conditions that income structures require to perform.

**Structures with regime alignment:**

- **Iron Condors (SPX/SPY):** The 7,575/7,580 zone has now served as gravity for two consecutive sessions. A condor selling the 7,613–7,651 upside and the 7,503–7,538 downside captures the expected move boundaries with appropriate wing spacing. The put wall at 7,575 and call wall at 7,580 serve as natural anchors for the short strikes on the inner legs.

- **Credit Spreads (Call Side):** Given the gap open scenario, a call credit spread positioned above 7,613 (R1) exploits the likely dealer resistance above the call wall. Positive gamma mechanics support a fade of any early momentum above 7,580.

- **What to avoid:** Debit spreads or outright directional structures that require a sustained breakout are misaligned with the current regime. Positive gamma suppresses the very trending behavior these structures need. Save directional premium deployment for the CPI week, where a regime shift becomes plausible.

**Week-ahead hedge note:** The elevated weekly risk around CPI data argues for **not being naked short vol into Tuesday/Wednesday**. Consider carrying defined-risk structures rather than naked credit exposure, or explicitly plan to close income trades before the CPI print. A significant inflation surprise — particularly a re-acceleration — could rapidly shift the gamma regime from positive to negative, reversing the structural mechanics and amplifying moves in either direction.

---

*Levels to watch at Monday's open: Call wall 7,580 / ES gap fill dynamic / Whether price holds above or below put wall 7,575 in the first 30 minutes. CPI week positioning begins Monday.*

---

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Monday, July 13, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## REGIME ALERT: POSITIVE TO NEGATIVE GAMMA FLIP

The structural story tonight is not the geopolitical headline — it is the gamma regime. The market closed in **negative gamma** after opening the session under a **positive gamma** framework. That is the lead risk factor heading into Tuesday's CPI print.

---

## 1. Session Flow: Macro-Driven Trend, Not Technical Chop

The morning's call for **MIXED/CHOP at 55% confidence** was contradicted by a **moderate trend session** to the downside. The miss was not a modeling failure — it was a macro override. **US-Iran military tensions** escalated intraday, driving WTI crude to **$74.06 (+3.71%)** and triggering broad risk-off repositioning. The catalyst was exogenous and binary in character, the kind of geopolitical shock that can overwhelm positioning signals.

Technology was the primary casualty. **Semiconductors in particular** saw concentrated selling — Micron off sharply, Sandisk suffering a double-digit decline — as profit-taking collided with macro fear. Energy was the sole sector with meaningful sponsorship, consistent with the crude spike. Per our sector data: **Technology -1.6%, Industrials -1.2%** lagged, while **Energy +1.0%, Consumer Defensive +0.3%** held relative ground. This is a textbook risk-off rotation, not a rotation with structural durability on its own.

SPX printed a session range of just **0.79%** (high: 7,565.37, low: 7,506.41) before closing at **7,515.34 (-0.80%)**. The range was narrower than a true trend day, but the directional character — relentless drift lower with no meaningful recovery — aligned more with trending behavior than the mean-reversion profile that positive gamma environments typically produce.

---

## 2. Regime Validation: The Flip Is the Story

The morning regime was **positive gamma** — a framework in which dealer hedging activity dampens volatility and fosters range-bound, mean-reverting behavior. The session did not behave that way.

**The closing regime is now NEGATIVE GAMMA**, and there is no observable flip level across the current chain — GEX is uniformly negative. This is not a marginal regime; it is a clean structural shift with no nearby stabilizer built into the options architecture.

What likely drove the flip: the geopolitical shock pulled implied volatility higher (VIX +14.18% to 17.16), repricing options and reshaping dealer net exposure across the chain. As put buying accelerated intraday and gamma profiles rotated, the closing mark reflects dealers now net **short gamma** at current price levels.

The practical implication: the positive gamma dampening effect that the morning's chop call was premised on is **no longer present**. Tomorrow's dealer behavior profile is fundamentally different.

---

## 3. VIX/Gamma Interplay: Confirmation, With a Caveat

VIX closed at **17.16, up 14.18%** — a materially larger move than the news-sourced estimates suggested. Market data is the source of truth here, and 17.16 represents a meaningful vol expansion, not a contained tactical hedge.

The VIX move is consistent with and confirmatory of the gamma regime flip. As dealers transitioned from net long to net short gamma positioning, their hedging flows **amplified** rather than dampened the directional move — precisely the mechanic expected in a negative gamma environment. The 10Y yield rising **6bps to 4.62%** adds another layer: rate vol and equity vol are moving together, compressing risk appetite from two directions simultaneously.

RSI(14) on SPY sits at **52** — still neutral, not oversold. Price remains **+1.1% above the 50-SMA and +7.9% above the 200-SMA**. The technical structure is not broken; this is a regime and catalyst story, not a trend reversal story. That distinction matters for sizing.

---

## 4. Tomorrow's Setup: CPI Into Negative Gamma

Tomorrow is a high-risk session by any measure. The combination of **CPI data at 08:30 ET** (four separate prints: headline CPI, Core CPI YoY, MoM inflation, Core inflation rate YoY) landing into a **negative gamma environment with no mechanical floor** from dealer positioning is the most consequential setup of the week.

**Key levels to watch:**

| Level | Price | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,550 | Overhead resistance; dealer supply above |
| R1 | 7,553 | Confirms call wall cluster |
| R2 | 7,590 | Bull case extension if CPI soft |
| Put Wall | 7,500 | Near-term structural support |
| S1 | 7,478 | First meaningful support on a break |
| S2 | 7,440 | Bear case target on hot CPI |
| Expected Move | ±81 pts | Implies range 7,434–7,596 |

The **Put Wall at 7,500** is the most critical near-term level. SPX closed at 7,515 — just **15 handles above the Put Wall**. In a negative gamma regime, dealers are **short options** and must **buy into rallies and sell into declines** to hedge. A CPI print that initially pushes SPX below 7,500 will not encounter mechanical dealer support; it will encounter dealer selling that accelerates the move. There is no in-range GEX flip to slow momentum.

Conversely, a soft CPI print that lifts SPX toward the **Call Wall at 7,550/R1 at 7,553** could see a sharp squeeze. In negative gamma, dealers would need to buy the rally to hedge short calls — amplifying upside momentum toward R2 at 7,590.

**NFIB Business Optimism at 06:00 ET** will be the overnight tone-setter, but with CPI two-and-a-half hours later, its market impact should be treated as temporary signal, not durable direction.

A Fed governor has already publicly flagged the possibility of **rate hikes if inflation comes in hot** this week. That statement is not background noise heading into tomorrow's print — it is the tail risk that makes a hot CPI particularly dangerous for equities from a rates-repricing standpoint.

---

## 5. Strategy Implications: Respect the Regime

In a **negative gamma environment with a high-impact catalyst**, dealer hedging flows will **amplify directional moves** in whichever direction CPI takes the market. This is emphatically not an environment for income structures that rely on range-bound behavior.

**Structures aligned with tomorrow's regime:**

- **Defined-risk directional spreads** (debit verticals) make sense for traders with a CPI directional view. The expected move of ±81 points provides strike selection guidance.
- **Long straddles or strangles** (pre-CPI entry) are viable for traders without a directional lean, capitalizing on the amplification dynamic inherent in negative gamma — though premium is elevated given VIX at 17.16, so timing entry carefully relative to tonight's overnight session matters.
- **Avoid naked or undefined risk short premium structures** — iron condors, short straddles, and credit spreads are structurally misaligned with a negative gamma, high-catalyst environment. Dealer flows will not contain the move.
- **Put spreads targeting S1 (7,478) or S2 (7,440)** as bear-case strikes offer defined risk for a hot CPI scenario, with the Put Wall at 7,500 as the near-term trigger level to watch.

**Position sizing:** Elevated risk applies both tomorrow and into the week given the Michigan Consumer Sentiment reading still ahead. Reduce notional exposure accordingly.

---

**Bottom line:** A geopolitical macro shock flipped the gamma regime intraday. We now enter a CPI print — the highest-impact data release of the week — in a uniformly negative gamma environment with SPX sitting 15 handles above its Put Wall and no mechanical stabilizer in the options chain. Tomorrow is a day to have a view, size it correctly, and let the directional structure do the work. It is not a day to sell premium and wait.

*— Head of Derivatives Strategy*
