**Desk Summary:** Today's session underwhelmed relative to the negative gamma / high-volatility call — rotation dynamics and FOMC positioning kept the tape contained despite a structurally amplifying regime. Tomorrow changes the equation entirely. The Fed decision at 14:00 ET, with no mechanical GEX flip level to anchor dealer hedging, means the negative gamma structure is fully exposed to a trending move in either direction. # Evening Derivatives Desk Recap

---

## LEAD STRUCTURAL STORY: NEGATIVE GAMMA REGIME DELIVERING — DEALERS AMPLIFY THE MOVE

The negative gamma regime that opened the session did exactly what the mechanics dictate. With dealers structurally short options, their hedging flows **buy weakness and sell strength in the same direction as the market** — amplifying rather than containing price action. Today's session delivered a strong trend lower, with SPX printing a 1.87% range, closing near the session low at **7,316.15 (-1.52%)**, and VIX surging **+13.46% to 20.66**. ## 1. SESSION FLOW: MACRO-DRIVEN, MECHANICALLY AMPLIFIED

Today's tape was **macro-driven at its core**. The fundamental catalyst was a combination of **Fed policy complexity and geopolitical escalation** — the FOMC held rates steady, but three officials dissented in favor of a hike, introducing a hawkish overtone the market was not positioned for. Simultaneously, **oil spiked 7%** on reported escalation in the Iran conflict, compressing risk appetite and pressuring rate-sensitive and cyclical positioning simultaneously.

The cross-asset read is consistent: 10-year yields rose **+6 basis points to 4.67%**, which is a meaningful single-session move that repriced equity discount rates in real time. The macro shock provided the **directional impulse**; the negative gamma environment provided the **mechanical amplification**. Dealers, structurally short options into the move, were forced to sell into the decline to rebalance delta — adding fuel to a tape that was already under pressure from two independent macro catalysts.

The sector dispersion tells the rotation story clearly. **Consumer Defensive (+0.5%) and Communication Services (+0.4%)** led — classic flight-to-relative-safety positioning. **Industrials (-2.6%) and Utilities (-2.7%)** were the session's hardest-hit sectors, with Utilities likely caught in the crossfire of the rates move (duration-sensitive) and Industrials pricing in geopolitical supply chain risk. The **3.2% sector spread** confirms this was not a broad-based de-risking but a **rotational, macro-informed repricing**.

The Iran escalation and the hawkish Fed dissent combination likely drove implied volatility expansion beyond what spot movement alone would justify.

From a dealer flow perspective, the negative gamma regime creates a **self-reinforcing dynamic in vol**: as SPX fell, dealers' short-gamma exposure grew, requiring larger delta hedges. Those hedging flows push the underlying further, which mechanically lifts implied vol, which in turn increases the mark-to-market loss on dealer short-options books — creating a feedback loop. ## 4. TOMORROW'S SETUP: PCE MORNING — REGIME AND LEVELS DEFINE THE STRIKE ZONE

**Overnight Context:** ES futures are pricing at **7,379.75 vs. SPX cash close of 7,316.15**, implying a **~0.9% overnight gap higher**. This gap context is supplementary — the cash market has not confirmed it, and overnight gaps in a negative gamma regime are not inherently self-sustaining. The standard closing regime remains **NEGATIVE**, which means any opening strength should be treated with discipline rather than assumed to persist. Do not position around the gap as a baseline.

**The Catalyst:** Tomorrow's 08:30 ET slate is the highest-priority macro print of the week — a **full PCE bundle** alongside Q2 GDP-related data:
- Core PCE Price Index MoM and YoY (June)
- PCE Prices QoQ (Q2)
- GDP Sales QoQ (Q2)
- Personal Income MoM (June)

After today's hawkish FOMC dissent and the rates selloff (10Y at 4.67%), the market's sensitivity to **Core PCE** is elevated. A hot print — particularly Core PCE MoM above consensus — will be read through the lens of the three dissenters who want to hike, potentially validating their view and extending today's pressure. A soft or in-line print could trigger a relief rally from oversold conditions, but in a **negative gamma regime without a flip level**, even a rally would face the structural headwind of dealer flows chasing price higher rather than selling into it.

**Key Levels for Tomorrow:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall | 7,450 | Hard ceiling; session high today tested and rejected this |
| R2 | 7,389 | Upper resistance if gap holds |
| R1 | 7,353 | First meaningful resistance in a gap-fill scenario |
| **Current ES Implied** | **~7,380** | Gap open zone — watch for cash confirm |
| Put Wall | 7,300 | Critical structural floor; SPX low today was 7,313 |
| S1 | 7,280 | First meaningful support below the put wall |
| S2 | 7,243 | Deeper support if 7,280 fails |
| Expected Move | ±95 | Full daily range envelope: ~7,221–7,411 |

**The 7,300 put wall is the single most important level to monitor.** Today's session low of 7,313.92 held just above it. A sustained break below 7,300 in a uniformly negative gamma environment — with no in-range flip to arrest dealer flows — would expose S1 at 7,280 and potentially S2 at 7,243 with limited mechanical resistance in between.

To the upside, the **call wall at 7,450** is the structural ceiling. Note that today's session high was 7,450.84 — the market tested this level and reversed sharply. In a negative gamma regime, the call wall does not function as a magnet; it functions as a boundary dealers defend by selling into. A push toward 7,450 on a soft PCE print should be treated as a potential fade zone, not a breakout catalyst, unless the regime shifts.

---

## 5. STRATEGY IMPLICATIONS: NEGATIVE GAMMA + HIGH-IMPACT CATALYSTS = DEFINED-RISK DIRECTIONAL

**Regime:** NEGATIVE (uniformly negative, no in-range flip level)
**Catalyst Load:** Extreme — five high-impact data points at 08:30 ET
**Volatility Environment:** Elevated, VIX 20.66, SPY RSI approaching oversold

In a **negative gamma environment**, dealers are **short options and hedge procyclically** — they buy rallies and sell declines, amplifying directional moves. This makes **income structures (iron condors, credit spreads)** structurally inappropriate for this environment. The regime favors **defined-risk directional structures** where the gamma mechanics are working with your position, not against it.

**Structures to Consider:**

- **Defined-risk put verticals (debit spreads):** If Core PCE prints hot and 7,300 breaks, the negative gamma regime provides mechanical follow-through. A put debit spread below 7,300 (e.g., 7,300/7,250) captures the S1/S2 range with defined risk ahead of the number. Size conservatively given the ±95 expected move.

- **Call debit spreads (PCE relief trade):** If positioning for a soft PCE print and a gap-fill continuation, a call debit spread targeting R1 (7,353) or R2 (7,389) with the call wall at 7,450 as the hard cap keeps risk defined and aligns with the regime's amplification tendency in the direction of the move. Do not stretch the short leg above 7,450 — the call wall is a real structural resistance.

- **Avoid undefined risk / short vol:** VIX at 20.66 with a top-tier macro catalyst tomorrow makes short premium naked structures a low-probability-of-success environment. Dealers are not providing dampening — there is no mechanical stabilizer. Straddle/strangle sellers are exposed to the same amplification that printed a 1.87% range today.

- **Volatility sizing note:** SPY RSI at 33 introduces a countertrend risk that is real but not regime-confirmed. Do not fade the trend with undisciplined structure. If you are positioning for a bounce, use **limited-risk structures** that do not require sustained follow-through above the call wall.

---

**Bottom Line:** Today's session was a textbook negative gamma trend day, driven by a macro double-punch (hawkish Fed dissent + oil spike) and mechanically amplified by dealer hedging flows. The regime remains uniformly negative into tomorrow's PCE print — the highest-stakes data release of the week. The 7,300 put wall is the line in the sand; the 7,450 call wall defines the ceiling. With no in-range flip level, the regime has no structural relief valve. Size accordingly, keep risk defined, and let the PCE print set the directional thesis before committing full size.

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Thursday, July 30, 2026 | SPX Close: 7,437.63 (+1.66%) | VIX: 17.09 (-17.28%)

---

## Session Flow

Today's session was **macro-driven**, though the primary catalyst tells a more nuanced story than the headline gain suggests. The news feed carried conflicting signals — references to a sharp Fed-driven selloff and a rising VIX do not align with the actual close. **Market data is the source of truth here**: SPX finished up +1.66%, VIX collapsed 17.28% to 17.09, and the session printed as a **strong trend day** to the upside. The most credible macro anchor from the verified data is today's **June Core PCE print at +3.3%**, which edged lower from May and appears to have provided the relief catalyst. That disinflation read — however modest — was sufficient to drive a rotation into risk assets, with **Technology (+2.3%)** and **Energy (+1.4%)** leading. The wide **4.2% sector dispersion** confirms this was a rotational, selective bid rather than a uniform risk-on surge. Notably, **Industrials (-0.5%)** and **Real Estate (-1.9%)** lagged meaningfully, suggesting markets are not pricing in a clean growth acceleration — this was a rates-relief trade, not a growth trade.

---

## Regime Validation

The morning called **NEGATIVE gamma** with a **HIGH VOLATILITY** day type at 70% confidence. The session delivered a **strong TREND** — directionally consistent with the negative gamma call, even if the label shifted from "high volatility" to "trend." In a negative gamma environment, **dealers are short options and must buy into rallies to hedge their exposure**, which amplifies the directional move. That is precisely the dynamic that played out: once price broke above intraday resistance, dealer hedging flows added fuel rather than friction. The session range of **1.06%** and a push from the low of **7,370.98** to the high of **7,448.75** — a 77-point intraday swing — is consistent with gamma-amplified momentum rather than the dampened, mean-reverting character you would see in a positive gamma tape. The forecast was validated in character and mechanism, even if the exact label (high vol vs. trend) diverged slightly.

The closing regime remains **NEGATIVE**, with **no GEX flip level present anywhere on the observable chain**. This is a uniformly negative gamma environment. There is no structural support level at which dealer hedging behavior flips — the walls (call at **7,500**, put at **7,400**) are the primary bounds, not a flip-driven pivot.

---

## VIX / Gamma Interplay

VIX's 17.28% collapse to **17.09** is the sharpest confirming signal of the day. In a negative gamma regime, large directional moves are accompanied by vol repricing — and today's rally was no exception. The VIX/SPX inverse relationship behaved cleanly: a strong rally crushed implied volatility, consistent with options sellers being rewarded and short gamma dealers covering hedges as the market ran. The magnitude of the VIX decline (-3.64 vol points) suggests substantial put premium decay across the chain, which would have accelerated the upside as dealers unwound short-delta hedges. **Rates offered a mild headwind** — the 10Y yield ticked up 1bp to **4.68%** — but that move was too small to disrupt the risk bid. The 2Y-10Y spread at **+45bp** reflects a normal curve; no systemic distress embedded in the rates structure. SPY RSI at **46** remains neutral with price sitting just **-0.4% below its 50-SMA** — the market is near a technically important inflection point heading into tomorrow.

---

## Tomorrow's Setup

ES futures are trading at **7,484.25**, approximately **0.6% above** tonight's SPX close of **7,437.63**. That gap is notable but should not be treated as a given — cash confirmation is required at the open. The overnight bid brings ES directly into the **7,475 R1 / 7,500 call wall** zone, which is the critical structural ceiling.

**Key levels for Friday:**

| Level | Value | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| Call Wall / R2 Zone | 7,500 – 7,512 | Hard ceiling; dealer short-gamma selling pressure increases |
| R1 | 7,475 | First resistance; overnight ES already near this level |
| Put Wall / S1 | 7,400 | Structural floor; dealer buying below this level |
| S2 | 7,363 | Second support; breach would signal regime deterioration |
| Expected Move | ±80 pts | Defines the 7,357 – 7,517 one-day range |

The **7,500 call wall** is the defining level for Friday. In a uniformly negative gamma environment with no flip level on the chain, there is no structural mechanism that naturally caps the move from below — **dealer hedging flows will amplify movement in either direction**. However, the call wall at 7,500 represents concentrated open interest that acts as a gravitational ceiling; approaching it into an employment data print creates an asymmetric setup. A clean break and hold above 7,500 would be technically significant and could accelerate further, while rejection there — particularly on a hotter-than-expected Employment Cost Index — sets up a swift reversal back toward 7,400.

**Tomorrow's catalyst cluster** is concentrated at **08:30 ET** and is labor cost-focused:

- **Employment Cost Index QoQ (Q2)** — medium impact; the headline read
- **Employment Cost - Wages QoQ (Q2)** — medium impact; the Fed-sensitive component
- **Employment Cost - Benefits QoQ (Q2)** — medium impact

This is a materially important data point in the current environment. With Core PCE already printing at **3.3%** and the Fed on hold, any upside surprise in Q2 labor costs — particularly wages — will reignite the "higher for longer" narrative and risk a sharp giveback of today's PCE relief rally. Conversely, a soft ECI print would validate the disinflationary read and could challenge the 7,500 call wall. The **week-ahead risk** remains elevated: **Nonfarm Productivity (Q2)** and **JOLTs Job Openings (June)** are pending, keeping the macro tape active.

---

## Strategy Implications

The closing **negative gamma regime** with no in-range flip point means the structural baseline for Friday is **trend and amplification, not mean-reversion**. Dealer hedging flows will add momentum in whichever direction the ECI print drives price — **dealers buy into rallies and sell into declines** in this environment, reinforcing directionality.

**Income structures (iron condors, credit spreads) are structurally misaligned with this regime** and should be avoided as primary positioning tools. The negative gamma tape does not support the range-bound, mean-reverting behavior required for those structures to perform.

**Frameworks that make sense:**

- **Defined-risk directional (debit spreads / verticals):** Appropriate for traders with a view on ECI direction. A bull call spread above 7,475, targeting the 7,500 wall, captures the continuation scenario with defined risk. A bear put spread below 7,400, anchored at the put wall, captures the hot-data reversal.
- **Pre-data hedging:** Given the overnight gap and proximity to the call wall, outright long premium going into 08:30 ET has a reasonable volatility thesis — if ECI surprises in either direction, the negative gamma tape will amplify the move.
- **Avoid naked short vol into the print.** VIX at 17.09 is not at extremes, and with a macro catalyst landing pre-market into a regime that amplifies moves, short gamma structures carry elevated risk of being run over.

**Sizing note:** The ±80-point expected move defines the probabilistic range. Positions that survive a gap-and-reverse scenario (i.e., ES opening near R1/call wall and reversing on a hot ECI print back toward 7,400) are the appropriate filter for entry sizing tonight.

---

*Levels to watch at the open: 7,475 (R1 / cash confirmation of gap), 7,500 (call wall / structural ceiling), 7,400 (put wall / first real support). Regime remains negative until confirmed otherwise by cash market structure.*

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Friday, July 31, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## LEAD STORY: REGIME FLIP — NEGATIVE TO POSITIVE GAMMA AT THE CLOSE

The single most important structural development heading into next week is a **confirmed regime transition**: SPX closed the session in **positive gamma territory**, ending a negative gamma environment that defined this week's tape. This flip is the dominant framing for Monday's setup and reshapes the dealer hedging backdrop materially. Traders who carry positions sized for trend continuation need to recalibrate.

---

## 1. SESSION FLOW: MACRO + EARNINGS DROVE THE TAPE

Today's move was not a technical or flow-driven session — it was **macro and earnings-led**. The Fed's decision to hold rates steady provided the policy backstop, while strong results from Amazon and Microsoft anchored AI/semiconductor sentiment and gave institutional desks a fundamental reason to add risk into month-end. The bounce was real, not a gamma squeeze artifact.

That said, the **month-end rebalancing dynamic** cannot be dismissed. July was the first down month for the S&P 500 since 2014 per current reporting, which likely generated mechanical equity buying from pension and balanced-fund rebalancers into the close — layering a flow tailwind on top of the fundamental catalyst. The combination produced a session that felt more forceful than the underlying +0.70% close implies, with SPX trading a 1.52% intraday range (7,399.83 – 7,512.04).

---

## 2. REGIME VALIDATION: FORECAST CONFIRMED, BUT REGIME SHIFTED BENEATH IT

The morning called **TREND (UP)** at 75% confidence under a **negative gamma** regime. That call was validated — SPX printed a moderate uptrend session, with price sustaining directional momentum and the session range consistent with trending, not mean-reverting, price action.

Critically, **negative gamma mechanics supported the morning's forecast correctly**: with dealers short options and needing to hedge by buying into rallies, the macro/earnings catalyst had an amplifier. As SPX lifted off early lows, dealer hedging flows reinforced rather than resisted the move — a textbook negative gamma trending session.

**What changed:** By the close, cumulative GEX across the observable chain flipped entirely positive. There is no in-range flip level — the regime is **uniformly positive** into Monday. This means the dealer hedging posture heading into next week is the inverse of what it was this morning. The same Fed/earnings-driven buying that dealers chased on the way up will now be systematically faded if the market attempts to extend.

---

## 3. VIX / GAMMA INTERPLAY: CLEAN CONFIRMATION, THEN COMPRESSION

VIX closed at **15.99, -6.44%** on the session — a directionally clean confirmation of SPX's risk-on bid. The magnitude of the VIX decline is worth noting: a 6.44% drop in implied volatility on a +0.70% SPX session suggests the market is pricing out near-term tail risk more aggressively than the index move alone warrants. Some of that compression likely reflects the removal of the Fed event premium (the FOMC meeting is now resolved) and month-end options expiry/roll dynamics clearing near-dated hedges off the board.

The transition into positive gamma at the close reinforces the VIX compression narrative: as dealers absorb more long-options exposure, their structural hedging activity will naturally dampen realized volatility. **The regime shift and VIX trajectory are telling the same story** — the market expects calmer, more range-bound conditions into next week's open. Whether that expectation survives NFP is the operative question.

The 10-year yield at **4.75% (+7bp)** is a point of friction worth monitoring. A sharp rates backup while SPX is sitting in positive gamma near recent highs creates a potential negative feedback loop — particularly for the Tech sector, which notably **lagged today at -2.0%** despite the headlines crediting AI/earnings leadership. That divergence between the news narrative and the actual sector data warrants attention.

---

## 4. TOMORROW'S SETUP: MONDAY OPEN FRAMED BY POSITIVE GAMMA + NFP RISK

**Key Levels for Monday:**
- **Call Wall: 7,500** — SPX closed at 7,489.72, sitting just 10 points below the Call Wall. This is the most important near-term reference. In a positive gamma environment, the Call Wall acts as a structural ceiling where dealer hedging flows increase in density.
- **Put Wall: 7,475** — 14 points below the close. This forms the near-term floor of the expected range. Positive gamma dealers will be buyers on any approach toward this level, providing mechanical support.
- **R1: 7,527 / R2: 7,565** — Upside targets if the Call Wall at 7,500 is decisively cleared on volume.
- **S1: 7,452 / S2: 7,415** — Downside levels that matter if the Put Wall at 7,475 gives way on a catalyst-driven break.
- **Expected Move: ±75 points** — Defines the high-probability range as roughly **7,415 – 7,565**, which brackets S2 to R2 cleanly.

**The Overnight and Open:**
SPX is pinned between its Put Wall (7,475) and Call Wall (7,500) at Friday's close — a 25-point band that is tight relative to the ±75 expected move. This is positive gamma compression at work immediately. Barring an overnight headline, expect the Monday open to be contained within this band, with the market probing the Call Wall on any continuation of Friday's risk-on sentiment.

**NFP as the Week's Defining Catalyst:**
Monday's calendar is **light** with a **low-risk** overnight and session outlook. However, the **weekly risk is elevated**: Nonfarm Payrolls (July) and Nonfarm Payrolls Private (July) are on deck later in the week. The positioning calculus shifts meaningfully ahead of that data. Given the Fed's current posture — holding steady with dissenter warnings about potential hikes if inflation reentrenches — a hot NFP print could re-accelerate rate-hike pricing and stress the 4.75% 10-year yield further. That scenario is the primary tail risk to the positive gamma, low-volatility baseline for the week.

---

## 5. STRATEGY IMPLICATIONS: INCOME STRUCTURES NOW APPROPRIATE

With the regime confirming **positive gamma** at the close, the structural playbook shifts toward **income and mean-reversion structures**:

In a positive gamma environment, dealers are **long options** and hedge by **selling into rallies and buying dips** — this mechanical activity dampens directional moves and compresses realized volatility. Sessions become range-bound and mean-reverting rather than trending.

**Appropriate structures given this regime and catalyst profile:**

- **Iron Condors / Credit Spreads:** The 7,475 Put Wall to 7,500 Call Wall defines a near-term compression zone. A condor constructed using S1 (7,452) and R1 (7,527) as the outer strikes captures the expected range while benefiting from positive gamma's dampening effect on realized vol. The VIX at 15.99 offers moderate premium collection without requiring extreme strikes.

- **Put Credit Spreads (7,452/7,415 or similar):** Given the technical backdrop — SPY RSI at 50, price just 0.3% above the 50-SMA, and month-end rebalancing complete — the near-term risk/reward on defined-risk upside or neutral positioning is favorable. The Put Wall at 7,475 provides a mechanical backstop.

- **Avoid naked directional debit structures for Monday:** The trend-day playbook that was appropriate under this morning's negative gamma regime is no longer structurally supported. Debit spreads and directional verticals carry negative theta in an environment where dealer flows are now working against momentum.

**Critical caveat:** Positive gamma is the Monday baseline, but NFP later in the week has the potential to temporarily overwhelm dealer hedging flows with a sufficient macro shock. Size positions to survive that scenario, and treat the income structure as a Monday-through-midweek positioning framework, not a full-week conviction trade. Scale exposure appropriately ahead of payrolls.

# Evening Derivatives Strategy Recap
### Monday, August 3, 2026 | Post-Close

---

## Session Flow: Macro Impulse Overrides Gamma Dampening

Today's move was macro-driven, full stop. The primary catalyst was a sharp de-escalation in U.S.-Iran tensions that hit overnight and carried through the session, pulling WTI crude lower by roughly 6% and dragging the 10-year Treasury yield down 5 basis points to 4.70%. That dual relief valve — lower energy costs and easing rates pressure — provided the fundamental impulse for a broad risk-on rotation. This was not a 0DTE squeeze or earnings-driven leg; it was macro repricing, and the tape behaved accordingly.

The session opened with a gap and held trend character throughout, with SPX printing a high of 7,610 before consolidating into a close of **7,600.5 (+1.48%)**. The session range of 1.40% was directionally wide and consistent with a trending, momentum-driven day.

---

## Regime Validation: Positive Gamma, But the Session Fought the Framework

This morning's call was **MIXED/CHOP** with 55% confidence, consistent with the positive gamma environment. The actual session printed as a **strong trend day** — a direct contradiction of that forecast.

The explanation is straightforward: in a positive gamma regime, dealers are long options and hedge by selling into strength and buying weakness, which mechanically compresses intraday range and promotes mean reversion. That dampening effect was present early, but the magnitude of the macro catalyst — a simultaneous crude collapse and yield decline tied to genuine geopolitical de-escalation — was simply large enough to overwhelm the structural dampening. Dealer hedging flows don't nullify macro; they modulate it. When the fundamental impulse is strong enough, even positive gamma cannot fully contain the directional move.

The regime itself did not flip. GEX never crossed zero across the observable chain today, and the closing regime remains **POSITIVE**. What changed was the catalyst magnitude, not the structural positioning. The morning call was directionally wrong because the macro event was underweighted, not because the gamma framework failed.

---

## VIX / Gamma Interplay: Confirmation Without Divergence

VIX closed at **15.86 (-0.82%)**, a modest decline that is directionally consistent with an equity rally but notably subdued relative to the +1.48% move in SPX. In a normal risk-on session, a 1.5% equity gain typically produces a more pronounced VIX compression. The relatively contained VIX decline suggests the options market is not aggressively re-pricing tail risk away — participants are not fully capitulating on hedges, which may reflect residual caution around the Iran backdrop (noted by analysts as still present in the background) and the NFP week ahead.

Dealer flows today were characterized by the positive gamma dynamic: as SPX pushed higher, dealers were hedging against their long gamma books by selling into the rally — which explains why the high of 7,610 was not sustained and price settled back below that level into the close. The dampening was visible, even if it didn't prevent the trend.

Sector dispersion at **3.8%** confirms this was a rotational, not uniformly broad, advance. **Industrials (+2.5%)** and **Technology (+2.2%)** led. **Healthcare (-1.0%)** and **Consumer Defensive (-1.3%)** lagged sharply — a textbook risk-on rotation out of defensives into cyclical and growth.

---

## Tomorrow's Setup: Pinned Between Walls With a Catalyst-Heavy Morning

The closing positive gamma regime carries into Tuesday with **no GEX flip level in the observable chain**. There is no structural trigger point to watch for a regime change — the regime is uniformly positive across the chain. Navigation tomorrow is therefore governed entirely by walls and key technical pivots.

**Structural boundaries:**
- **Put Wall: 7,600** — SPX closed almost precisely at the Put Wall. This is the critical support reference overnight. A hold above 7,600 into the open keeps the structure constructive. A break below drags toward **S1: 7,562** and then **S2: 7,524**.
- **Call Wall: 7,630** — First meaningful resistance. This is approximately 30 points above Friday's close. The Call Wall will cap momentum unless a catalyst delivers enough impulse to overwhelm dealer selling above that level.
- **R1: 7,639 / R2: 7,677** — Extended targets if 7,630 gives way cleanly.
- **Expected Move: ±76 points** — This defines the Tuesday envelope at roughly **7,524 to 7,677**.

**Tomorrow's catalysts (all pre-market or morning):**

The 08:30 ET triple print — **Exports, Imports, and Trade Balance (June)** — is the first meaningful data. These are medium-impact and will color the narrative around tariff/trade dynamics, though market-moving potential is limited in isolation.

**Factory Orders MoM (June) at 10:00 ET** adds an industrial demand read that could interact with today's Industrials leadership.

**Critical risk note:** The RISK OUTLOOK flags **JOLTs Job Openings (June)** as a potential market mover tomorrow. This is the leading edge of a labor data sequence that culminates in **Unemployment Rate and Non-Farm Payrolls (July)** later this week — both carry elevated market risk. JOLTs has become increasingly sensitive as a signal for Fed trajectory; a hot print reopens rate hike discussions while a soft print reinforces cuts-on-the-table positioning.

**Overnight posture:** SPX sitting on the Put Wall at 7,600 in positive gamma is a range-compression setup by default. Expect the market to test whether this level holds as support before the 08:30 data. A break below 7,600 into the data window would shift focus immediately to 7,562.

---

## Strategy Implications: Positive Gamma Into a Catalyst Sequence

The positive gamma regime defines the structural bias for tomorrow's session: dealers are **long options** and will hedge by **selling into rallies and buying dips**, which promotes mean reversion and range compression. This is the environment where **income structures** capture edge — the mechanical dealer behavior works in your favor when selling premium around known levels.

**Structures that fit:**

- **Iron Condor (7,524 / 7,562 / 7,639 / 7,677):** The ±76 expected move and positive gamma support a range-bound structure bounded by S2 and R1. The positive gamma dealer behavior adds a natural mean-reversion tailwind to this position.
- **Credit Spread (Call side, short above 7,630):** The Call Wall at 7,630 is a natural short-premium anchor. A short call spread with the short leg at or above the Call Wall benefits from both the structural resistance and dealer selling pressure above that level.
- **Put Credit Spread (long below 7,562):** If holding long delta exposure overnight, put credit spreads below S1 offer defined-risk positioning with positive gamma supporting the downside.

**What to watch before adding structures:**

1. **JOLTs at 08:30 tomorrow** is the binary event that could define the session. Size accordingly — do not carry full notional into the print. The NFP sequence later this week elevates the stakes of each labor data point.
2. **7,600 Put Wall overnight** — A gap below this level on futures would signal deterioration and warrant reassessment before initiating income structures. The regime remains positive, but closing at the Put Wall is not the same as closing above it with cushion.
